- **Golden transcripts of the `host-adapter`.** `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/fixtures/adapter-parity/transcript.json` was recorded from the stage 1 adapter over the real `WorkspaceService` with Prisma stores (PostgreSQL), a scripted sandbox and the test dispatcher: every method of the four contracts, the `inactive` refusal after the end of the prompt and the `unavailable` refusal of a facade without terminals. UUIDs, times and durations are normalized. `tests/support/capture-adapter.ts` imports the kernel through the test build path (the `runtime` cannot depend on the kernel); re-recording runs with `DORIC_TEST_DATABASE_URL` and `DORIC_TEST_ADAPTER_CAPTURE=true` and is only possible while the adapter exists.

## Stages 5 to 7 — sandbox adapter (57-WP-S)

- **Installed bundle `bundles/sandbox-adapter` (project `bundle-sandbox-adapter`).** Provisional until stage 10, version `0.0.0`, it provides `sandbox/v1@1.0.0` and requires only `log/v1`. It runs in the generation's process, next to `runtime` and `direct`. It reads `DORIC_SANDBOX_PROVIDER`, `DORIC_SANDBOX_IMAGE` and `DORIC_SANDBOX_SSH` from the inherited environment, with the same rules as today: only `firecracker` chooses Firecracker, and only an exact `true` turns SSH on. Client, machine registry, resources, pool limits and network are those of `index.ts`. The API points are these: `acquire` calls `pool.acquire({ identity, signal })`; `discard` calls `discardWorkspace` on Docker and does nothing on Firecracker; `machines`/`machine` read the registry; `describe.durableWorkspace` is true only on Docker. The activation's cleanup calls `pool.dispose()`.
- **Registry moved, not deleted.** `vms.ts` was copied without change to `src/host/registry.ts` (`createMachineRegistry`). `agents/doric/src/lib/vms.ts` and `tests/vms.test.ts` stay in the kernel, because the cross-review takes `agents/doric` out of 57-WP-S and the current boot still uses them. They go away in 57-WP-K, together with the `sandbox_pool` stage, which is left with only the two log lines.
- **The `sandpool` logger.** `sandpool` requires a pino logger. The adapter passes a pino at `debug` level (the same as the kernel) whose destination forwards each record to `log/v1`: `msg` becomes the message, the numeric level becomes `debug`/`info`/`warn`/`error`, and the other fields (including `component: 'sandpool'`) go as `fields`.

## Stages 5 to 7 — stage 7 data migration (57-WP-7)

- **SQL migration `bundles/foundation/postgres/prisma/migrations/20261010000000_runtime_documents`.** It runs in `prisma migrate deploy`, in one transaction, and is idempotent: with the `runtime.migration/v1 legacy-prisma` marker present it does nothing. It copies `project` → `runtime.project/v1`, `thread` → `runtime.thread/v1` (`pending = null`, `usage` verbatim, `lastStarted` from the last `prompt.started`), `messages`/`checkpoints` → `runtime.history/v1` `<id>:messages` and `<id>:meta` (`messageCount = jsonb_array_length(messages)`), and `thread_event` → `runtime.thread.events/<id>` with sequence, gaps, `created_at` and counter (`last_sequence`) preserved. Revision 1 and the legacy `updated_at` on each document.
- **Value of the stream entries.** It follows X-5 and the real schema: the `threadEventEnvelopeSchema` envelope (`projectId`, `threadId`, `promptId`, `type`, `event`), with `createdAt` coming from the protocol's `created_at`, and not the `{ promptId, type, event, createdAt }` of the original table of §10.1.
- **No indexes in SQL.** No row of `storage_type` or `storage_index_entry` is written; the first `declareType` of each type builds the entries (Q10). The test proves that the paginated reads after that answer the order and the pages of the legacy stores.
- **Verification and swap.** Before the swap, counts (projects, threads, histories = 2 × threads, events), `config_snapshot`, `messages`, `checkpoints`, the counter and the md5 of `sequence:event` per Thread are compared; any difference does `RAISE EXCEPTION` and undoes everything. The swap writes the marker (`source: 'prisma-legacy'`, as in the `runtime` schema), a `storage_commit` (`max + 1`) that names it (G-15) and deletes the legacy rows (O5/HC-009: pending human approval). The empty tables go away in a later migration.
- **New database.** In a database without data the migration writes only the marker and its commit. Until 57-WP-K the kernel still writes to the legacy tables; since the marker already exists, those rows are neither refused nor reimported.
- **Refusal in `postgres.database/v1.connect()` (X-14).** With rows in `project`, `thread` or `thread_event` and without the marker, `connect()` rejects with `Legacy Project and Thread data has not been migrated; run prisma migrate deploy.` (fatal in `database_client`). The check is the first trip to the database: if it itself fails (database unreachable), the error is left to the first query, in `credential_activation`, as today.
- **Legacy oracle.** `agents/doric/tests/helpers/legacy` re-exports the Prisma stores of `src` while the kernel uses them; 57-WP-K, when it removes them from `src`, freezes a copy there. The test of `persistence.test.ts` that sets up a schema without the name columns does not apply the stage 7 migration.

## Stages 5 to 7 — Direct executor (57-WP-D)

- **Installed bundle `bundles/runtime/direct` (project `bundle-direct`).** It provides `executor/v1@1.0.0` and requires `tools/v1`, `skills/v1`, `models/v1`, `host-config/v1` (X-6, in place of `core.configuration/v1`) and `log/v1`. The build runs `cli.js build`, and the baseline `contracts.published.json` records `executor/v1`. Composition and staging stay with 57-WP-K.
- **Execution moved without a change in behavior.** These pieces came from `agents/doric/src/lib/agents/direct/executor.ts` without change: the text of the system prompt, the input headers, the repair of interrupted calls, the write points (`response.finished`, `tool.finished` and the `finally`), the `providerId` in `response.started` and the pause without recording a failure when the tool's generation is lost. Redaction and the persistence error with retry now belong to the runtime's `PromptSession`: `direct` hands over the raw history and event.
- **Order of the reads.** The order is: history, `host-config/v1.current`, `tools/v1.list` (with `await`, because the list comes from another process), `skills/v1.list`, `models/v1.resolve` and, finally, the resolution of the tool configs. Today's order is kept: the tools and skills of the lease, then the provider, then the configs. A tool that was not made with `defineTool` is refused with the same message as `boot.ts`.
- **`direct.history/v1`.** Without the `meta` part, `messageCount` is the length of `messages`, like today's `jsonb_array_length`. In a `rewind`, `messageCount` is the real length of the cut, the same as the current `slice`, even if the boundary goes past the end.
- **Definition by ID.** `defineService(ExecutorV1.id, …)` is used because the loose output schema of `run` does not match the exact type of the typed overload.
- **Tests.** The scenarios of `direct.test.ts` and `direct-prompts.test.ts` were ported to `bundles/runtime/direct/tests` and run through `executor/v1` with a fake session. The cases of attaching the Host and the sandbox (`attach`/`release` and the prompt's sandbox) no longer exist in `direct` and stay with the runtime (57-WP-R2). The exact request bodies come from the E2E baseline (scenarios 06 and 16). The UUIDs that the normalizer replaced in the `pattern` of the schemas were restored to the real values (nil and max UUID) of `tools.json`.

## Stages 5 to 7 — the core's `models/v1` and models adapter (57-WP-M)

- **`models/v1` in the core (`src/host/models/`).** The core provides `models/v1` (core version `1.2.0`, baseline regenerated) over the `models.provider/v1` registrations seen by `whenAvailable`. The descriptor of each registration (`kind()`, `advertised()`) is read once, when the registration appears, and keeps its registration position; `kinds()` returns the advertised ones in that order, like `providerKinds`, and `kind(id)` finds any of them, `codex` included. A removed registration discards the providers it built; the next `resolve` rebuilds them from the active configuration.
- **Active configuration without reading `host-config/v1`.** The core receives no product configuration (X-6) and cannot require `host-config/v1` without a cycle. That is why `prepare` defines the active set: a saved snapshot (`{ configuration, revision, updatedAt }`) becomes active; a configuration without `revision` is a candidate, built before saving, and the next saved snapshot with the same providers adopts it without rebuilding. Protocol for `host-config` (57-WP-K): at boot, `prepare(current)`; on `replace`, `prepare(filled)` before `store.replace` (failure → `rejected`) and `prepare(snapshot)` after. This keeps today's behavior: providers are built once per replacement, a write that fails swaps nothing, and a prompt in progress keeps the instance it resolved. The same snapshot again builds nothing.
- **Secret per call.** `create` receives the values without the `secret` fields and a `secret` callback that reads `secrets/v1.resolve` in the core (missing credential → `''`, as today). The `SecretSource` of `llms` already accepts `() => Promise<string>` and is awaited before each request, so the adapter passes the callback as the value of the `secret` field, without an extra wrapper (G-2). A rotation reaches the next request of an already built provider.
- **Catalog.** `models/v1.catalog` resolves the credential of the kind's first `secret` field and calls the registration's `catalog`; `failed` (or a registration that fails) logs `warn('Model catalog could not be read', { kind })` in `log/v1`. An unknown kind returns `absent`, as `readCatalogEntries` does today (the design said `failed`).
- **Catalog filling.** `readModelProperties` and `executionProfile` come from `config/models.ts` with no change of rule, with structural types, and go out through `core/contracts` (it widens the rule "`core/contracts` exports only …"), receiving a catalog reader, which `host-config` wires to `models/v1.catalog`. Known difference: on the `PUT /config` path, the `warn` line carries `{ kind }` instead of `{ providerId }`, because the input of `models/v1.catalog` does not have the provider id. Preserving the field requires an optional `providerId?` in that input (owner: `packages/bundle`).
- **Installed bundle `bundles/models-adapter` (project `bundle-models-adapter`).** Provisional until stage 10, version `0.0.0`, it provides `models.provider/v1@1.0.0` (`many`) and requires only `log/v1`. It registers `openai`, `openrouter` and `codex` in the order of `supportedKinds` of `llms`. `create` calls `createProviderForKind` with `identity: { id: providerId, name: providerId }`, one `createFetchTransport()` per provider, and returns `toLanguageModel(provider)`. The logger of `llms` is a pino at `debug` whose destination forwards to `log/v1`, as in the `sandbox-adapter`. `./host` is exported for the core's tests.
- **Several registrations of a `many` contract in the build.** The build recorder (`packages/bundle/src/build/record.ts`) refused a contract provided more than once in the same environment. A `many` contract provided again by the same bundle now counts once in the descriptor; for `one` contracts the refusal stays.
- **Definition by ID.** `defineService(ModelsV1.id, …)` and `defineService(ModelsProviderV1.id, …)`: the typed overload reads the one-item tuple of `kind(id)` as `string` and refuses the `readonly` types of `llms`.

## Stages 5 to 7 — REST and Socket.IO transport (57-WP-T)

- **Embedded bundle `bundles/foundation/transport` (project `bundle-transport`).** `transport/kernel` exports `createTransport({ app, io, logger, readHostResources? })`, which returns an `EmbeddedBundle` `transport@0.0.0` without `provides` and with `requires` of `runtime/v1`, `runtime.threads/v1`, `runtime.lease/v1`, `runtime.terminals/v1`, `sandbox/v1`, `tools/v1`, `models/v1`, `secrets/v1` and `host-config/v1` (all `^1.0.0`). Routes, messages, codes, mount order, `Cache-Control` and the final handler came without change from `agents/doric/src/{lib/http,routes,lib/events}`; the wiring in the kernel and the removal of the originals stay with 57-WP-K. `readHostResources` is optional and serves the tests; the default is the `node:os` reader, with the CPU sample in module state.
- **Stubs resolved at call time.** The activation only creates stubs with `services.use`, registers `/status`, `/projects` and `/threads`, and mounts the routes. No call to the generation happens in `activate`. Each lease read uses `services.use(RuntimeLeaseV1, { projectId })` or `{ threadId }` per request. The calls pass no signal or deadline; a rejection reaches `handleHttpError` (500), like an error escaped from the service today. Waiting for an active generation (G-11) belongs to the stub and to 57-WP-H, not to the transport.
- **`watch` through `whenAvailable`.** The socket subscribes with `whenAvailable(RuntimeV1, (runtime) => runtime.watch(listener))`. The returned `Disposable` is tracked by the callback's scope, so the subscription ends when that provider leaves the active view. Since the kernel swaps the overlay in `publish`, the subscription of a draining generation ends at the swap, not when the process stops. Keeping the notices of generation N during draining (G-1, item 5) requires a change in the platform or in the handover and stays with 57-WP-H.
- **`GET /vms` from the active generation only (G-10).** `sandbox/v1.machines()` and `machine(id)` answer through the active provider, because the transport only sees the active view. This is only acceptable together with 57-WP-H (G-1): with the handover, each Project has a single owner and the machines of a draining generation leave with it.
- **`PUT /config` forwards the raw body.** It follows the decision of 57-WP-0: `host-config/v1.replace(request.body)` validates. `invalid` → 422 `invalid_config` with the message; `rejected` and also a rejection of the stub → 503 `configuration_rejected`, because the current `catch` turns any other failure into 503 (the design said 500 for stub rejections).
- **Credentials over `secrets/v1`.** The schemas `CredentialCreateSchema`/`CredentialUpdateSchema` were copied from `agents/doric/src/lib/credentials/kind.ts` (the kernel's copy goes away in 57-WP-K). The response is the description returned by `secrets/v1`, the same as `publicCredential` of the saved record. The `ContractError`s `credential_invalid`, `credential_missing`, `credential_name_taken`, `credential_kind_immutable` and `credential_storage_unavailable` become 422, 404, 409, 409 and 503; any other error goes to the 500.
- **`POST /providers/models` over `models/v1.kind`.** The strict schema `{ kind, configuration }` (kind with `trim`, from 1 to 128) and the field rule of `ProviderValuesSchema` are applied to the descriptor of `models/v1.kind(id)`, which includes the internal kinds (`codex`), like `providerKind` today. Unknown kind or refused field → 422 `invalid_provider` without reading the catalog; `failed` and `absent` → 502. The log of the read failure now belongs to the core (57-WP-M).
- **Socket snapshot.** `terminals(projectId)` became asynchronous. It is read once, after the registry and the tree, and the same value goes to `project:snapshot` and `terminal:snapshot`. Today these are two synchronous reads in a row, which give the same value.
- **Tests.** `routes.test.ts` ports `workspace-routes`, `host-routes.test.ts` ports `config-routes`, `credential-routes`, the routes half of `vms` and the terminal routes, `http-edges.test.ts` covers the mount order, `no-store` on the following routers, malformed JSON → 500, Express's HTML 404, IDs per router and the host half of `resources`, and `sockets.test.ts` ports the cases of `socket.test.ts` with `watch`. All of them run the activated bundle over fake providers of each contract (`createTestServices`), because the host of the `runtime` (57-WP-R2) does not exist yet; running the same cases over the real in-memory runtime is left to 57-WP-K/E.

## Stages 5 to 7 — the `runtime` store over `storage/v1` (57-WP-R1)

- **`bundles/runtime/runtime/src/host/store/`.** `createRuntimeStores(storage, { executor })` returns `ProjectStore` and `ThreadStore` (`types.ts` of 57-WP-0) over a shared set of documents; `runtimeStorageTypes` lists the four types for the activation's `defineType` (57-WP-R2). The mappers, the state rules (`excludedStates`, `startedAt`, `finishedAt`, `open`), the page by `createdKey` with an exclusive cursor, the `reconcile` and the fold of `unfinishedPrompts` came from `projects.ts`, `threads.ts`, `usage-store.ts` and `storage.ts` with no change of rule.
- **Write protocol.** Each write is a `commit` with `expect` of the revision read, a queue per document in the process and a `{ value, revision }` cache (revision `expected + 1` after the commit). `revision_conflict` and `sequence_conflict` discard the cache, reread and recompute, up to 8 times. The append uses `expectedSequence = lastSequence` of the document, so the document and the stream's counter do not diverge.
- **Protocol time.** The `createdAt` of an event is that of the stream entry; the store rereads the entries it has just written (one `read` per commit) to answer the `ThreadEvent`. The fold in the same commit uses a provisional time; when the backend's differs, a second write of the document (without moving `updatedAt`) writes the backend's into `pending[].acceptedAt` and `lastStarted.at`, and only for `prompt.accepted` and `prompt.started`. If the process goes down between the two writes, the provisional time stays (a difference of milliseconds).
- **The executor's part in the rewind.** `types.ts` does not receive the executor; the factory receives `executor.rewind({ projectId, threadId, history, promptId, removedPromptIds })`. The store computes `from`, the removed prompts and the survivors from the stream, asks for the patch and writes everything in one commit (truncate, marker, document, parts). No events of the prompt or no patch → `undefined`.
- **Pure helpers in the store.** `queueSnapshot` (with `storageQueueError`), the usage projection (`projectUsage`, `usageTotals`, …) and `delegatedResult` stay in `store/` and go out through `store/index.ts`, because the store uses them and is the lower layer. The domain (57-WP-R2) must import them from here instead of keeping copies.
- **Sanitization where `json()`/`storable()` used to be.** Body of the events, `usage`, `configSnapshot`, result text and history parts. Name and `cwd` are not changed.
- **Differences the domain cannot reach.** The `promptId` of an append is validated as a UUID before the commit (the `uuid` column refused it); a read of events past the counter answers `[]` (the protocol refuses with `invalid_input`); `create` on a missing Project throws `Inactive project.` (before, a Prisma error); `saveHistory` on a missing Thread rejects; in `pending`, a `text` that is not a string becomes `''` and an invalid `source` becomes `{ kind: 'user' }`, because the document's schema requires them; a missing field is omitted instead of `paused: undefined` after a `prompt.resumed`.
- **Order of `usage().threads`.** The requested Thread and then the descendants level by level, in `createdKey` order. Today's recursive CTE defines no order within a level.
- **Deletion above `maxBatch` (R12).** The deletion of a Project or of a subtree is a single commit; above the limit the backend refuses with `limit_exceeded` before writing. The log of that refusal stays with the domain.
- **Tests.** `tests/store/store.conformance.test.ts` ports the store cases of `persistence.test.ts` and runs in memory and, with `DORIC_TEST_DATABASE_URL`, on PostgreSQL with the migrations of `postgres`. `tests/store/support.ts` serves `storage/v1` over the backend (deferred declaration of the type and schema validation on each `put`, like the core's facade); since it wraps the already conformant memory backend, the conformance kit does not run again. `progress.property.test.ts` compares the incremental fold and the lazy fold (`pending: null`) with the old fold, over 60 logs generated with a seed and over the only complete log of the E2E baseline (`13-replay`, from sequence 5, in `tests/store/fixtures/baseline-logs.json`). `append-cost.test.ts` measures 10k `text.delta`: one commit and one read per event, no `get` or `query` (G-12, the store's part; the cost of `secrets/v1.redactions()` belongs to 57-WP-R2).

## Stages 5 to 7 — `runtime` domain and services (57-WP-R2)

- **Host part of the `runtime` (`bundles/runtime/runtime/src/host/`).** `activate` defines the four storage types, registers `runtime.prompt-finished/v1`, sets up the ports (57 §4.3) and calls `provideRuntime`, which provides `runtime/v1`, `runtime.threads/v1`, `runtime.workspace/v1`, `runtime.lease/v1`, `runtime.terminals/v1` and `terminal/v1` and defines `runtime.hooks/v1`. The activation does no data work; `recover` does. `runtimeStores(storage, ports)` wires the executor's part in the rewind to `executor/v1` scoped to the rewritten prompt. The build runs `cli.js build`, there is a `contracts-baseline` (`contracts.published.json`) and the test runs `tests/**`.
- **Storage types in `provides`.** The build CLI requires every type defined in `activate` to be in `provides`; the manifest gained `runtime.project/v1`, `runtime.thread/v1`, `runtime.history/v1` and `runtime.migration/v1` `@1.0.0` (the manifest test of 57-WP-0 was adjusted).
- **Domain moved with no change of rule.** `service.ts`, `runner.ts`, `runtime.ts`, `prompts.ts`, `storage-pause.ts`, `cwd.ts`, `git.ts` (→ `git-credentials.ts`, with the credential resolution), `usage-context.ts`, `colors.ts` and `events/serialization.ts` + `credentials/redact.ts` (→ `serialization.ts`) are in `domain/`; `files`, `file-changes`, `git-status`, `branches`, the sandbox half of `resources` and `terminals` are in `provisional/`. Queue, usage and `delegatedResult` come from `store/` (57-WP-R1), with no copy in the domain. Log messages and fields are today's, now through `log/v1` (`log.warn(mensagem, campos)`).
- **No hold on generation loss.** `held`, `pauseForGenerationLoss`, `resumeHeld`, `suspendForGenerationLoss` and `resumeAfterGenerationLoss` (D4-16) were deleted, not moved (X-12): the runner dies with its generation and the new generation resumes through `recover`. The handover between live generations belongs to 57-WP-H.
- **Execution through `executor/v1`.** In the dispatch, under the lock: `checkStorage`, `state(running)`, `executor.checkpoint({ history, promptId })` written by `saveCheckpoint`, and `prompt.started`. The execution creates the `PromptSession` (history and event redacted with `eventJson`, history before the event, retry of the last history and of the pending event), binds the prompt's Host to the scope (`bindings.attach`) and calls `run` with `{ signal }`; the binding is released when the execution settles. The error read is `session.failure ?? erro`, because the `ThreadPersistenceError` class does not cross the dispatcher. The 3 s storage guard wraps that call.
- **History format.** A format that the executor does not read makes the prompt fail before any execution (`agent.failed` and `prompt.finished` `failed`); `doric.diagnostics/v1` has no report method, so the code `history_format_unsupported` goes in a `log.error` with `threadId`.
- **Redaction.** `redactor.current()` is `[...secrets/v1.redactions(), ...registrados]`; the Git token applied to a sandbox is registered in the runtime (no longer in the core's cache). The terminal stream is synchronous: it uses the last list read (`latest()`), which triggers a reread in the background, and each terminal start reads `current()` first. A rotation reaches the terminal in the chunk after the reread, not at the same instant as today.
- **Git credentials through `secrets/v1`.** `byId` → `resolve`, `byKind` → `resolveByKind`, `byUnclaimedKind` → `resolveByKind(kind, { exclude })`, with the same messages in the `warn`. The claimed credentials are the `secret` fields of the kind of each provider, read in `models/v1.kind`, and they are only read when the token has no configured choice.
- **`recover` once per process.** The second call returns the first summary without work; `dispose` is idempotent.
- **Lease reads.** The Project reads use `call.scope.projectId` and the Thread reads (`git`, `branches`) use `call.scope.threadId`; without the required id, `ContractError('invalid_input', leaseScopeMessage)`. As in the stage 1 adapter, the refusals of the prompt methods (`inactive`) and of scope are thrown synchronously by `execute`.
- **Hooks and large deletion.** `onProjectReadyHook` and `onProjectClosingHook` fire without waiting after the `ready` and `cancelling` commits; a failure of the hook is ignored. A deletion refused with `limit_exceeded` (R12) is logged (`Deletion exceeds the storage batch limit`) and propagated; nothing is deleted.
- **Tests.** The cases of `workspace-service`, `workspace-queue`, `coordination`, `workspace-lifecycle`, `storage-pressure`, `rewind`, `prompt-events`, `event-json`, `usage-context`, `workspace-cwd`, `workspace-github`, `workspace-resume`, `workspace-restart` (PostgreSQL), the runner half of `workspace-direct`, the service half of `workspace-files`, `workspace-git`, `branches`, `file-changes`, `terminals` and the sandbox half of `resources` were ported and run through the runtime's contracts, from a consumer bundle, over the real store (memory; `workspace-restart` on PostgreSQL), with fakes only in the required contracts and a scripted `executor/v1`. `tests/contracts/adapter-parity.test.ts` replays the golden transcript of 57-WP-0 against the runtime's providers and is equal after normalization. Adjustments: the machine input case uses a complete `result` `source` (the store of 57-WP-R1 normalizes an incomplete one to `user`), and a second recovery in the same process returns the first summary.

## Stages 5 to 7 — generation handover (57-WP-H)

- **Platform, waiting for an active generation (G-11).** `ServiceRuntime.awaitProvider` is filled in only in the kernel. A stub without a provider (not pinned by `whenAvailable`) of a contract that the generation's bundles supply, called while no generation is active (respawn window), waits for the next `onActive` and then dispatches again. The call's signal aborts the wait. With the host stopping, or with a `failed` generation (empty view), it answers `unavailable` as before. In that case a synchronous method returns a promise, which only happens for remote contracts.
- **Platform, late kernel providers.** `ServiceDirectory.observe` sees every registration and removal. After a registration or a local removal in the kernel, the manager compares `kernelProviders()` with what each ready process already knows (per microtask) and sends `provide`/`unprovide`. A process that is still starting is synchronized at `ready`. The runner registers and withdraws those remote providers.
- **Platform, draining generation (G-1, item 5).** `LayeredDirectory.setOverlay(next, { retain: true })` keeps the previous overlay: its registrations leave the read (`providers`, `active`), but stay active for whoever already holds them (`isActive`). The removal reaches the subscribers only when the overlay itself removes the registration or with `release`. The manager retains at the swap and releases when the generation stops or dies. So the transport's `watch` over N lives until N stops, and the transport did not need to change. When the active generation goes down there is no retention.
- **Platform, order at the swap.** In `#activate`, the `onActive` listeners run before the stop check of the replaced generation, and that one is already `draining`. A call started in the listener (the `drain`) counts as in flight and keeps N alive.
- **Owner of the Project.** `runtime.project/v1` gained `owner: { generation, incarnation } | null`, optional, internal and ignored by the mapper. Neither writing the owner nor clearing it moves `updatedAt`. It is written at creation and at the `claim` of `resumeProject`: the `claim` fails if the current owner is another live process, and the Project stays in standby. The clearing (`release`) happens at host stop, at termination and at drain. The process identifies itself by `doric.generation/v1.describe()`; without that contract (single-process host) no owner is written and everything works as today. The contracts went to `runtime/v1@1.1.0` and `runtime.project/v1@1.1.0`, with the bundle at `1.1.0` (minor change; the published baseline stays at 1.0.0).
- **`recover` intact and new `recoverWith`.** Changing the input of `recover` to a tuple with an optional `handover` is a major change by the build's semver rule. That is why `recover()` stayed the same and `recoverWith({ live })` came in, with the same result cached per process. The Projects whose owner is in `live` are left out of `threads.reconcile`, `projects.reconcile`, `resumeInterrupted` and `recoverProjects`: the store's two `reconcile` accept a Project predicate and the service accepts an `include`. An owner that is not in `live` is dead and gets the `host_restarted` semantics.
- **`drain(released)` (minimal API).** The design said "N does not start a new prompt". It ended up like this: N brings no Project back (`resumeProject` refuses), and new input does not reach N, because the stubs resolve the active generation. The prompts already queued in N and the ones fired by a running prompt (delegation) keep running. Without that, a parent prompt that waits for a child would stall the drain. When a Project goes idle (no task and no acquisition), N closes the terminals (a deviation already foreseen in (6), only reachable through the internal `publish()`, D4-13), releases the lease, clears the owner and calls `released(projectId)`. The state of Project and Threads stays as it is, without `queued`. A background terminal result that arrives later is discarded with the terminals.
- **`adopt({ live })`.** Serialized after recovery. Each Project in standby whose owner became null, or is no longer in `live`, is adopted: with a dead owner, first the `reconcile` of that Project only (with today's log); then `resumeInterrupted` of it only and `resumeProject` (`claim`, acquisition by name, G-3).
- **Waiting in standby, recorded deviation.** Every operation that needs the local runtime of a Project in standby waits for the adoption: the Project's `exclusive`, `projectFor` and the lease reads. A prompt, a pause, a termination or `ssh` sent to N+1 during the drain wait for N to finish the running prompt, instead of answering `inactive` or `unavailable`. This only happens in the internal `publish()`, and the routes still have no timeout. The storage reads (`find`, `list`, events) do not wait. The terminal list of N+1 does not show the terminals of N.
- **Kernel, `installHandover` in `agents/doric/src/lib/bundles/handover.ts`.** In `onActive(g)`, synchronously: `drain` on each `draining` generation not yet drained, with `released → adopt` on the active generation and another `adopt` when the drain ends; then `recoverWith({ live: draining })` on g, which also covers the respawn. On `onLost` of a draining generation: `adopt` without it. The wiring to the boot (the boot still calls `recover()`) stays with 57-WP-K.
- **Hold per execution not adopted.** The runtime does not take `doric.generation/v1.hold` per prompt. What keeps N alive is the `drain` call in flight, so `onLost` of N arrives with `executions: []`.
- **Tests.** `packages/bundle/tests/handover.test.ts` uses real processes and covers: a call waiting for the respawn and aborted by the signal; a kernel provider registered and removed after the boot reaching the generation; the `whenAvailable` of a kernel bundle keeping the provider of N until N stops; and a call started in `onActive` keeping N alive. `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/domain/handover.test.ts` uses two runtimes over the same storage (memory and PostgreSQL) and covers: E2 (N finishes the prompt in its lease, M waits and adopts, one lease at a time, no pause), E3c (N dies while draining, M reconciles with `host_restarted` and resumes) and orphan owners of a host that went down. `agents/doric/tests/handover.test.ts` covers the kernel's orchestration.

## Stages 5 to 7 — kernel integration (57-WP-K)

- **Boot.** `bootstrap` → `sandbox_pool` (only the two log lines, read from the same environment that the `sandbox-adapter` reads in the generation; `src/lib/bundles/sandbox.ts` repeats the read because the adapter's package does not export `sandboxSettings`) → `bundle_load` (`startBundles` with `host-config` and `transport` embedded, `assertStructural`, `Bundles loaded` with `bundleCount` 9, which excludes the embedded ones) → `database_client` (`postgres.database/v1.connect()`; the kernel no longer reads `client()`, G-13) → `credential_activation` (loading of the credentials and `storageIdentity`) → `configuration_activation` (`host-config/v1.current()` and `models/v1.prepare(snapshot)`, G-16) → `workspace_reconciliation` (`runtime/v1.recover()` through the envelope; the three recovery lines now come from the runtime through `log/v1`) → `installHandover` and `watchStructural` → `platform.ready()` → `network_binding`. The stop is `server.close`, `io.close`, `runtime/v1.dispose()`, removal of the listeners and `platform.stop()` (`postgres` disconnects last).
- **`assertStructural`.** `runtime/v1`, `executor/v1`, `sandbox/v1`, `models/v1` and `host-config/v1` need a provider whose bundle is `active`, and the `transport` must be active. Otherwise the kernel logs `Bundle recovery mode` with `reason` (`structural providers unavailable: …`), stops the platform, and the boot ends on the usual fatal path, at the `bundle_load` stage. A generation that does not come up (bundles `quarantined` with `generation_failed`) falls on the same path.
- **G-5, structural loss after the boot.** `watchStructural` re-evaluates the catalog on each `onActive`; if a generation has exhausted its respawns and the active view was left without a structural provider, the kernel logs `Bundle recovery mode` and exits with code 1, as the process used to die before. Decision recommended by the cross-review; it still asks for explicit approval (§5 of the cross-review).
- **`host-config/v1` (X-6).** The embedded `host-config` stays in `src/lib/bundles/configuration.ts` (not in `configuration/**`, so as not to change the tests' imports) and now supplies `host-config/v1`, also requiring `models/v1` and `tools/v1`. `current()` loads the settings space once (a load that fails is tried again on the next call) and then answers the active snapshot in memory, like the old `ConfigService`. `replace(body)` runs the `PUT /config` pipeline in the same order, in a serial queue: schema (`invalid`, `The Doric configuration is invalid.`), credentials rule (`invalid` with today's message), filling from the catalog (`readModelProperties` of `core/contracts` reading `models/v1.catalog` per provider), rule of the tool fields over `tools/v1.list()` (`invalid`), `models/v1.prepare` of the candidate configuration, write, and `models/v1.prepare` of the saved snapshot, which activates that revision. Any other failure keeps the active configuration and answers `rejected` (503). The `warn` `Model catalog could not be read` is now the core's, with `{ kind }` instead of `{ providerId }`; logs do not enter the E2E comparison.
- **Refusal of legacy data.** The kernel does not log the reason of the `connect()` refusal: the fatal path still logs only the stage (`database_client`), as in every boot failure.
- **Platform, `many` contract registrations through the envelope.** The kernel identified a remote provider by `(contrato, bundle)`. The three `models.provider/v1` registrations of the `models-adapter` became a single one in the kernel, and `prepare` failed with `Unknown provider kind: openrouter` at boot. Now each advertised registration carries a stable `key` of the process that advertises it (`RemoteProviderDescriptor.key`), the call carries `record` and `unprovide` carries the `key`; a descriptor without `key` works as before. Test in `packages/bundle/tests/envelope-records.test.ts`.
- **Platform, optional output through the envelope.** An `.optional()` output of a contract defined by a bundle publishes only the inner schema, so the kernel refused `undefined` (`project(id)` of a nonexistent Project answered 500 instead of 404). The output of a remote provider now accepts `undefined`: the providing process has already validated the result against its own zod schema. Same test file.
- **Call in flight at the loss of the generation.** A request whose dispatch was already in the process that died fails with it (500 `internal_error`); the calls made after the loss wait for the respawn (G-11). Before stage 4 the same crash brought down the whole host.
- **Composition and build.** `composition.json` enables `runtime`, `direct`, `models-adapter` and `sandbox-adapter`; `build-bundles` and the `.Dockerfile` compile and stage those bundles and the `transport` (embedded, without staging). `agents/doric` depends on `core` (contracts) and `transport`.
- **Tests.** The moved tests went away (§13.2), `generations.test.ts` (its premise was the workspace in the kernel) and `postgres-client.test.ts` (the kernel no longer uses the Prisma client). With no earlier port, these were ported to `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests`: `usage`, `storage-persistence` and `storage-health`. `config.test.ts` covers the new service; `reference-race.test.ts` runs the pipeline and the credentials directly (the HTTP mapping belongs to the transport); `secret-logs.test.ts`, `boot.test.ts` and `integration.test.ts` bring up the compiled host as a process, with PostgreSQL, a real Docker sandbox and a scripted OpenRouter endpoint. `integration.test.ts` also kills the generation's process during a prompt and checks the `host_restarted` pause and the resumption by the respawned generation (end-to-end E3 evidence). The stage 7 oracle was frozen in `tests/helpers/legacy` (Prisma stores and dependencies, with the sandbox types coming from `runtime/contracts`).
- **Sandbox settings without drift.** The kernel keeps the copy of `sandboxSettings` (a bundle has no public entry and the kernel does not import bundle code); `tests/sandbox-settings.test.ts` compares the copy with that of the `sandbox-adapter` staged in `dist/bundles` for several environments, so a change in only one of them fails the test.

## Stages 5, 6 and 7 — consolidated decisions (57-WP-G)

Index of the design decisions D-S5 to D-S7 (§16 of the design, with the cross-reviews). The detail of each one is in the WP sections above; here there is only what was decided and where the code diverged from the design.

- **D-S5-01 `runtime` contracts.** `runtime/v1` (Projects, `recover`, `recoverWith`, `drain`, `adopt`, `dispose`, `watch`), `runtime.threads/v1`, `runtime.workspace/v1`, `runtime.lease/v1` (scope `projectId`/`threadId`), `runtime.terminals/v1` (provisional until stage 10) and `runtime.hooks/v1` (fired without waiting). Minimal API.
- **D-S5-02 `runtime` storage.** Types `runtime.project/v1`, `runtime.thread/v1`, `runtime.history/v1` and `runtime.migration/v1`; stream `runtime.thread.events/<threadId>`; the `pending` projection and the `usage` aggregate folded in the same commit as the event. The `runtime` registers `runtime.prompt-finished/v1` in `events/v1`.
- **D-S5-03 `terminal/v1` in stage 5.** The kernel's `host-adapter` went away entirely; the terminal registry and `terminal/v1` stay provisionally in the `runtime` and go to the `terminal` bundle in stage 10. It asks for explicit approval (O5, HC-009).
- **D-S5-04 Recovery and stop.** Boot order of 57-WP-K: `bootstrap` → `sandbox_pool` → `bundle_load` (with `assertStructural`) → `database_client` → `credential_activation` → `configuration_activation` → `workspace_reconciliation` (`runtime/v1.recover()` and wiring of the handover; `onHostReadyHook` before the listener) → `network_binding` → `ready`. At stop: server and sockets, `runtime/v1.dispose()`, `platform.stop()`.
- **D-S5-05 Embedded transport.** Package `bundles/foundation/transport`, activated as an embedded bundle in the kernel, which hands it the Express app and the Socket.IO server.
- **D-S5-06 `sandbox/v1`.** Only `acquire` is local; `describe`, `discard`, `machines` and `machine` are asynchronous and serializable (X-16), because `GET /vms` runs in the kernel. Provisional `sandbox-adapter` adapter until stage 10.
- **D-S5-07 withdrawn.** `core.configuration/v1` does not exist. The configuration stays in the kernel's embedded `host-config`, which supplies `host-config/v1` (`current`, `replace`) with the `PUT /config` pipeline in a single place (X-6). This diverges from decision 86 of the desktop, which foresaw `settings/v1`, `secrets/v1` and `models/v1` in the settings sections (G-1 of the desktop review); the UI still calls the same routes.
- **D-S5-08 Generation handover.** Owner `{ generation, incarnation }` on the Project, `recoverWith({ live })`, `drain(released)` in N and `adopt({ live })` in N+1, orchestrated by `installHandover` in the kernel (G-1). The deviations (waiting in standby, closing of the terminals of the released Project, prompts already queued in N keep running in N) are only reachable through the internal `publish()` (D4-13). The `restart_required` fallback was not needed.
- **D-S6-01 Executor session.** `executor/v1` is local and `run` receives a `PromptSession`; `checkpoint` and `rewind` are the executor's part in the rewind.
- **D-S6-02 `direct.history/v1`.** Parts `messages` and `meta { checkpoints, messageCount }`, opaque state for the `runtime`.
- **D-S6-03 `models/v1`.** `resolve`, `kinds`, `kind`, `catalog`, `prepare`; `models.provider/v1` with `kind`, `advertised`, `catalog`, `create`; the `models-adapter` registers `openai`, `openrouter` and `codex` in that order. Each `many` registration crosses the envelope with its own `key` (57-WP-K).
- **D-S6-04 `direct.hooks/v1`** stays out until there is a consumer.
- **D-S7-01 Data migration.** `20261010000000_runtime_documents`, executed by `prisma migrate deploy` in one transaction: copy, verification, `runtime.migration/v1 legacy-prisma` marker, removal of the legacy rows. Idempotent and irreversible; `postgres.database/v1.connect()` refuses legacy rows without a marker (X-14, fatal in `database_client`); `storageGeneration` does not change. The removal of the legacy rows asks for explicit approval (O5, HC-009).
- **Asynchronous tool validation (G-4).** The asynchronous `check` of `RemoteTool` and the asynchronous validation of `tool`/`agent` (4-WP4-C) stay, but no longer run during prompts: `direct` receives the local tools through the unwrap of home. `GET /tools` and `requireToolConfigs` still read the metadata of `RemoteTool`.
- **Logs (G-9).** The debug lines of the sandbox pool and of `llms` come out of the generation, through `log/v1`, now with `bundleId`. The kernel keeps `Doric starting`, `Sandbox pool configured` and `Doric listening {sandboxProvider}`, read from the environment. The logs do not enter the E2E comparison.
- **Processes (G-14).** The CPU sample of `GET /resources` moves to the transport (kernel, the same process as today's host) and the `fetch` of the `web` tool moves to the generation's process (the same machine). No change in behavior.
- **Structural loss after the boot (G-5).** `Bundle recovery mode` and exit with code 1, as the process used to die before. It asks for explicit approval (HC-009).
- **`packages/host`.** `Host` stops being an API between packages: only the `runtime` uses it internally, until stage 10 decides (O4).

## Stages 5 to 7 — E2E gate (57-WP-E)

- **Frame normalization (G-8).** The harness's `compare.mjs` normalizes only `stack` strings, on both sides: frames in `agents/doric/dist/` (kernel and staged bundles, such as `direct`, which left `src/lib/agents/direct/`) become `at <host-frame>`, with consecutive frames collapsed into one; frames in `/workspace/packages/` keep function and file and lose `:linha:coluna` (the `agent` package changed in stage 1). This formalizes the `stack` difference in `17-failures-and-http` accepted in stages 1, 2/3 and 4; name, message, order of the package frames and the other fields are still compared. G-8's premise that the frames of `packages/**` would stay identical to the baseline does not hold: `agent.js` has already changed lines since stage 1.
- **Run blocked by disk.** The first run of `./run-all.sh migrated doric-host:migrated` paused every prompt with `queue.paused {storage_low}`: the disk of the Docker VM had 9.1 GB free of 98 GB (9.3%, below the 10% limit). The host behaved like the current one; the gate was not completed and must be repeated after freeing space in the VM.

## Stage 8 — `packages/bundle` (8A)

- **No new `bridge-protocol.ts` or `remote.ts`.** As the cross-review (C-1) says, 8A only adds to the stage 4 envelope: the `end` type (`EndMessage`, with `error?: WireError`) and the control messages `lifecycle`/`lifecycle-result` (`AppLifecycleHook`). The `ProcessLink` still refuses `subscribe`/`event`/`unsubscribe`/`end` with `protocol_error`: between kernel and generation the listener crosses through the callback codec; the stream types serve the bridge and the desktop IPC (8F/8H). The normalization by tuple size [S8-12] is the one of the stage 4 `remoteRecord`, already covered by `envelope.test.ts`.
- **Streams [S8-20].** `streamListener(item)` (in `bundle/contracts`) is a `z.custom` with `x-doric-local: 'callback'`; the member's JSON Schema writes the item's schema in `x-doric-stream` (`streamItemSchema` in `bundle/runtime`). A stream method whose output is the `Disposable` crosses as an optimistic record (the `Disposable` is synchronous, as in-process). `toStreamObserver` treats a function as `{ next }`.
- **`doric.catalog/v1` gains `distribution()`, `artifact({ sha256 })` and `watch(listener)`** (minimal API [S8-9]) in the platform specification; only the host supplies them, through `providePlatformServices({ distribution })`. The codes `not_found`, `disconnected` and `unknown_command` enter `contractErrorCodes`.
- **`DistributionCatalog` stays in `bundle/contracts`** (it is the output of a platform method), re-exported by `runtime/distribution.ts`. The `descriptor` travels as a JSON object and the desktop validates it when resolving (`invalid_descriptor`); the host's identity is `hostBoot` plus `storage` (`StorageIdentity`), instead of loose `hostId`/`storageGeneration` (cross-review G-6). Distributed candidates use `source: { kind: 'installed', dir: '<id>@<version>' }`.
- **`distributionFor({ resolved, states, hostBoot, platformVersion, storage })`** reads the files of the directory of each installed bundle; the host computes it once per generation. Embedded bundles enter without files.
- **Platform providers in the runtime.** `catalogSnapshot`, `statesOf`, `providePlatformServices` and `LogSink` move from `loader/platform.ts` to `runtime/platform-providers.ts` (browser-safe); the loader re-exports them.
- **`build/parts.ts`.** It only rebuilds a part that has a source (`src/desktop/index.ts`, `src/client/index.ts`, `src/client/styles.css`); a `dist/desktop` emitted by another step stays as it is. Inline sourcemap only in `--development`; no minification. The client CSS uses `@tailwindcss/node` with the `Scanner` of `@tailwindcss/oxide` (already installed by `@tailwindcss/postcss`); without an explicit `@source` the root is the CSS folder. `esbuild` and Tailwind are loaded on demand by `cli build`, so that the running host does not load them.
- **Dry recording.** `cli build` also records `dist/client/index.js` (`client` environment); a client that touches `document`/`window`/`customElements`/`HTMLElement`/`localStorage` fails with an explicit error until the stage 9 recorder. In the build, `<id>/contracts` resolves through the workspace links (the bundles are `<id>` packages with `exports['./contracts']`); the `module.registerHooks` stays in the 8F runner.

## Stage 8 — `packages/desktop` (8B)

- **Components copied, not regenerated.** `npx shadcn@4.21.0 add --all` was run in a temporary directory: the current registry reproduces none of the 34 vendored components (imports, `cn` from `'cn'`, the `button` hover, the `sidebar` classes, `next-themes` in `sonner`) and `--all` would bring 27 unused components with `recharts`, `react-day-picker`, `embla-carousel-react`, `input-otp`, `vaul`, `@shadcn/react` and `next-themes`. The package carries only the 34 files of the renderer (plus `hooks/use-mobile.ts` and `lib/utils.ts`), copied from `20775a6`. `verify-provenance.mjs` undoes the import rewrites and the patches listed in `PROVENANCE.md` and compares the SHA-256 with `tests/fixtures/provenance.json`. A new component comes in through the CLI and from then on is listed in the record.
- **Base UI components rederived from the registry (2026-10-17).** With the switch from Radix to Base UI, the 26 components that used a Radix primitive, or that the `base-nova` style writes differently, were regenerated with `npx shadcn@4.21.0 add` in the `base-nova` style (`radix-nova` in Base UI), formatted with the repository's Prettier, and the local patches were applied back. Before that, the current output of `radix-nova` was compared with the renderer's files: apart from import order, `'use client'` and `cn` from `'cn'`, they only differed by the patches of `PROVENANCE.md`, so the visuals and the ARIA are kept. The provenance record now states the origin of each file (`renderer` at `20775a6` or `registry`), and `verify-provenance.mjs` gains `--record-registry`.
- **Relative `.js` imports instead of `@/`.** Inside the renderer's webpack, `@/` already points to `app/doric-renderer/src`, and the consumers' TypeScript would resolve `@/` through its own `paths`. That is why the components import `./<x>.js`, `../lib/utils.js` and `../hooks/use-mobile.js`. That form resolves the same way in webpack (Nx's `extensionAlias`), in esbuild, in `tsc` and in Node. `components.json` and the `paths` of `tsconfig.json` keep `@/` only for the CLI.
- **Local `toast` patch [S8-21].** `sonner.tsx` re-exports `toast` from `sonner`. It is the only new patch, and the verifier undoes it.
- **Vendor boundary.** `packages/desktop/src/components` enters the exclusions of Biome and Prettier, like `app/doric-renderer/src/components/ui`. `src/styles/tokens.css` also enters, because it is generated.
- **Tokens: variants without repetition.** In `tokens.ts`, `shared` holds what `:root` declares and `.dark` does not override with another value: the faces, `--font-weight-medium`, `--radius`, the shadow geometry, `--tracking-normal` and `--spacing`. The variants hold the rest, verbatim. `tokens.css` emits `:root` (light + shared), `.dark` and the window variables; the value resolved in each variant is identical to that of the old `styles.css`, and the test compares with the copy from `20775a6`. `resolvedVariables(tokens, variant)` is the shape of `doric.tokens/v1.get()`.
- **Window variables.** The default includes `--doric-safe-right: 0px` besides top and left, because `applyChrome` also writes it. The light window background is `#e7e5e4`, the sRGB conversion of the light `--background`. The dark one stays `#1c1b19`, which is 2 units per channel away from the conversion (`#1e1b18`), and the test accepts that tolerance.
- **Build.** `tsc --build` emits JS and `.d.ts`, so that the `import` exports of `dist/` work in Node. Then `scripts/build-tokens.mjs` generates `src/styles/tokens.css` and `dist/tokens.json` from `dist/tokens/{tokens,css}.js`. `desktop:test` depends on `build`.
- **Fonts copied, not moved.** The renderer still references `src/assets/fonts` until 8G, so moving the fonts would break its build. The fonts are copied byte for byte to `assets/fonts/`, and 8G deletes the originals with the split of `styles.css`. In `fonts.css`, the URLs become `../../assets/fonts/`. 8C rewrites those URLs to `fonts/` when it emits the platform's `theme.css`.
- **`defineReactElement`.** The listed properties become accessors on the prototype, which store the value and re-render. The root is only created when the element is connected and has `services`. `wrap` is built a single time, with the first `services`. Tested with `happy-dom` (devDependency of `desktop/ui`).
  - Replaced by bundle isolation, stage 5(b) ([OD46](../../designs/bundle-isolation-concept.md#decisions) (a)): the root no longer belongs to the element. Every element of a class renders through `createPortal` into a single React root that the class shares, created at the class's first connect, each one inside its own error boundary, and `dispose()` removes the element's content right away (`flushSync`).

## Stage 8 — `packages/desktop` (8D)

- **Package written by hand in the format of the existing `tsc` packages** (`storage`): `project.json` with `build`/`typecheck`/`test` through `nx:run-commands`, exports with the `@org/source` condition, `lib: ["es2022", "dom"]`, tests in `node --test`. Besides `.`, the subpath `desktop/protocol` exposes only the types and zod schemas of the protocol, so that main can import them without the DOM code. The link in `node_modules` came from `npm install`; the references of the root `tsconfig.json` are left to the `nx sync` of 8I.
- **`WireError`/`WireScope` are not imported.** The stage 4 envelope is not exported by `bundle/runtime`, and 8D does not change `bundle`. That is why `protocol.ts` defines `BridgeError { code, message, details? }` and `BridgeScope = Omit<CallScope, 'windowKey'>`, which have the same shape.
- **`RemoteMethodSpec = { tuple, local, stream }`.** It joins the shape of 13.5 with the `stream` of 11.3a. `remoteMethodSpecFromSchema(input, output)` reads the published JSON Schemas. A tuple gives the size [S8-12]. `x-doric-stream` on the last item marks a stream. Any other `x-doric-local` (or a local output of a method that is not a stream) makes the method `local`, because only JSON and streams cross the desktop bridge. A tuple whose whole member becomes a local marker publishes `tuple: null`, which does not matter, because the method is `local`.
- **Own remote records, without `remoteRecord`.** The stage 4 `remoteRecord` depends on a `ProcessLink`. The window's record calls `desktop.call`/`subscribe`. The input is a `z.tuple` of `z.unknown().optional()` with the published size, or `z.unknown()`, and the output is `z.unknown()`. The real validation stays in main (ajv) and in the provider. No ajv in the renderer: the packaged CSP has no `unsafe-eval`. An abort sends `cancel(id)` and waits for main's answer (a notice, as in the envelope). A stream method sends `subscribe` with the tuple without the listener and returns a synchronous `Disposable`. The first `end` closes the observer, and events after it are discarded.
- **`scope.windowKey` local through a window `Directory`.** The stage 1 `dispatch` calls `createHopCall` without `windowKey`, and changing that would require editing `bundle`. Instead, `WindowDirectory` wraps the local records (not the remote ones), and each `invoke` receives a child call with `windowKey` = the window's key, created by `createHopCall({ arg: call, windowKey })`. The call inherits signal, deadline and generation; the trace gains a child span. The splash (`key: null`) uses the plain directory. Calls that leave the window still have no `windowKey`, which main fills in.
- **Local `doric.tokens/v1` as a direct record in the directory.** `definePlatformService` is not public in `bundle/contracts`. The record uses principal `doric`, version `PLATFORM_VERSION` and the same schema as the platform specification. The others (`catalog`, `bundle`, `log`, `diagnostics`) come from `providePlatformServices`, without the distribution members.
- **Diagnostics forwarded by `WindowDiagnostics`**, a subclass of `DiagnosticsStore`. It sends, fire-and-forget, on `doric.shell/v1.diagnostic`, the items `{ kind: 'quarantine', bundleId, reason }`, `{ kind: 'report', entry }` and `{ kind: 'recovery', reason }`. `doric.shell/v1.log` receives the `LogRecord` unchanged. Failures of those calls are discarded.
- **Commands coming from main (18.3 revised) already in 8D.** `serveShellCommands` subscribes to the `doric.shell/v1.commands` stream and runs `services.use(CommandsV1).execute(command, input)` with the principal `doric`. Then it answers `commandResult` with `{ id, ok: true }` or with `{ id, ok: false, error }`. Without a `commands/v1` provider in the client, which is the case for stage 8 with the legacy, the answer is `unavailable`.
- **Keys.** The table of `doric.shell/v1.keybindings` is `{ key, command }[]`, with `key` in Electron's accelerator format. `CmdOrCtrl` is Meta on macOS and Control on the other systems, and the modifiers must match exactly. A key that matches calls `keybinding({ key })` without waiting for the answer and calls `preventDefault()`. With the empty table of stage 8, nothing is intercepted (R9).
- **Bootstrap.** `bootstrapWindow` accepts injectable `importPart` and `keyTarget`, besides `desktop` and `document`, for the tests. An error in any step up to `mount` produces `reportFailure({ kind: 'bootstrap' })`. With the core quarantined in the `workspace` window, it produces `reportFailure({ kind: 'core', bundleId, message })`, where `message` joins the reasons with `; `. The flush passes `request.deadline` to `onFlushHook` when the deadline is greater than zero (stage 9 revision). The CSS `<link>`s receive `data-bundle` with the bundle's ID.

## Stage 8 — `app/doric-platform` (8C)

- **One esbuild per package, with `splitting`.** Each folder (`react@<v>`, `@lexical/<nome>@<v>`, `zod@<v>`, …) comes out of a single build with every subpath as an entry, so shared internal modules (e.g. `zod/v4/core` between `zod` and `zod/mini`) become `chunk-*.js` and have a single instance. Every specifier of the import map is external to every build, including the package itself: `react-dom/client.js` imports `react-dom`, and `scheduler` is bundled inside `react-dom`. Conditions `[<modo>, 'module']`, `process.env.NODE_ENV` defined, `minify` only in production.
- **Transitive Lexical.** Starting from the renderer's 11 packages plus `lexical`, the build follows the `@lexical/*` dependencies (24 packages, including `@lexical/yjs`, which bundles `yjs`). `@lexical/react` does not export a root, so there is no `@lexical/react` key, only the subpaths. The duplicate subpaths `./X.js` point to the same file as `./X`. `exports` wildcards (`zod/v4/locales/*`) are expanded into the existing `.js` files.
- **`PLATFORM_VERSION` comes from `bundle/runtime`** (dist), and the `bundle@`/`desktop@` folders use that value. Since phase 1 of the redesign, the folders use `PLATFORM_ASSETS_VERSION` (`1.0.0`), which does not go up with `PLATFORM_VERSION`.
- **Licenses.** The package's own `LICENSE` when it exists (the platform packages have none; since `b0a32f2b` the repository has an MIT `LICENSE` at the root). In `licenses/<pkg>@<v>.txt`, each bundled package, read from esbuild's `metafile`. Packages without a license file (several `@radix-ui/*`) get a text with the `license` field of the manifest, instead of failing the build.
- **`theme.css`** is compiled with `@tailwindcss/node` + oxide's `Scanner`, like `build/parts.ts`, and 8C rewrites `../../assets/fonts/` to `fonts/`. The fonts and the two OFLs go to `desktop@1.0.0/fonts/`, and `tokens.json` is copied from `packages/desktop/dist`.
- **Nx outputs listed per folder**, not the whole `assets/platform`, because `splash@<v>/` (8F) lives in the same folder and a cache restore must not delete it. For the same reason, the build only cleans the folders it emits itself. `inputs` includes `^default` (sources of `bundle` and `desktop/ui`) and `NODE_ENV`.
- **Absolute import map values `doric://app/platform/…`**, as in the design. The rebase to `file://` in the `utilityProcess` stays with 8F.
- **Load test in Node.** `tests/fixtures/apply-import-map.mjs` applies the map with `module.registerHooks`, and the CommonJS `require('react')` of `react-dom/server` also goes through it. So a component with `useState` renders `<Button>` with a single React instance. `DORIC_PLATFORM_ASSETS` allows testing another output, such as the `development` mode.

## Stage 8 — the core's desktopMain part (8E)

- **`src/shared/` with more files than the design.** Besides `hooks.ts` and `log.ts`, the modules that they and the local `settings/v1` use, all environment-independent, were moved from `src/host/`: `partition.ts`, `redact.ts`, `state.ts`, `writes.ts`, `issues.ts` and `settings/registry.ts` (now `shared/settings-registry.ts`). So `shared/` does not import `host/`. The imports of the host and of the tests were updated, with no change in behavior.
- **`OpenSettingsCommandV1` keeps the explicit type** `ref<CommandDefinition<'core.open-settings/v1', typeof openSettingsInput>>`, instead of `ref<typeof openSettings>`. With `import type` from `src/desktop`, the bundle's `tsc` would start compiling and emitting the desktop part, which `tsconfig.json` excludes and which esbuild generates. The two types are identical, and `desktop/commands.ts` has an assignment that stops compiling if they diverge.
- **Typecheck of the part.** `tsconfig.parts.json` (`noEmit`, `include: src/desktop/**`) runs in the `typecheck` target. `tsconfig.json` excludes `src/desktop/**` and `src/client/**`. `tsconfig.spec.json` still includes `src/**`, and the `test` target also runs `tests/desktop/*.test.js`.
- **`commands/v1` defined by ID** (`defineService(CommandsV1.id, …)`). The overload by ref does not accept the tuple of `commandsMethods.execute`, because the first published item is `string`, and not `ContractId`. Invalid input for the command becomes `invalid_input`, an ID without a registration becomes `unknown_command` [S8-17] and a repeated ID becomes `registration_conflict`, in the synchronous `register`.
- **`windows/v1`.** The core's policy in stage 8 is the identity, and the only refusal is the empty key (`invalid_input`), checked before the hook. The handler of `onBeforeOpenWindowHook` sees the request without prefix and the inherited `call.scope.windowKey`. It does not see the original `call.caller`, because the caller is never inherited and the hook is run by the core. The caller appears only in the key's prefix, and the design (22.1) assumed the opposite.
- **Local `settings/v1`** uses the codes of the real contract (`settings_conflict`, `unknown_settings`, `settings_missing`, `invalid_input`), not the design's `conflict`. The file is `<doric.cache/v1 directory>/settings.json` (`{ generation, spaces }`). It is read on the first read and rewritten through a temporary file and `rename` on each `set`, in the core's write queue, and only comes into existence on the first `set`.
- **Version and baseline.** The core goes to `1.3.0`, with `commands/v1`, `windows/v1`, `menus/v1`, `core.hooks/v1` and `core.open-settings/v1` in `provides` and with `hooks/v1`, `log/v1` and `settings/v1` also in `desktopMain`. The build's comparator classifies a new environment in a published contract as a shape change (`major`), although the schemas are the same and the host's consumers do not change. That is why the baseline `contracts.published.json` was regenerated by the `baseline` command, as the design says. The comparator's rule was not changed (it belongs to `packages/bundle`).
- **Tests** (`tests/desktop/*`) activate the `desktopMain` environment in-process with `resolveEnvironment` and `activateEnvironment`, the desktop part as the build emits it (`dist/desktop/index.js`) and a fake of main's broker (`doric.windows/v1`, `doric.menus/v1`, `doric.cache/v1`) registered in the directory as a remote provider, which is the real boundary of the utility process.

## Stage 8 — renderer shell (8G)

- **Root chosen by a pure function.** `src/legacy/root.ts` (`legacyRootFor`) decides `settings` for `/settings` and `/settings/…` and `workspace` for the rest, and `legacy/mount.tsx` applies it to `location.pathname`. So the `legacy-mount.test.ts` test runs in Node without a DOM, like the other renderer tests. `legacy/settings.tsx` sets `document.title = 'Doric Settings'` (the title of the old `settings.html`). The `Toaster` comes from `desktop/ui/sonner`.
- **Nx's `index.html` with `baseHref: false` and `deployUrl: '/platform/shell@1.0.0/'`.** "Removing `baseHref`" would make the plugin use the default `/` and emit `<base href="/">`. With `deployUrl` the injected scripts and CSS become absolute under the shell's path, and the page has no `<base>`. `output.publicPath` is the same path, for the dynamic chunks.
- **`styles.css` also does `@source` of `packages/desktop/src`.** With two sheets, a utility that both emit counts by its position in the later sheet. If the renderer's sheet contains every utility of `theme.css` in the same order, the cascade is that of a single sheet. Comparison done (Tailwind 4.3.3, the old sheet of commit `a1e3e08` against `theme.css` + the new one): 976 utility selectors, the same and in the same order. The only declaration differences are the `.dark` font, radius and shadow variables that `tokens.css` does not repeat (`:root` already defines them with the same value on the same element) and the theme's new `--doric-*` and `.chrome-bar` variables. In `@reference` mode, Tailwind emits `var(--x, valor)`, so theme variables not used by `theme.css` are not missing. The `stylesheet.test.ts` test pins the inclusion and the order.
- **Deletions blocked by permission.** The environment refused to delete versioned files. They stay on disk, but unused: `src/components/ui/**`, `components.json`, `src/hooks/use-mobile.ts`, `src/utility/utils.ts` and `tests/palette-contrast.test.ts`. No code imports them after the codemod, and `src/assets/fonts/*.woff2` (exact copies in `desktop/ui`) also stays. That is why the assets rule that ignores `fonts/*.woff2` was kept. Removing it now would copy fonts that no CSS uses. The renderer's `palette-contrast.test.ts` reads the tokens of `styles.css` and fails until it is deleted (the version in `desktop/ui` already runs).
- **Project references.** `tsconfig.app.json` references `desktop/client` and `desktop/ui` (what `nx sync` generates), so that `typecheck` resolves `@org/source` without putting another package's sources in the `rootDir`.

## Stage 8 — Electron platform (8F)

- **Main plays the role of kernel through the public types.** `bundle/runtime` now exports the stage 4 envelope, the runner (`runGeneration`), `definePlatformService` and `platformContracts`: main supplies `doric.*` services, which bundles cannot, and that is why they stay out of `bundle/contracts`. Main keeps a directory with `doric.windows|menus|cache|tokens|notifications|log/v1`, sends them in the `init` as `kernelProviders`, and registers what the generation advertises (`provide`) in a directory per generation.
- **Lifecycle hooks through `hooks/v1.execute(AppHooksV1, …)`.** `runGeneration` does not handle the `lifecycle` control messages, and the runner belongs to `packages/bundle`. So main calls the `hooks/v1` advertised by the generation with the caller `doric`, the owner of `AppHooksV1` (the codec crosses `AppHooksV1` by identity). Without a provider, `onBeforeQuitHook` allows and the others do nothing, as in 13.3.
- **One instance of `bundle/contracts` per generation.** The runner is an esbuild build with `splitting` of three entries: `generation-runner.mjs` (registers `module.registerHooks` before any module with `zod`), `generation-runner-main.mjs` and `generation-contracts.mjs`. In the process, `bundle/contracts` resolves to `generation-contracts.mjs`, which shares the chunks with the runner's runtime. The platform's `bundle@1.0.0/contracts.js` does not serve here, because the runtime imports internal names that `contracts/index.ts` does not export. `zod` stays external and resolves to the platform's files, and `<id>/contracts` to the cache. The hook also marks as ESM the `.js` files of the platform and of the cache, which have no `package.json` with `type`.
- **The utility process channel.** `child.postMessage` and `process.parentPort` (structured clone) instead of a `MessageChannelMain` pair. `stdio: 'inherit'`, and the runner's arguments (import map and parts) go in argv, as base64 JSON. An exit after `ready` drops the generation's items and shortcuts and restarts the process once, without a new `onAppReadyHook`.
- **The host bridge `/doric/bridge` (contract with 8H).** Each envelope message is emitted under the name of its `kind`, carrying the whole message: `call`, `cancel`, `subscribe` and `unsubscribe` from the desktop, and `result`, `event` and `end` from the host. Without a connection, or with the deadline past, the call answers `unavailable` / `Doric backend is unavailable.`, and a connection loss closes the streams with `disconnected`. A refusal by the server (namespace middleware), which Socket.IO does not retry, is also tried again every 1 s (P4).
- **Artifacts.** The cache downloads all distributed files of every bundle that is not disabled, and not only the desktopMain and client parts, because `<id>/contracts` and the schemas of any bundle may be needed (the core's desktop imports `core/contracts`). Each file goes through staging, `fsync` and `rename`. On a verification failure, the bundle is quarantined with `invalid_part` and the message `artifact hash mismatch: <path>`.
- **`distributionFiles` includes the closure of relative imports of `contracts/**.js`** (`packages/bundle/src/loader/distribution.ts`). `core/contracts`re-exports`host/models/catalog.js`, and without that file the desktop did not load `core/contracts`. The test is in `distribution.test.ts`.
- **Menu: the Electron 44 default has no Help.** The comparison in Electron itself (`Menu.getApplicationMenu()` at `ready`, unpackaged) showed App, File, Edit, View and Window. That is why the `help` role only appears with contributed items, and the four links of S8-15 are not reproduced (S8-15 revised). A generation that has not defined a skeleton uses the last one defined, with its own items, because the core only defines the skeleton in `onAppReadyHook`, which a background generation does not receive.
- **`ready()` before `onAppReadyHook`** (a window opened at activation, like the minimal test core) counts as the first `ready()` as soon as the hook fires.
- **Quarantine evidence of the client part (22.1).** A contract that no bundle supplies gives `missing_dependency` at resolution, not `dependency_quarantined`. That code appears when the provider of the dependency fails. That is why the case is in `desktop/client/tests/bootstrap.test.ts` (a provider that fails at activation, the dependent quarantined, the others active and `ready()` called), and not in `activate.test.ts`, which does not reach `ready()`. A desktop quarantine stops the splash at `bundle-failure` until "Continue without them". That is why the `doric.diagnostics/v1.report` report to the fake host is checked in `tests-electron/splash.test.ts`, and not together with the bundles of `bridge.test.ts`.
- **Quit (17.3).** A close held by the protocol cancels Electron's quit. When the window actually closes during an already decided quit, main calls `app.quit()` again.
- **Migration of the selection.** The static page of `doric://app` is `splash@<v>/blank.html` (from 8F), and not `shell@<v>/blank.html` (from the renderer). The `localStorage` of `file://` is read from `assets/legacy-origin.html`.
- **Origin migration owned by the core [closing of a platform leak].** The migration code of `doric.selection` and the name of the `selectionOrigin` marker left the platform. The platform offers `doric.migrations/v1.storage({ key, marker })`, only for `desktopMain`, and returns `migrated`, `none`, `skipped` or `failed`. The caller chooses only the key and the marker; the `file://` origin page, the `doric://app` page, the packaged mode and the logging of failures belong to the platform, and the contract accepts no origin field. The marker is a record of the state (`migrations`), with names limited by a pattern and at most 32 entries; the file of existing profiles keeps `selectionOrigin: true`. The core (`bundles/foundation/core/src/desktop/migrations.ts`) sends `{ key: 'doric.selection', marker: 'selectionOrigin' }` and awaits the answer in `onAppReadyHook`, before opening the primary window; a call failure is logged by the core and does not prevent the window. The `DesktopPlatform.start(callback)` call went away: `main.ts` no longer passes a migration. Kept: it copies only for packaged profiles, never overwrites a destination value, runs once, a failure logs and marks it as done, and the hidden windows are closed. Difference in the diagnostic log: the failure line is now `Storage origin migration failed` (before, `Selection origin migration failed`) and carries the marker. Consequence for order: the migration runs when the core reaches `onAppReadyHook`; in recovery mode, without an active core, it only runs when a core activates. The layout keeps its own `doric.selection` constant for the rendering's `localStorage`; the two copies must match.
- **Legacy channels.** `senderIsAllowed(url)` accepts the `doric://app` origin on any path [S8-1], and `registerWorkspaceHandlers` and `registerTerminalHandlers` no longer receive `allowedUrls` and gain `available()`. In `last-good-core` mode, all of them answer `Doric backend is unavailable.`. `doric:settings:flushed` and the `doric:settings:flush` push go away, and the preload's `settings.onFlush` runs in the flush phase of the close protocol. `doric:settings:open` runs `core.open-settings/v1` in desktopMain with the caller `doric`.
- **Preload.** A window without a close handler, or whose veto handler fails, answers `{ allow: true }`, so as never to trap the window. The channel names are literals in the sandboxed preload, and a test compares them with those of `desktop/client`.
- **Dispatcher.** A bundle without an active client part in the window's generation receives `unavailable`. A `doric.*` contract, or one outside `requires` ∪ `provides`, receives `undeclared_requirement`. A window whose shell is from a generation that main no longer retains is reloaded, and the call answers `unavailable`. In a desktopMain stream, the listener crosses the link as a function (callback codec), so the provider's `end` does not cross; the end arrives with the process's exit. A `scope.windowKey` coming from the window is already refused by the strict schema, and main fills it in.
- **Tokens and chrome.** `doric.tokens/v1` replicates the `resolvedVariables` of `desktop/ui` (`--doric-window-background: var(--background)`), and a test pins the equality. `titleBar: 'none'` has a zero safe area. The `WindowCatalog` without `storage` publishes `hostId: ''` and `storageGeneration: 0`.
- **Build of main.** `app/doric/webpack.config.js` restricts `ts-loader` to `src/`, because the workspace packages come from the ESM `dist` and recompiling them as CommonJS swapped imports for `require`. `externalDependencies: ['socket.io-client']` bundles `bundle`, `desktop/client`, `zod` and `ajv` into main and into the sandboxed preload. The outputs of `build` are listed per file, because `assets/platform` and the runner belong to other targets. `build-runner` (esbuild) emits the runner and the splash in `dist/app/doric/assets`.
- **Electron tests.** `doric-app:test-electron` runs `node:test` with `isolation: 'none'` inside Electron's main, in a temporary profile. An in-process run only ends at `beforeExit`, which Electron never reaches, so the runner emits it when all top-level tests have finished. The harness's clock skips the splash's 5 s (the minimum time is covered by the unit test with a fake clock). The fake host is a Socket.IO server in the same process.
- **Questions Q1–Q5 as in the cross-review §6.** Q1: main writes only the last path per key, in `doric/windows.json`, without bounds. Q2: the 30 s deadline and the wait for "Continue without them". Q3: development profiles do not migrate. Q4: the recovery buttons appear and call `doric.catalog/v1.disable({ bundleId })` and `resetComposition()` on the host (HR-6). If the call fails, the failure goes to the log and the same state comes back. Q5: the isolation stays the same as today.

## Stage 8 — host bridge and catalog (8H)

- **A seam in the kernel instead of a provider of the host's own.** `startKernel` gains the `distribution(source)` option, and the kernel passes it on to `providePlatformServices`, because `doric.catalog/v1` is a single platform provider and the host cannot register another. The `KernelDistributionSource` exposes the published catalog and the live states of the generation that the call sees (0 = the active one), the retained generations, the storage identity and `onActive`. The logic (HR-2..HR-4) is in `agents/doric/src/lib/bridge/catalog.ts`.
- **`KernelPlatform.methodSpec(contract, method)`.** The bridge needs the spec of the active provider to spread the input tuple and to tell a stream method apart (`x-doric-stream`). Without a provider, the call goes on to the stub, which answers `unavailable` (or waits for the respawning generation, G-11).
- **Bridge calls run as principal `doric` in the active view.** The stub validates the input with zod, applies the message's `scope` and `deadline`, and `cancel` aborts the call. The message's `caller` and `trace` are not propagated (the call opens a new trace). In stage 8 the desktop only makes platform hops (`caller` `doric`, D-14); the per-caller allowlist is HR-10 (stage 9).
- **`call` on a stream and `subscribe` on a plain method answer `invalid_input`**, as in the desktop dispatcher. A malformed message receives `invalid_input` (`result` or `end`) when it has an `id`.
- **Files per generation, live states.** The hashes are computed once per published catalog (`distributionFor`). On each `distribution()` the host recomputes the snapshot, the states, the reasons and the `storage` (HR-8). `artifact` looks for the hash in the retained generations and in the active one, rereads the file and checks the SHA-256 before serving. An unknown hash, or one whose file changed on disk, answers `not_found`.
- **`watch` emits only when the number of the active generation changes.** A respawn reactivates the same generation and published nothing.
- **`hostBoot`.** `randomUUID()` per host process, created in `startBundles` (option `hostBoot` for tests).
- **Startup log unchanged.** The `Network interfaces configured` line still has `['/status', '/projects', '/threads']`, for parity. `/doric/bridge` does not enter that list.
- **HR-7.** The Dockerfile's commands do not change: the `cli.js build` of each bundle already builds the parts with esbuild (devDependency of the root), and `stage` copies the whole `dist`. Only the comment was updated. The test checks that the core in `dist/bundles` has a distributed `desktop/index.js`.

## Stage 8 — composition recovery actions (HR-6)

- **Overrides file next to the composition (Q4).** The host keeps the recovery actions in `composition.overrides.json`, in the directory of the active composition (`compositionOverridesPath(DORIC_COMPOSITION)`), never in the product's database. The format is `{ "disabled": string[] }`, strict. The write is atomic: a temporary file renamed over it. The composition file is never rewritten. `.gitignore` ignores `composition.overrides.json`, because the development default falls in `agents/doric/`.
