- **Applied on every catalog read.** `readHostCatalog` reads the overrides after the composition (option `compositionOverrides` of `StartHostOptions`) and removes each disabled bundle from `enabled`. This holds at boot and on each `publish()`. The catalog then shows the bundle with the `disabled` state that resolution already uses for bundles outside `enabled`, and the desktop removes it from `enabled` [S8-8]. An unreadable file becomes a catalog problem (`issues`) and is ignored: the composition as written holds, so that a bad file does not trap the host.
- **Served by the kernel.** `doric.catalog/v1` gains `disable({ bundleId }): void` and `resetComposition(): void` (contract and schemas in `contracts/platform.ts`). The kernel only registers them with `compositionOverrides`; `startBundles` always passes the path (option `compositionOverridesPath` for tests). The logic is in `process/composition-actions.ts`: the actions run one at a time, write or delete the file and call `generations.publish()`.
- **Refusals.** `disable` of a bundle outside the `enabled` of the written composition answers `not_found`. `disable` of the core answers `invalid_input`, because without a core the host does not come up and can no longer receive "Return to default composition". In both cases nothing is written.
- **Change in the kernel.** When the publication answers `kernel_restart_required` or `failed`, the override stays written and holds at the next boot. The call answers `unavailable` with the reason, and the desktop logs `Recovery action failed` and returns to the same state.
- **Log lines** (platform log): `Bundle disabled` `{ bundleId, path }`, `Composition reset` `{ path }`, `Composition published` `{ generation, path }`, `Composition change applies at the next host start` (warn, `{ status, reason, path }`) and `Composition overrides replaced` (warn, when `disable` replaces an unreadable file). The documents define no log lines for HR-6; these are the minimum to audit the actions.
- **Desktop unchanged.** 8F already called the two methods. The splash's Electron tests use a fake host that serves the methods and publishes a new distribution; the two buttons reach the `workspace` window.

## Stage 8 — Electron integration and E2E (8F integration, 8J)

- **One remote route per contract in the window.** The core supplies `hooks/v1`, `log/v1` and `settings/v1` in `desktopMain` and in the host. `generationView` listed both routes in `WindowCatalog.remote`, and the window's directory refused the second registration (`Contract "hooks/v1" already has an active provider`). Because of that, every window of the real app failed at bootstrap. Now `remote` has one record per contract, the same one that `routeOf` chooses (`desktopMain` first, then host).
- **The fake host of the Electron tests publishes the core's host contracts.** `tests/support/core.ts` now lists the host contracts of the core's descriptor, as the real host does. Without that, the defect above did not show up in the Electron tests. With the fix undone, `platform.test` fails again.
- **`windows-protocol.test` (Electron).** It uses `net.fetch('doric://app/…')`: real platform, bundle and schema files answer 200 with the file's type. Missing files, unknown bundles, files outside the distribution, traversals and `doric://other/` answer 404, and the other paths answer the shell. It also covers the semantics of `open`: the same key focuses the window and navigates to another path, different keys open different windows, `focusOrOpen` of a key never opened answers `not_found`, and the last path is reopened and survives a restart (`doric/windows.json`). Chromium resolves `..` and `%2e%2e` before the handler. That is why traversal is tested with `%2F` and `%5C`.
- **Stage 8 E2E in a copy of the harness** (`scratchpad/e2e-stage8`). It uses its own compose project, ports and fake model image, the renderer on port 4201 through a patch in the checkout, and the changes of §23 in `ui/lib/app.mjs`. The readiness probe sends `accept: text/html`, because `/` only answers through the dev server's history fallback. The copy's normalizations (the model's port and the local date before `<clock>`) apply to both sides and change no assertion.
- **The host image needs `@tailwindcss/oxide` at the root.** `packages/bundle/src/build/parts.ts` imports `@tailwindcss/oxide`. In the checkout it arrives through `@tailwindcss/postcss`, which is a dependency of the renderer. The image does not copy `app/`, so `npm ci` does not install it and the Dockerfile's `tsc --build` fails. The E2E image was built with `npm install -D --save-exact @tailwindcss/oxide@4.3.3` applied only to the copy of the checkout. The same command must be run at the root (8I/8A).

## Stage 8 — design decisions (S8-1 to S8-23)

They complement the "Desktop and UI" section. They sit in a section of their own, like D4-1 to D4-18 of stage 4.

- **S8-1** Origin checks compare `protocol === 'doric:'` and `host === 'app'`, never `origin`, which is `'null'` for `doric:`.
- **S8-2** The shell knows neither routes nor titles; each page sets `document.title`.
- **S8-3** Platform paths use `PLATFORM_VERSION` (`1.0.0`; since phase 1 of the redesign, `PLATFORM_ASSETS_VERSION`); third-party paths use the npm version.
- **S8-4** `reservedWidth = 80` (today's `pl-20`), in the tokens.
- **S8-5** The safe area is constant (left 80) on every platform and in full screen, preserving today's layout. `titleBar: 'none'` has a zero area.
- **S8-6** `doric.windows/v1.lastPath(key)` (minimal API); the core reopens `workspace` at it. Only the path is kept, without bounds (Q1).
- **S8-7** `doric.catalog/v1` and `doric.diagnostics/v1` exist in `desktopMain` and in the client.
- **S8-8** The host's quarantine reasons reach the desktop's resolution; disabled bundles leave `enabled`.
- **S8-9** `doric.catalog/v1.distribution()`, `artifact({ sha256 })` (base64) and `watch()`.
- **S8-10** `WINDOW_READY_DEADLINE_MS = 30_000`.
- **S8-11** `doric.shell/v1` and `doric.splash/v1` are internal technical contracts, callable only by the window's platform (`bundleId: null`) and by the splash.
- **S8-12** Remote records normalize only by tuple size; validation stays in the provider and at the process boundary.
- **S8-13** Stage 8 keeps the legacy React tree bundled by webpack; the platform's React serves only the bundle parts.
- **S8-14** Skeleton labels are ignored on entries with a role.
- **S8-15** (revised in 8F) The Electron 44 default has no Help; the `help` role only appears with contributed items.
- **S8-16** No Settings menu item nor `Cmd+,` in stage 8 (parity above the concept's skeleton).
- **S8-17** `commands/v1.execute` of an unknown ID throws `unknown_command`; main falls back to the focused window.
- **S8-18** The bundle's own contracts are the external `<id>/contracts` in the built parts.
- **S8-19** The generation runner is `assets/generation-runner.mjs`.
- **S8-20** Streams: the `streamListener(item)` marker, the `{ next, end? }` observer, the `subscribe`/`event`/`unsubscribe`/`end` bridge, and `end(disconnected)` on loss of the bridge.
- **S8-21** The import map has exactly the specifiers of the decision; Radix and lucide are bundled in `desktop/ui`, and `toast` is re-exported by `desktop/ui/sonner`.
- **S8-22** The desktop's local generation numbers are keyed by `(hostId, hostBoot, geração do host)` (G-6); HR-2 publishes `hostBoot`.
- **S8-23** The dispatcher allows a client part its `requires` plus its `provides`; an unreachable host and the deadline answer `unavailable` / `Doric backend is unavailable.`; the host bridge's allowlist is HR-10 (stage 9).
- **G-16** `WindowHooksV1` is defined in the client already in stage 8, earlier than `migration.md` says. Only fixtures use it, because production has no client part in stage 8.
- **Root (8I).** `@tailwindcss/oxide@4.3.3` comes in as an exact devDependency of the root (via `npm install`), because `packages/bundle/src/build/parts.ts` imports it and the host image does not install the renderer's dependencies. After the consolidation, `npx nx sync` keeps a reference to `packages/desktop` in the root `tsconfig.json`.
- **HR-6 implemented** (see "Stage 8 — composition recovery actions (HR-6)"). The splash's recovery buttons call `disable` and `resetComposition` on the host, which publishes a new generation.

## Stage 9 — platform deltas (WP0)

- **Contracts.** `navigation/v1.chain()` (D9-3), `regions/v1.items`/`subscribe` with `RegionEntry.owner` (D9-4), `doric.elements/v1` (D9-2) and `doric.connection/v1` (D9-5) only in the client, `doric.windows/v1.focus(key)` (D9-7), and `doric.notifications/v1` with environments `['desktopMain', 'client']` (D9-6, transitional). New error code `route_conflict` (D9-18). `WINDOW_FLUSH_DEADLINE_MS` stays in main (cross-review), not in `bundle/contracts`.
- **`doric.shell/v1`.** It gains the `connection` stream (main pushes the current status on subscribe and then each change, to every window) and the `notify` call (validated with `notificationRequestSchema` and shown by main's single notification function). The status comes from the `/status` monitor via `ConnectionRelay` (`app/doric/src/connection/relay.ts`), which reads `disconnected` until `main.ts` attaches the monitor. The legacy `doric:connection:status` push to the `workspace` window stays until WP6.
- **`createElementScope(principal)`.** A child scope of the owner's activation scope. `provide`, `whenAvailable` and every stub call made through the element's `services`, even later (a kept stub, a hook registrar), run in that child. For that, the stubs and the facades with functions are wrapped in a Proxy. Data and plain Disposables come back unwrapped.
- **Bootstrap.** `mount` becomes optional. Without `mount`, `<${core}-outlet>` replaces the children of `body` (D9-1). Without the tag defined, the result is `reportFailure({ kind: 'core', message: 'Core bundle "<core>" did not define <<core>-outlet>.' })`. The core quarantined in the `workspace` window still reports its own reason. The legacy renderer keeps passing `mount` and `flushParticipants` until WP6, which removes both.
- **Recorder with a DOM.** `build/dom-shim.ts` installs only the missing globals (`HTMLElement`, `customElements`, `document`, `window`/`self`, observers, `matchMedia`, `localStorage`/`sessionStorage`, `requestAnimationFrame`, `navigator.clipboard`) during the import and the dry activation of the client part, and removes them afterwards. The import failure cites the module and the rule. The stage 8 case ("refuses a client part that touches the DOM during activate") was replaced by the stage 9 cases.
- **Parts (`parts.ts`).** `src/client/workers/*.ts` → `dist/client/workers/*.js`, without externals, because workers do not see the import map. The `.woff2`/`.woff`/`.ttf` loaders become `file` in `client/assets/`. The CSS that esbuild extracts from the JS imports is appended after the Tailwind output in `client/styles.css`, and the intermediate `index.css` is removed. The externals stay those of stage 8.
- **`bundle/testing`.** `createMinimalHooks` was promoted from `tests/support` to `src/testing/minimal-hooks.ts`, and the test file now re-exports it. `minimalUiCore({ id? })` is the client part of the minimal core: `hooks/v1`, `ui/v1` (tag prefix), a minimal `navigation/v1` (static paths and `:param`), `regions/v1` and `<id>-outlet` with `<p>Minimal core</p>`. It is a fixture, not the default core.
- **`desktop/ui/react-element`.** It gains `portalHosts(properties)` (D9-12): a `display: contents` host element without a root of its own and `useHosts()` via `useSyncExternalStore`, in connection order.
- **R-4 verified.** `desktop/ui/src/components` differs from `app/doric-renderer/src/components/ui` only in the import paths and in the re-export of `toast` (S8-21).
- **Dependency.** `happy-dom@20.8.9` came in as a devDependency of `packages/desktop` (via `npm install`; the lockfile only gains the workspace entry).

## Stage 9 — E2E harness (WP7)

- **The `e2e-stage9` copy.** It starts from `e2e-stage8` with the stage 8 change set already present (`DORIC_E2E_WINDOW_URL`, optional `settings.html` probe). The default compose project becomes `doric-e2e-stage9` so as not to collide with the stage 8 copy; the ports stay those of stage 8 and must not run in parallel.
- **Selection fallback (D9-20).** `readSelection(page)` in `ui/lib/context.mjs` reads `localStorage['doric.selection']` and, if the key does not exist, rebuilds `{ selectedThreadId, version: 1 }` from `/projects/:projectId/threads/:threadId` in the window's URL (`null` outside that route, as in the baseline without a selection). `settle()` and u14 use that helper; no assertion, role or accessible name changed.

## Stage 9 — the core's client part (WP1)

- **Files.** `bundles/foundation/core/src/client/**` (ui, navigation, route-match, regions, commands, links, menu-context and the four elements), `src/desktop/commands-bridge.ts` (`core.commands/v1`, D9-8) and `CoreCommandsV1` in `core/contracts`. The manifest goes to 1.4.0 with `ui/v1`, `navigation/v1`, `regions/v1` and `core.commands/v1`; the baseline `contracts.published.json` was regenerated, because semver treats a new environment of `hooks/v1`, `log/v1` and `commands/v1` as a shape change.
- **Local contracts win over the remote ones in the window.** The `remote` of the window's catalog carries every desktopMain contract, including the core's `hooks/v1`, `log/v1` and `commands/v1`, and the window's directory refused the client provider with `provider_conflict`. `activateClientBundles` (`desktop/client/src/activate.ts`) does not register the remote record of a contract that the window's client resolution already provides. The desktopMain provider stays reachable only through the local provider (`core.commands/v1`).
- **`display: contents` in `connectedCallback`.** A custom element constructor cannot create attributes (`document.createElement` fails in Chromium), so the structural elements apply the style on connect. `<core-unavailable>` stays `hidden` for the same reason.
- **The core has no CSS of its own.** The utilities of `<core-not-found>` (`p-4`, `text-sm`, `text-muted-foreground`) are already in the platform's `theme.css`. A second utilities sheet loaded after it would reorder the cascade (for example `.hidden` after `md:flex`). `src/client/styles.css` was removed.
- **Same origin by scheme and host.** `doric://app` is not a special scheme, so `URL.origin` is `"null"` for it and for any other non-special URL. `navigate` and the link interception compare `protocol` and `host`.
- **Route conflict message.** `route_conflict` names the requested path and the bundle that owns the earlier one: `The path "<p>" is already registered by <owner>.`. The quarantine code is `activate_failed`, because `route_conflict` is not in the runtime's set of registration refusals (`dispatch.ts`); the design foresaw `registration_failed`.
- **Navigation notification.** `navigate` and `popstate` always notify; a registration or its disposal notifies only when the active match changes. `current()` and `chain()` keep their identity while the match does not change. The `path` menu context is only resent when the pattern changes.
- **The client's `log/v1` without redaction.** `createLog({ redact: false })`: the window never has resolved secrets and `secrets/v1` is a host contract. Querying it on each record would make a call through the bridge.
- **Window filter in the window hooks.** A registration of `doric.window.hooks/v1` keeps `scope.windowKey`, and `execute` runs only the handlers of the call's window. Inside a window the directory always stamps its own key, so the test exercises the provider's definition.
- **D9-7.** `core.open-settings/v1` without `path` first calls `doric.windows/v1.focus('settings')` and only opens the window if it does not exist.

## Stage 9 — `history` bundle (WP5)

- **Files.** `bundles/desktop/history/**`: `src/contracts` (`HistorySnapshotsV1`, loose `z.looseObject` schemas of Thread and event, without importing `runtime/contracts`), `src/desktop/store.ts` (port of `app/doric/src/workspace/thread-history.ts`) and `src/desktop/index.ts`. Only a desktopMain part; the part's `typecheck` uses `tsconfig.parts.json`, like the core. `npm install` linked the package (`package-lock.json`) and `nx sync` added `bundles/desktop/history` to the references of the root `tsconfig.json`.
- **Identity.** The namespace is `<cache>/<encodeURIComponent(hostId)>/<storageGeneration>/<encodeURIComponent(threadId)>.json`, read from `DoricCatalogV1.get().storage` at activation. Without `storage` the store stays disabled (`read` → `null`, `write` without effect) until the next generation. The legacy directory `<userData>/thread-history/` is neither read nor deleted.
- **Routing of `write`.** Every update of `write({ threadId, updates })` applies to the call's `threadId` (the contract's `snapshot` carries no `threadId`), except `deleted`, which uses its own `threadId`. Today's `error` type does not exist in the contract (the chat does not forward it).
- **File without `thread.id`.** A file whose `thread` has no string `id` is treated as corrupted (`null`), because the output of `read` is validated by the schema; before, only an object was required.
- **Service test.** `tests/support/desktop.ts` brings up a real desktopMain with the built part, a test core with `createMinimalHooks` (`bundle/testing`) and a fake `doric.cache/v1`; it proves the namespace path, the flush in `onBeforeQuitHook` (without veto) and at deactivation, and the disabled store.

## Stage 9 — chat data (WP4a)

- **Package.** `bundles/desktop/chat` (project `bundle-chat`, version `1.0.0`): `src/contracts` (`ChatViewsV1`, `ChatLexicalV1`, `ChatHooksV1` with `onSandboxActivityHook`, `renderBlockSchema`, `CHAT_INTENT_EVENT`) and the client part with `api`, `stores`, `domain`, `utility`, `queries` and `services`. The baseline `contracts.published.json` was generated by the `contracts-baseline` target. `npm install` linked the package in `package-lock.json` and `nx sync` added `bundles/desktop/chat` to the references of the root `tsconfig.json`.
- **Relative imports.** The modules moved from the renderer swap the `@/` alias for relative paths with `.js`, because the tests run in ESM through `node --test`. The rules and the 21 ported test files (plus the cases of `changedSandboxProjects`) did not change.
- **Own types.** `domain/workspace.ts` copies `Thread`, `ThreadEvent`, `ThreadUpdate` and `ThreadHistory` from the renderer, instead of using the types of `runtime/contracts`. The host's record crosses the bridge unchanged, so the projection and the cache see the same shapes as before.
- **`threads-api` adapter.** In today's order, first main's guard runs (`The item identifier is invalid.`, `Enter a prompt before continuing.`, `Invalid prompt revision.`), then the input validation of the REST route (uuid of the Thread and of the prompt) and finally the route's message for each outcome of the host (`storageMessage` on `storage_low`). Bridge failures (`unavailable`, `disconnected`, `generation_lost`) give `Doric backend is unavailable.`; `invalid_output` gives `Doric returned an invalid response.`; any other failure gives `The request could not be completed.`.
- **Thread stream.** The chat wires `runtime.threads/v1.watchThread({ threadId, afterSequence }, observer)`, with the `ThreadWatchItem` items of WP-H: `snapshot` with the payload fields (`threadId`, `projectId`, `project`, `thread`, `events`), `event`, `updated` and `deleted`. The reading of the items is as loose as that of the socket payloads. A provider without that method answers `The Thread event stream failed.`.
- **End of the stream.** An end with `disconnected` shows nothing and resubscribes from the cursor on the next `connected` of `doric.connection/v1`, as the socket used to reconnect. An end with another error shows the message once and does not resubscribe, like the server's `disconnect()` after `workspace:error`. There are no longer "watches of the destroyed window": the watches live in the activation (D9-17) and end in `closeAll()`, at deactivation.
- **Cache.** The store hands the applied updates to `history.snapshots/v1`, converted to the flat shape of the `history` contract (`snapshot` with only `thread` and `events`). Events go in the 100 ms flush, and the held ones also go when the watch is released or at deactivation, because main's absorber wrote each event as it arrived. `error` is never written. The chat imports only the types of `history/contracts` and repeats the ID in the `ref`, because a value import would require `history/contracts` in the import map of compositions without `history`. `layout.workspace/v1` uses the same scheme, with a local structural type, until the layout publishes the contract.
- **Notification.** The Thread shown comes from the window's route (`routeThread(navigation.current())`), no longer from the selection store.
- **External blocks.** `domain/external-blocks.ts` is pure and runs each external projector over the whole list. The blocks come out sorted by `sequence`. A projector that throws does not contribute on that event and keeps its state. A versioned type without a projector becomes a `chat.generic` block, drawn by `<chat-block>`. The embedded projector (`projector.ts`) did not change: it claims the unversioned types and the aliases of `legacyEventTypes`, so registering one of them gives `registration_conflict`. A renderer with an undefined element gives `invalid_input`.
- **`chat.lexical/v1`.** The `frozen` code applies after `freeze()`, which the surface calls when it creates the window's first editor. Removing a registration after that does not change the frozen list.
- **Activation.** `index.ts` registers the route `/projects/:projectId/threads/:threadId` (parent `/`), defines the chat's elements and adds the `composer` item (order 40) to `layout.footer`. The final wiring is in "Stage 9 — integration and parity".

## Stage 9 — chat surface (WP4b)

- **Files moved.** `components/organisms/conversation/**`, `thread-queue`, the chat's 11 molecules (with the copy of `toolbar-divider`) and the hooks `use-composer`, `use-connection-status`, `use-delegated-source`, `use-queue-caret`, `use-thread-chat`, `use-thread-nav`, `use-thread-queue` and `use-thread-stop`. The logic did not change: `@/` became a relative path with `.js`, `sonner` became `desktop/ui/sonner`, `messageFrom` comes from `domain/errors.ts` and `window.doric.threads.*` became `threadsApi.*`. The removal of the renderer's copies stays with WP6.
- **Elements.** `<chat-view>` (props `projectId`, `threadId`) and `<chat-composer>` each have their own root (`StrictMode`, `chatQueryClient`, `TooltipProvider`) and do not mount `Toaster`, because the window's belongs to the layout. `<chat-block>` draws a block without a renderer: type, id, time (only in `chat.generic`) and payload as JSON.
- **The Thread's record.** `useThreadRecord` reads `layout.workspace/v1.thread(id)` with a subscription. Without a layout, it asks `threadsApi.get(id)` once per Thread. With a layout and without the Thread in the tree, nothing is drawn, as today without a selection. The service returns a new copy on each call, so the hook keeps the previous reference while the content (JSON) does not change: the conversation's effects depend on the Thread's identity, as they depended on the store's record.
- **Delegated sender.** `useDelegatedSource` uses the tree's record and opens the Thread with `navigation.navigate(threadPath(...))`. The selection comes from the route.
- **External blocks in the editor.** `ChatBlockNode` (`chat-block-node`, a stable `DecoratorNode` that cannot be deleted, like the turns) enters the list after the embedded nodes. `InsertThreadTurnNodes` receives `blocks` and, at the end of each sync pass, puts each block before the first turn opened by a later event, or above the queue and the prompt. A block that is already in place is not moved, so its element is not recreated. Without external blocks, the list is an empty constant and the sync of the turns stays identical to today's. The element is created once per tag with `ui.create(tag, { block })`, and the following versions of the block are assigned to the `block` property.
- **`ui.create` through the activation's services.** The block elements are created with `chatServices().use(UiV1)`, not with the element's services. `createElementScope` (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/services.ts`) returns a Proxy for any result with methods. A DOM element has methods, so it would stop being a `Node` and `appendChild` would fail. It is a platform defect (WP0), recorded for a fix.
- **Actions.** `onBeforeRenderBlockHook` runs per block after the projection. Until the answer, and when it fails, the block is drawn with `actions: []`. From the result only `data` and `actions` come in; `id`, `type`, `promptId` and `sequence` stay the projector's.
- **Intents.** `IntentDispatcher` is a `div` with `display: contents` (no box and no role). It listens to `chat:intent` and calls `commands/v1.execute(command, input)` through the element's services. A failure becomes `toast.error(messageFrom(error))`.
- **Lexical freeze.** The editor extension is set up once per registration, in the window's first conversation, with `freeze()`. The registered extensions enter the dependencies, after the embedded ones, and the registered nodes enter after `ChatBlockNode`. Name, namespace, theme, the 13 nodes, the markdown nodes and the 9 plugins stay in today's order.
- **Footer composer.** `<chat-composer>` draws the divider, "Stop execution" and `ComposerButton` (`composer-controls.tsx`) for `layout.workspace/v1.selectedThread()`, with the `action` and `disabled` rules of `workspace-footer.tsx`. Without a layout, there is no selected Thread and sending stays disabled.
- **CSS.** `src/client/styles.css` carries the chat rules of the renderer's `styles.css` without changing the layer. The Lexical placeholder and the conversation's scroll and highlight rules stay in `@layer base`, as they were; the queue's editing surface and the `editor-*` theme stay outside any layer. The renderer's copy goes away in WP6.
- **Build.** `bundles/desktop/chat/tsconfig.json` gained `jsx: react-jsx`, because the part's esbuild reads that file and, without the option, generated `React.createElement` without an import. `tsconfig.spec.json` compiles only the folders without JSX, and `tsconfig.client-spec.json` compiles the window tests (happy-dom).

## Stage 9 — the host's UI members (WP-H)

- **R-STREAM.** `runtime/v1.watchProject({ projectId }, listener)` (1.2.0), `runtime.threads/v1.watchThread({ threadId, afterSequence }, listener)` (1.1.0) and `runtime.terminals/v1.watchProject(projectId, listener)` (1.1.0) are stream methods (`streamListener`, output `Disposable`). The items repeat the emissions of the `/threads` and `/projects` namespaces (`runtime/contracts` `watches.ts`). The Thread snapshot keeps the whole `thread:snapshot` payload (`threadId`, `projectId`, `project`, `thread`, `events`). The Project one keeps `terminals`. The terminal items go out in a stream of their own: `snapshot`, `updated`, `removed` and `output`. A snapshot read that fails closes the stream with `ContractError('replay_failed', 'The snapshot could not be loaded.')`. An invalid input (an ID that is not a UUID, a negative cursor) answers `invalid_input`.
- **Algorithm ported, not shared.** `runtime/src/host/services/watches.ts` ports the algorithm of `transport/src/host/sockets/workspace.ts`: it subscribes before reading, holds the notices until the snapshot, deduplicates and delivers the events without gaps, and holds no terminal output before the snapshot. The `runtime` cannot depend on the `transport`, and the socket keeps its copy. The cases of `sockets.test` were ported to `runtime/tests/contracts/watches.test.ts`. A remote listener that rejects (closed window) is ignored.
- **Stream between processes.** `memberSpec` (`bundle/runtime`, `envelope/validators.ts`) turns the tuple item with `x-doric-stream` into a `streamListener`. Without that, the kernel's `methodSpec` for a provider of the generation lost the mark, and the bridge answered `is not a stream` (the bridge → kernel → generation path did not work for any bundle stream).
- **`tools/v1.catalog()` (1.1.0, core 1.5.0).** It returns, in the order of `list`, `{ name, description?, settings }`, the same fields that `GET /tools` answers. The route still maps `list` on its own. The `tools/v1` fakes of the tests gained `catalog`.
- **`resources/v1`.** The `bundles/features/resources` package has only contracts, without a manifest, like `terminal`. It exposes only `host()` (minimal API). The Project's resources stay in `runtime.lease/v1.resources`. Stage 10 adds `project` as a minor version when the `resources` bundle takes over. The embedded `transport` now provides `resources/v1@1.0.0` (descriptor `local: false`) over the same reader as the route, so route and contract share the process's CPU sample.
- **R-ALLOW (HR-10).** `agents/doric/src/lib/bridge/allowlist.ts` lists the only methods that the bridge serves to the desktop:
  - the substitutions of the revised table 21.1 of stage 8;
  - `doric.catalog/v1` `get`/`distribution`/`artifact`/`watch`/`disable`/`resetComposition`;
  - `doric.diagnostics/v1.report`.

  Any other method answers `unavailable` (`<contract> <method> is not available to the desktop.`), before any resolution, in both `call` and `subscribe`. Among the refused ones are `secrets/v1.resolve`/`resolveByKind`/`redactions`, `runtime/v1.recover`/`dispose`/`watch`/`vmSsh`, the prompt controls in `runtime.threads/v1`, `storedEventsAfter`, `runtime.lease/v1.exec`/`writeFile`, `terminal/v1.run` and `tools/v1.list`. R-SECRET is contained in that rule.

- **R-ERR.** `runtime/contracts` `rest-errors.ts` carries, per result of the `runtime` contracts, the `{ status, code, message }` that the REST route answers (`restErrors.*`, `notFoundError`, `conflictError`, `restContractError` → `ContractError(code, message, { status })`). `transport/tests/rest-errors.test.ts` calls each route with each result and compares with the table. Outside the table are:
  - the input refusals of the routes (422 `invalid_*`), whose validation belongs to the adapter;
  - the lease reads, which stay data (R-DATA);
  - the messages of `host-config/v1`, `models/v1` and `secrets/v1`, which belong to the settings adapter.
- **Open items.**
  - The documentation (the allowlist and the new contracts) stays with WP6.
  - The messages of invalid input and of configuration stay with the owners of the adapters.

## Stage 9 — layout frame (WP2a)

- **Package.** `bundles/desktop/layout` (project `bundle-layout`, version `1.0.0`): `src/contracts` (`LayoutTabsV1`, `LayoutWorkspaceV1` and the schemas) and the client part. The `contracts.published.json` baseline was generated by the `contracts-baseline` target. `npm install` linked the package in `package-lock.json`, and `nx sync` added `bundles/desktop/layout` to the references of the root `tsconfig.json`. The moved modules swap `@/` for relative paths with `.js`.
- **Runtime types.** `domain/workspace.ts` uses `Project`, `Thread` and `ProjectColor` from `runtime/contracts`, as types only. The fixtures of the ported tests gained `lastSequence` and `configRevision`. `projectColors` is a local copy with `satisfies`, and the `ref`s of `runtime/v1` and `runtime.threads/v1` are built from the ID in `api/host.ts`, because the window's import map does not guarantee the contracts module of a host bundle.
- **Requires.** `hooks/v1`, `ui/v1`, `navigation/v1`, `regions/v1`, `commands/v1`, `runtime/v1@^1.2.0` (projects and the `watchProject` of WP-H) and `runtime.threads/v1`. The provisional requires (`runtime.lease/v1`, `runtime.terminals/v1`, `resources/v1`) come in with WP2b.
- **`workspace-api` adapter (R-ERR).** In today's order, the main's guard runs first (`Enter a valid name before continuing.`, `Enter a name between 1 and 80 characters.`, `The item identifier is invalid.`, `Choose a color from the palette.`), then the REST route's validation (uuid: `The project ID is invalid.` / `The thread ID is invalid.`; non-uuid parent: the 422 message of `POST /projects/:id/threads`) and finally the route's message for each outcome of the host (`The project was not found.`, `The thread was not found.`, `The thread cannot perform this operation.`). Bridge failures (`unavailable`, `disconnected`, `generation_lost`) give `Doric backend is unavailable.`; `invalid_output` and a repeated cursor give `Doric returned an invalid response.`; any other gives `The request could not be completed.`. `threads.get` of a Thread that does not exist gives `undefined`. A delete retries every 500 ms while the host answers `active` (port of `retryWhileActive`).
- **Project watch.** `api/project-watch.ts` ports `project-events.ts` over `runtime/v1.watchProject({ projectId })`: one watch per window, the whole snapshot on each subscription, notices of another Project discarded. An end with `disconnected` or `unavailable` shows nothing and resubscribes on the next `connected` of `doric.connection/v1`, or after 1 s if the connection did not drop (the reconnection delay of Socket.IO). An end with another error shows `The Project event stream failed.` once and does not resubscribe, like the server's `disconnect()` after `workspace:error`. The snapshot's `terminals` are left to the provisional part.
- **Selection in the route (§5.5).** `stores/route-selection.ts` ports the selection store: the same `shouldWriteSelection` rule, but it writes `pathFor(seleção)` with `navigation.navigate`. It is a push when the path is that of the last selection marked by the user (rows, breadcrumb, creation, `beginThread` in another Project) and a replace in the other cases. The startup restore (`startRestore`) reads the route's Thread, or, at `/`, the legacy record. A restored Thread enters the route's selection before the settle, so the path does not flash to `/`.
- **Deviation: restore failure.** The design says to keep the path and the legacy key when the read fails. The current code did not do that: the mount effect already marked `changed`, so the `failed` settle wrote the screen's selection and erased the stored value. For parity, the `failed` settle writes the path of the screen's selection (usually `/`), and the legacy key is erased on any settle.
- **Navigation from outside (rule 5).** `routeIntent` is pure. A described Thread is selected with the semantics of `selectThread`. A Thread that is not described is fetched as in the restore; if it is gone or the read failed, `rewriteRoute` returns the selection's path with replace. `/` with a Thread selected keeps only the Project. The store's own navigations are ignored by the listener.
- **`layout.tabs/v1`.** The owner is `tabOwnerOf(rota, Project selecionado)`. The `element` tab keeps `element: object` in the domain, for the tests without a DOM. `open` of an open id only focuses. `close` removes the tab and calls `ui.dispose`. An unknown id in `close`/`focus` does nothing. Without an owner, `open` gives `ContractError('unavailable', 'No thread or project is selected.')`. The operations are synchronous and answer promises; a refusal is a rejection.
- **`layout.workspace/v1`.** It is local, with `thread(id)` across all described Projects, `selectedThread()` only when the tree describes the selection, and `subscribe` over the `workspaceStore` (any change notifies).
- **Refresh.** `projectActivity` reads a structural terminal (`id`, `state`, `command`, `cwd`). Trigger 1 (the chat's `onSandboxActivityHook`) calls `refreshProject` on the layout's activation. The cases of `changedSandboxProjects` went to the chat.
- **Activation.** `index.ts` wires the adapters, the connection mirror, the Project watch and the two contracts, defines `<layout-workspace>` and the portal hosts, registers `/` and adds the region items (cwd, usage, new terminal). The final wiring is in "Stage 9 — integration and parity".

## Stage 9 — the layout's provisional pieces (WP2b)

- **Folder.** `bundles/desktop/layout/src/client/provisional/{files,git,terminals,usage,resources}/{components,hooks,stores,domain,utility,api}`, plus `api/` (common adapter), `portals.tsx` and `index.ts` (`initProvisional`/`disposeProvisional`, called by the layout's activation). The renderer's files were copied with the logic unchanged; only the imports and `window.doric.` → `api.` changed. The sandbox types (`ProjectChange`, `ProjectTreeNode` etc.) stay in `files/domain/project-files.ts` until stage 10. The dead `FileViewer` (E.13) was not moved; `file-viewer.tsx` has only `FileBody`.
- **Requires.** `runtime.lease/v1@^1.0.0`, `runtime.terminals/v1@^1.1.0` (the stream of WP-H) and `resources/v1@^1.0.0`. The Thread usage reads `runtime.threads/v1.usage`. The lease reads go through `services.use(RuntimeLeaseV1, { projectId })` or `{ threadId }`.
- **Adapter (R-ERR, R-DATA).** In today's order: the main's guard (`The item identifier is invalid.`, `The workspace path is invalid.`, `Invalid branch name.`, `The working directory is invalid.`, `Terminal input is invalid.`, `Terminal dimensions are invalid.`), the route's validation and the result the REST client gave for each status. Lease states are data (`pending` with `retryAfterSeconds: 1`, the route's `Retry-After`), `not_found` is `not_found`, and `invalid_path`/`not_directory`/`not_file` are `invalid_path`. `file` without a path and `fileDiff` with an empty path answer `invalid_path`, like the route's 422. Git: `missing` → `The thread was not found.`, `pending`/`inactive` → `{ repo: false, status }`. Branches without a sandbox give the empty list with `Preparing environment…`/`Environment unavailable.`. On a switch, `refused` shows the host's message and `inactive` gives `The thread cannot perform this operation.`. Usage `undefined` → `The thread was not found.`. The REST client's response validations were ported (`Doric returned an invalid response.`).
- **Terminals.** Every terminal failure shows `Doric could not complete the terminal request.`, like the terminals REST client (including `create` without a sandbox, and `input`/`resize` of a terminal that is gone). Deviation: a lost bridge (`unavailable`, `disconnected`, `generation_lost`) shows `Doric backend is unavailable.`, like the other calls to the host (stage 8 §14.3). Input is serialized per terminal in the adapter (port of `terminal-ipc.ts`).
- **Terminal streams.** `terminals/api/terminal-watch.ts` ports `terminal-events.ts` and the preload's listeners over `runtime.terminals/v1.watchProject`: one listener per Project or terminal (the newest replaces it), the terminal snapshot read only to find the Project, the stream's snapshot rereads and sends the output, a buffer of 2 097 152 characters, reconciliation by offset, and the generation incremented on a drop. A drop (`disconnected`/`unavailable`) is silent and resubscribes on the next `connected`, or after 1 s, as in the Project watch (WP2a). Any other end shows `The terminal connection failed.` (the socket's `connect_error`) and does not resubscribe.
- **Monaco and xterm.** They are loaded with `import()` when the editor or the terminal mounts (`files/components/monaco.ts`, `terminals/components/xterm.ts`), so no module touches the DOM on import. The editor's worker is `src/client/workers/editor.worker.ts` (outside `provisional/`, because the build compiles workers only from that folder), created with `new Worker(new URL('./workers/editor.worker.js', import.meta.url), { type: 'module' })`. Since Monaco arrives after the first render, the editors are created with the text of the current render and reapply language, text and layout when they are ready. The CSS of Monaco and xterm is extracted by esbuild and appended to the layout's CSS.
- **`desktop/ui/cmdk`.** The `BranchPicker` uses cmdk's `Command.Input asChild`. A copy of cmdk inside the bundle would not see the store of the `Command` of `desktop/ui`, so `desktop/ui` gained the `./cmdk` entry (`src/cmdk.ts`, only `export { Command as CommandPrimitive } from 'cmdk'`), served by the import map like the others.
- **Region items.** `layout-cwd` (footer, 10), `layout-thread-usage` (footer, 30) and `layout-new-terminal` (header, 10) are portal hosts (`portalHosts` of `desktop/ui/react-element`) defined and registered on activation. `ProvisionalPortals` (it receives the Thread, `changes`, `onShowChanges` and `onNewTerminal`) renders the content in them and must stay in the workspace view. The "New terminal" button leaves the `WorkspaceHeader` when the header gains the `layout.header` region; until then the two coexist only in the code, because nothing mounts the frame.
- **Tests.** The renderer's 10 suites (`change-tree`, `cwd`, `file-diff`, `files`, `oklch`, `resources`, `sandbox-reads`, `terminals`, `thread-git`, `usage`) came to `bundles/desktop/layout/tests`. New ones: `terminal-watch.test.ts` (the 3 cases of `terminal-events` and the stream's rules) and `provisional-adapter.test.ts` (states, guards and messages per outcome).

## Stage 9 — `settings` bundle (WP3)

- **Package.** `bundles/desktop/settings` (project `bundle-settings`) has only a client part; there is no `src/contracts` and no `contracts.published.json`. `tsconfig.json` is the client's typecheck (`noEmit`); the tests without a DOM use `tsconfig.spec.json`, and the window tests (happy-dom, core and settings built) use `tsconfig.client-spec.json`. The test target uses `--test-force-exit`, because the React Query timers keep the process alive after the last test.
- **Manifest.** `requires` uses `host-config/v1` in place of `core.configuration/v1`, as the cross-review settled. The ref is declared in the adapter by id (`ref('host-config/v1')`), and only the types come from `runtime/contracts`, which is not in the window's import map. `tools/v1.catalog()` is the one of WP-H.
- **Adapter (`api/settings-api.ts`).** It joins the three layers the old call went through, with the same messages: the guards of `validation.ts` (`desktopConfiguration` discards Tools and `execution.maxToolResultChars`, and omits an empty or `null` reference), the validations of the REST routes (`/providers/models` validates the draft against `models/v1.kind` before the catalog; `/credentials` validates fields and UUID) and the response readers of `api.ts`. A bridge failure `unavailable`/`disconnected`/`generation_lost`/`protocol_error` becomes 'Doric backend is unavailable.'; `invalid_output` becomes 'Doric returned an invalid response.'; the rest becomes the message of the final handler, 'The request could not be completed.'. In `replace`, any other exception becomes 'The Doric configuration could not be activated.', like the route.
- **Section routes.** An exact `/settings` is swapped (replace) for the last section of this window, or else `/settings/providers`. The replace runs in a microtask, so that no listener of `navigation/v1` receives `/settings` after the new route. The listener lives in the activation, not in the page.
- **Page.** `<settings-page>` is the only React root. The nav rows are portals into the `settings-link` hosts that `<core-region name="settings.sections">` creates, filtered by the search on label and description. The `SidebarMenu` stays mounted with `hidden` when nothing matches, next to "No sections match", so that the region does not discard the items. The active section is a portal into the host that the page's `<core-outlet>` holds, with the section as `key`, so the local state is lost on the switch, as before. Switching sections, by click or by history, closes the provider page.
- **Flush on close.** `onFlushHook` is registered with the element's services while the page is mounted, and the platform's 1.5 s deadline applies.
- **Execution picker.** `<settings-execution-picker>` has its own root with `StrictMode`, `QueryClientProvider` (one client per bundle and window) and `TooltipProvider`. It has no flush and no toast. The page's toasts use `desktop/ui/sonner`, the same store as the `Toaster` the page mounts.
- **CSS.** `src/client/styles.css` generates the utilities that the settings components use, over the platform's theme, with no rules of its own. Accepted risk: the utilities sit in a second sheet after `theme.css`, and the order between two conflicting classes from different sheets may differ from the renderer's single sheet. The check is left to CDP.
- **Platform (outside WP3, needed).** `packages/bundle/src/build/parts.ts`: a `require()` of an external inside an embedded CommonJS dependency (`use-sync-external-store`, used by `@tanstack/react-table` and requiring `react`) generated esbuild's `__require`, which throws in the window. Now it resolves to an ESM module that re-exports the external, which is still the import map's instance. The new case is in `parts.test.ts`.

## Stage 9 — integration and parity (step 6)

- **Old selection.** `doric.selection` is removed when the restore settles as `none` or `restored`. On a `failed` it stays, as the saved copy stayed before, and the next opening at `/` tries again; it goes away when a later change is written.

- **Composition and image.** `agents/doric/composition.json` enables `layout`, `settings`, `chat` and `history` (§9.1). The `.Dockerfile` and the `doric:build-bundles` target compile and `stage` the four bundles, so that the host distributes the parts to the desktop.
- **Shell.** `app/doric-renderer/src/bootstrap.ts` calls `bootstrapWindow({})`: every window shows `<core-outlet>` (D9-1). The `legacy/` tree is no longer mounted and stays unused until its removal in WP6. The renderer's `styles.css` sheet is still loaded by the shell until the cut (§9.4 is left to WP6).
- **Layout frame (rest of WP2a).** `views/workspace-view.tsx` is the old `views/app.tsx` with the same hooks and props. `<layout-workspace>` is the `/` route, and the conversation is the child route in the inner `<core-outlet>`, mounted only with a Thread selected. The content footer is the `layout.footer` region inside the same `<footer data-slot="workspace-footer">`. The header receives the `layout.header` region in place of the "New terminal" button, which is now the region's own item. The sidebar receives `layout.sidebar` below the tree. The gear executes `core.open-settings/v1` by ID, without importing `core/contracts`. The window's `Toaster` stays in the layout. `project-sidebar`, `thread-branches`, `workspace-tabs` and the sidebar footer came from the renderer, changing only the imports and `window.doric.projects.file` → `api.projects.file`. The `element` tab (`layout.tabs/v1`) shows the title, without an icon. The layout keeps a structural copy of `chat.hooks/v1` with only `onSandboxActivityHook`: hooks match by ID, and importing `chat/contracts` would break the window in a composition without chat (s9-08). The layout's `tsconfig.json` gained `jsx: react-jsx`, like the chat's, because esbuild reads the JSX mode from there.
- **Bundles embedded in the desktop.** `host-config` and `transport` run in the kernel, stay outside the composition and have no directory. In the desktop they are now `embedded` candidates: active in the host, outside `composition.enabled` and without a desktop part. This keeps them from being disabled and from quarantining, by dependency, the parts that require `host-config/v1` or `resources/v1`. `EmbeddedBundle.documents` carries the contract documents that an installed bundle would have in `schemas/`. The host's distribution lists them as files, with a hash, and serves them from memory, so that the desktop routes the calls.
- **Two copies of `bundle/contracts` in the window.** The shell bundles its own copy, and the parts use the import map's. `disposableSchema` now carries `x-doric-local: 'disposable'` in zod's global registry, which all copies share. The check of the basic format (`hooks/v1.define`) and the envelope's encoding recognize the schema by that mark, not only by identity.
- **Empty tuple in ajv.** `noInput` generates `prefixItems: []`, which the 2020-12 meta-schema refuses. The validators of main and of the host remove that field before compiling (`compilableSchema`). The meaning is the same, because `maxItems: 0` still applies. The published documents do not change.
- **React elements without an attribute in the constructor.** `defineReactElement` and `portalHosts` set `display` in `connectedCallback`. A custom element created by `document.createElement` cannot leave the constructor with attributes, and `style` is an attribute.
- **Harness.** `ui/stage9.mjs` runs s9-01 to s9-08. Navigation inside the window uses a click on a same-origin anchor, because main does not load another document in the window. The settings window is closed by main (`BrowserWindow.close()`), like the title bar or Cmd+W. A `window.close()` from the renderer does not go through the close protocol. s9-07 and s9-08 change files inside the container of the stack itself and restore them.

## Stage 9 — cut (WP6)

- **Semantic IPC removed.** The preload exposes only `window.doric.desktop`; the namespaces `projects`, `threads`, `config`, `tools`, `providers`, `credentials`, `resources`, `terminals`, `connection`, `notifications` and `settings` go away. `main.ts` registers only the platform's `doric:desktop` dispatcher and attaches the `/status` monitor to the `ConnectionRelay`; gone are the Thread, Project and terminal event services, the history in `<userData>/thread-history/` (left intact on disk), `doric:settings:open`, `doric:notifications:show` and the `doric:connection:status` push. `app/doric/src/workspace/**`, `src/notifications.ts`, `src/connection/ipc.ts` and `src/app/api/terminals.ts` stay out of `tsconfig.app/spec` until the physical removal. The Electron test `tests-electron/ipc-surface.test.ts` replaces `workspace-boundary`: the real page sees only `desktop`.
- **Legacy flush removed.** `createDesktopApi` loses `onFlush`, and `desktop/client` loses `flushParticipants`; the `flush` phase runs only `WindowHooksV1.onFlushHook`. The Electron case of the flush deadline now blocks the flush through `desktop.onCloseRequested`.
- **Host URL.** `HOST_URL` lives in `app/doric/src/platform/bridge/host-bridge.ts` (§10.9 revised), read by `main.ts` and by the `/status` manager. The stage 9 harness gained `patches/stage9-desktop-host-url.patch` for that file; the baseline's patch is still the old one.
- **Shell.** `app/doric-renderer` compiles only `src/bootstrap.ts` (webpack kept, development server on 4200 kept, decision "Dev"). No `@/` alias, no copy of `assets/` (faces and licenses come from the `theme.css` of `desktop/ui`). `package.json` keeps `desktop/client` and `desktop/ui`; `npm install` removed from the lockfile only `remend` and `use-stick-to-bottom`, unused in any bundle. `test` cleans `dist-tests` first and runs only `tests/stylesheet.test.ts`; the renderer's suites were ported to the bundles in the earlier WPs.
- **CSS (§9.4).** The shell's sheet keeps the window rules (base layer, `.chrome-bar`, the footer's weight invariant). The conversation rules stay only in the chat; the Monaco and xterm sliders go to `bundles/desktop/layout/src/client/styles.css`, without Tailwind directives. Deviation from the design: the shell still emits the whole ordered set of utilities (`source(none)` with `@source` of the shell itself, of `packages/desktop/src` and of `bundles/*/src/client/**/*`). A later sheet that re-emits a subset would change the position of those classes in the cascade relative to the renderer's single sheet, and the layout re-emits none. The sources are now named: before, `@tailwindcss/postcss` scanned the working directory (the repository root under Nx). The resulting set is what the live surfaces use; only classes of legacy copies (the renderer's `ui/`) go away.
- **Files pending removal.** The environment blocks the removal of files; each legacy path is listed, with its reason, in the orchestrator's list of pending removals.

## Stage 9 — design decisions (D9-1 to D9-20)

They complement the "Desktop and UI" section, like S8-1 to S8-23. D9-2, D9-5, D9-6 and D9-7 are minimal platform APIs accepted by cross-review §6.

- **D9-1** The bootstrap creates `<${composition.core}-outlet>`, not a fixed `core-outlet`.
- **D9-2** Platform contract `doric.elements/v1` (client): `define`, `owner`, `attach`, `release`, callable only by the window's active provider of `ui/v1`.
- **D9-3** `navigation/v1.chain()`: the chain of routes from the root to the active one, the same reference until the next navigation.
- **D9-4** `regions/v1.items(region)` and `subscribe(region, listener)`.
- **D9-5** `doric.connection/v1` (client): `status()` and `subscribe(listener)`, fed by the `doric.shell/v1.connection` stream; only Connected/Disconnected.
- **D9-6** `doric.notifications/v1` in the client too (transitional until the `notifications` bundle of stage 10). The window forwards through `doric.shell/v1.notify`, because the dispatcher refuses `doric.*` coming from parts.
- **D9-7** `doric.windows/v1.focus(key)`; `core.open-settings/v1` without `path` focuses the open settings window instead of navigating it.
- **D9-8** `core.commands/v1` (desktopMain, the core's): `execute(id, input?)` delegates to `commands/v1` there; the client's `commands/v1` forwards the misses to it.
- **D9-9** `layout.workspace/v1` (client, local): `thread(threadId)`, `selectedThread()`, `subscribe(listener)`.
- **D9-10** `chat.hooks/v1.onSandboxActivityHook({ projectId })` (observe) triggers the layout's refresh coordinator for files and Git.
- **D9-11** The chat's region item `{ id: 'composer', tag: 'chat-composer', order: 40 }` in `layout.footer`.
- **D9-12** The layout's provisional items are region items too (`layout-cwd` 10 and `layout-thread-usage` 30 in `layout.footer`, `layout-new-terminal` 10 in `layout.header`), drawn by the layout's root with portals.
- **D9-13** The execution picker is the `settings` item `{ id: 'execution', tag: 'settings-execution-picker', order: 20 }` in `layout.footer`.
- **D9-14** Settings sections are child routes `/settings/<id>`; `/settings` is replaced by the last section shown in the window, or else `/settings/providers`; the provider subpage is page state.
- **D9-15** Props of `settings-link`: `{ label, path, icon?, description? }`, with `icon` from a fixed map (`server`, `zap`, `wrench`, `git-branch`, `key-round`).
- **D9-16** The chat's embedded projector claims every event type that is not `<dono>.<nome>/vN`, plus the aliases of the runtime and of Direct of stage 7; the generic block applies only to versioned types that no projector claims.
- **D9-17** Persistent client work (watches, history writes, notifications, autosave) uses the bundle's activation `services`, never the element's.
- **D9-18** Router rules: it matches only the `pathname`, strips a trailing `/` (except `/`), an empty segment or a malformed `%` does not match, static before parameter at the first differing segment, the same shape is `route_conflict`, `navigate()` to the current destination without `replace` does nothing, and `search`/`hash` are kept on parameter updates.
- **D9-19** `history.snapshots/v1` = `read(threadId)` and `write({ threadId, updates })`; the chat feeds it with what it receives, and the history keeps the 250 ms debounce and flushes on `onBeforeQuitHook` and on deactivation.
- **D9-20** `localStorage['doric.selection']` goes away after being converted into a path; the E2E harness reads the selection from the window's path.

## Stage 10 — contracts and order (10A)

- **D10-5 delivered.** `tools/v1@1.2.0` and `skills/v1@1.1.0` gain the optional key `order` (safe positive integer, `orderKeySchema`) in the options of `register` and in `RegisteredTool`/`RegisteredSkill`. `list()` (and `tools/v1.catalog()`) sorts by `order`, with the entries without a key after all the ordered ones, and breaks ties by registration order (which the loader keeps in bundle order). The core goes up to `1.6.0` and `contracts.published.json` was rewritten; `files`, `git` and `threads` now require `tools/v1@^1.2.0`. The keys of table 2.1 are in the `register` calls of `files`, `git` and `threads` and in the frontmatter of every first-party `SKILL.md`.
- **`order` stays out of the skill's record.** Only frontmatter v1 accepts `order` (v0 still refuses unknown keys). The loader passes the value in the options of `skills/v1.register`; `SkillSchema`/`SkillRecord` do not change, so `indexText` and the system prompt stay identical.
- **D10-6 delivered.** `skills/v1.register` resolves `allowed-tools` against the whole `tools/v1.list()` seen by the call; the code and message of `unavailable_tool` do not change. The registration of the skills of installed bundles moved out of `afterActivate` (which now only registers the manifest's v0 tools, `registerInstalledTools`) into the new `afterPhase` option of `activateEnvironment` (`registerInstalledSkills`), which runs after the phase's last bundle, in activation order, in the host, in the kernel and in the generation process (the latter reopens the `activating` mark so that `settle` collects the refusals).
- **Late quarantine.** A refusal in `afterPhase` quarantines an already active bundle with the same code that `afterActivate` would give: it fires `onDeactivateHook` if observed, undoes the activation scope (tools and providers), runs the cleanup and quarantines the active dependents with `dependency_quarantined` and the loop's message. If the failure enters recovery (core or foundation), the bundles activated after it stay `inactive`.
- **Semver comparator.** `memberChange` now compares `items` and `prefixItems` (argument tuples) element by element with the same rule; before, any change inside an input tuple counted as major.

## Stage 10 — shared client (10M)

- **Fourth new package (G-10).** `packages/desktop` (npm `desktop/reads`) exists besides `storage`, `desktop/client` and `desktop/ui`: it is a source-code library without window state, which each client part (and `desktop/ui`) embeds. It exports `sandbox-reads`, `files` (without the dead `collapsedPath`), `cwd`, `messageFrom`, `project-refresh` (types from `@tanstack/query-core`, a type-only peer), the `project-files` vocabulary, and `answerOf`/`LeaseOutcome`/`LeaseAnswer` (pending with `retryAfterSeconds: 1`). The language's name is still `fileLanguage` (the design said `languageOf`).
- **`change-tree` and `file-diff` in `desktop/reads`.** `FileTree` (in `desktop/ui/workspace`) decorates rows with `changeClassName`/`changeLetter`, and `DiffEditor` (in `desktop/ui/code`) counts lines with `changedLineCounts`; the two pure modules, used by files and git, go to `desktop/reads` with their tests.
- **`desktop/ui/workspace` and `desktop/ui/code`.** Explicit subpaths in `exports`, a copy of the stage 9 versions (layout) with relative imports internal to the package. `useReadError`/`ReadFeedback` gain an optional `toastId` (default `useId()`). `PanelState` leaves `project-files-sidebar.tsx`. Monaco is still loaded by `import()` in the first editor.
- **Platform build touched (R-8.1 was not delivered).** `app/doric-platform/build.mjs` now: gives the `file` loader to `.ttf` in the desktop/ui group; discards the per-chunk CSS of the split build and collects, in one pass without splitting over all the entries, the imported CSS (Monaco's) once, in import order, appended to the end of `theme.css` (G-11), with the codicon font in `assets/`; and builds the workers of `desktopUi.workers` (`workers/editor.worker.js`, source `src/code/editor.worker.ts`). The appended CSS is, block by block, equal to what the layout embeds today (56 sheets, same order). Until 10P switches the layout to `desktop/ui/code`, the window loads that CSS twice (identical rules, no visual effect).

## Stage 10 — host contracts (10R)

- **Runtime `1.3.0`.** `runtime.lease/v1@1.1.0` gains `withLease(task)` (R-5.1), `runtime.workspace/v1@1.1.0` gains `withThread(threadId, options, task)` (R-5.2), `runtime.threads/v1@1.2.0` gains `enqueueTerminalResult`/`failTerminalResult` (R-5.3), `runtime.hooks/v1@1.1.0` gains `onThreadsClosingHook` (R-5.4) and the new contract `runtime.usage/v1@1.0.0` has `read(threadId)` (R-5.5). `withLease` and `withThread` are local only: the task is `callback` and the answer is `object` (`LeaseOutcome`, `ThreadTaskOutcome`; the name `ThreadOutcome` already existed in `model.ts`).
- **`withLease` and `withThread` reuse the domain.** `withLease` is the `withLease` of `service.ts` exposed, with the scope rule of the lease reads (`invalid_input` without a Project); `withThread` answers `missing`/`inactive`/`pending` like `git`/`branches`, runs under the lock when `exclusive`, uses `projectFor` with `resume` and calls the runner's `hint` after the task with `refreshHint`. `busyCwds` is the list of cwds of the Threads with an active or queued prompt, as in `branches`.
- **Delivery of the terminal result.** The runner now uses the same `terminalResult`/`failTerminalResult` functions that the contract exposes: the text, the redaction, the `terminal` source, the log `Terminal result delivery failed {threadId}` and the closing with `terminal_result_failed` do not change. For a Thread that this process does not have alive, `enqueueTerminalResult` does nothing and `failTerminalResult` only writes the log.
- **`onThreadsClosingHook`.** Executed and awaited together with the current `terminals.close` at the three points (`close`, `suspend`, `drainProject`), before awaiting the task; a handler failure is swallowed. It does not fire for an empty list of Threads (closing a Project without Threads makes no noise). Without a handler, nothing changes.
- **`usage-context.ts` does not leave the runtime yet.** `runtime.threads/v1.usage` still fills in the capacity as before; the same algorithm was copied to `bundles/foundation/core/src/host/models/capacity.ts` (`models/v1.contextWindow`). Removing the runtime's copy is left to whoever switches the consumer (10L).
- **Core `1.7.0`: `models/v1@1.1.0.contextWindow` (R-6.3).** Candidates: the providers of the active configuration (the one of the last `prepare` with a revision) whose kind answers `catalogCapacity()` true and that are the `providerId` or, without it, list the model. With exactly one candidate, it uses the provider cached by `resolve` and reads `models()`; it answers a finite capacity above 0 or `undefined`, and never throws. For that, `prepare` keeps the names of `providers[].models`.
- **`catalogCapacity` in `models.provider/v1@1.1.0`.** Basic contracts require an exact implementation, so the method is mandatory for whoever provides the contract. The `models-adapter` (now `0.1.0`) answers `kind.id === 'openrouter'`, the fixed rule of `usage-context.ts`; the provider bundles of stage 10 must implement it. The core treats a failure of the method as `false`.
- **Core `secrets/v1@1.1.0.watchRedactions(listener)` (R-2.1 revised).** A stream (`streamListener`) that delivers the complete list of `redactions()` on subscribing (after the load) and after each effective `create`, `update`, `delete` and `view().addRedaction`. `create`/`update`/`delete` resolve only after every listener has received it (it awaits a remote listener's promise); a listener that fails does not fail the write. The tokens that the runtime registers in its own redactor (the Git token applied to the sandbox) are not in `secrets/v1`: whoever migrates the terminal (10D) must cover that case.

## Stage 10 — additions to the stage 9 surfaces (10P)

- **Versions.** `layout` `1.1.0`: `layout.workspace/v1@1.1.0` (`selection`, `threads`, `connected`, `setBottomVisible`; `subscribe` also notifies connection changes), `layout.tabs/v1@1.1.0` (optional `icon`, `ariaLabel`, `tooltip` in `open`, `close({ id, threadId? })`, `current`, `subscribe`), and the new contracts `layout.panel/v1@1.0.0` and `layout.sidebar/v1@1.0.0`. `chat` `1.1.0`: `chat.hooks/v1@1.1.0.onThreadUpdateHook`. `settings` `1.1.0`: `settings.config/v1@1.0.0`. The new fields of `open` are optional (minor version); without `icon` the tab still has only the title.
- **The new members are local and synchronous.** The read methods and `setBottomVisible`, `show`, `toggle` and `setDraft` return a value directly, like those of `layout.workspace/v1@1.0.0`; the ones that receive a function (`subscribe`, `addThreadRows`, `setDraft`) are `x-doric-local`.
- **Project panel.** The frame (the "Project views" strip, `layout.panel.header` between the tabs and the panel button, content, the `project-files-footer` footer with `layout.panel.footer` after the provisional monitor) moved from `provisional/files` to `components/organisms/project-panel.tsx`. The provisional Files and Changes are embedded entries of the same strip until 10N, and the open state/active view moved from `useState` to the panel store that `layout.panel/v1` reads. The draggable order stays in local state; new views enter at the end, in the region's order. A region item mounts only with the panel open and selected (it remounts on a view switch); `badgeTag` is created by `ui/v1` inside the trigger.
- **`layout.main.bottom` mounts only when visible.** The region's items mount only while some item has asked `setBottomVisible(id, true)`, so whoever decides the visibility is the owner's client state (in `activate`), not the element mounted in the region (the design said the element; an element that is not mounted cannot ask to appear). The split is the same as in stage 9 (conversation min 20%, panel 35%/min 6rem) and holds the provisional manual terminal and the region.
- **Rows under the Thread.** `addThreadRows` with a repeated id fails with `registration_conflict`; a `count` that throws counts as 0. The registered rows mount as `<tag thread-id>` after the provisional terminal rows and before the child Threads.
- **A terminal tab does not keep the refresh.** The refresh's criterion "some tab reads the sandbox" now also excludes the element tab with `icon: 'terminal'`, as it already excluded the embedded terminal tab.
- **Layout on `desktop/ui/workspace` and `desktop/ui/code`.** Every import of `tab-strip`, `tree-guides`, `toolbar-divider`, `file-tree`, `tree-skeleton`, `change-counts`, `read-feedback`, `code-view` and `diff-editor` points to the subpaths; Monaco's CSS leaves the layout's `styles.css` (it stays only in `theme.css`) and the layout's worker is no longer loaded. `utility/move-item.ts` stays in the layout because `domain/workspace-tabs.ts` runs in the Node tests, which do not load `desktop/ui/workspace`. The dead copies were removed.
- **`onThreadUpdateHook`.** The conversation store calls the observer on receiving each live `event` of an open Thread (name and Project of the open record), before the 100 ms flush, and each `updated` (name and Project of the record itself); never for `snapshot` or history. Chat executes without awaiting and swallows the failure.
- **Delegated block (R-9.7) on the existing node.** Instead of the projector emitting an external block, `DelegatedTurnNode` stays the owner of the DOM (`mt-6 font-conversation`, `data-prompt-id`, `data-result-prompt-id`, `getTextContent()`) and, when there is a renderer for `chat.delegated-input/v1`, hosts the registered element with `block = { type, id: turnKey, data: { promptId, input } }` and `focused`, passing on the key's `toggle()`. Without a renderer, chat draws the turn as today, so nothing changes until 10H registers its own. `ChatBlockElement`, `DelegatedInput` and `DELEGATED_INPUT_BLOCK` leave `chat/contracts`.
- **`<chat-readonly-markdown text>` (R-9.9).** The conversation's theme was extracted to `conversation/theme.ts`; the element uses `ReadonlyMarkdownView` with that theme, which is the same one that `ReadonlyMarkdown` reads from the surrounding editor.
- **`settings.config/v1` without a `settings/contracts` subpath.** The ref and the types live in `src/client/services/settings-config.ts`; the consumer (10G) keeps a structural copy, as `chat` does with `layout.workspace/v1`. The view publishes the draft of `use-config.ts` on a bridge; `setDraft(update)` writes to the bridge at once (two writes in a row compose) and calls the same setter, so the debounce, the flush on close and the save messages are today's. Before the load, `draft()` is `undefined` and `setDraft` is ignored.
- **Section header from the active link (R-9.8).** The `<h1>`, the icon and the breadcrumb use the `settings.sections` link whose `path` is the current route; without a link (before mounting), the embedded section as before. For the five embedded sections the text and the icon are the same.

## Stage 10 — `web` bundle (10F)

- **Copy, not move.** `bundles/features/web` receives verbatim copies of `files/src/host/tools/web.ts`, `files/tests/web.test.ts` (without the sandbox and the `fakeHost`, which the tool never uses) and `files/src/skills/12-web-research/SKILL.md` (now `web-research/`, `order: 1200`). The bundle does not enter the composition yet: `files` keeps registering `web` (700) and the skill until 10L, which removes the originals and enables `web`; enabling both at the same time would violate the global uniqueness of names.
- **Minimal dependencies.** The host of `web` requires only `tools/v1@^1.2.0` and depends on `bundle`, `tool` and `zod`; there is no client part and no contracts (`export {};`).

## Stage 10 — provider bundles (10B)

| Topic                     | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Provider shape            | `openai`, `openrouter` and `codex` implement the `models.provider/v1@1.1.0` of stage 6 (`kind`, `advertised`, `catalog`, `create`, `catalogCapacity`), not the draft's `traits`/`listModels` shape. The secret arrives through `catalog({ secret })` and `create({ secret })`, resolved by the core; the bundles require only `log/v1`, not `secrets/v1`. |
| Catalog failure           | The `openrouter` bundle answers `failed` without logging anything; the one that logs is `models/v1` (current behavior).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Descriptors               | Each bundle declares its own `ProviderKind` copied from `llms` `kinds.ts`, with the `ModelKindDescriptor`/`ModelField` types of `bundle/contracts`. The `tests/fixtures/provider-kind.json` fixture of each bundle was generated from `llms` before the removal and is the proof of equality.                                                             |
| No `contracts` of its own | The bundles have no `./contracts` subpath, like `models-adapter`: they publish no contract of their own.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Order of the kinds        | `models/v1.kinds()` follows the registration order; the composition (10L) must activate `openai`, `openrouter`, `codex` in that order to keep `GET /providers/kinds`.                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Removal                   | `bundles/models-adapter` and the closed catalog of `llms` stay until the host switch (10L).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

## Stage 10 — `notifications` bundle (10K)

- **No platform file.** Stage 8 already delivers `app/doric/src/platform/native/notifications.ts` (a cut at 200 code points, an empty title or `!isSupported()` silent, `failed` logged); 10K only creates `bundles/desktop/notifications`.
- **Outside the composition until 10N.** The bundle is not enabled yet: the chat's `announce` still shows the notice, and enabling both would show each notice twice. 10N enables `notifications` and removes `announce` and `doric.shell/v1.notify` in the same commit.
- **Thread shown.** The client reads `navigation/v1.current()?.params.threadId` (as in the design), without requiring the pattern of the chat's route; only the conversation route has `threadId`, so the result is the same.
- **Structural copy of the hook.** `chat.hooks/v1.onThreadUpdateHook` is copied into `src/client/observer.ts` (as the layout does with `onSandboxActivityHook`), so as not to import `chat/contracts`.
- **`navigation/v1` in desktopMain.** The requirement is satisfied by the core's client `navigation/v1` (`providedElsewhere`); the desktopMain test declares that client contract in the test core.

## Stage 10 — `docker` and `firecracker` bundles (10C)

- **Renaming.** `packages/docker` and `packages/firecracker` became `packages/docker-sandbox` and `packages/firecracker-sandbox` (`git mv`; npm name and Nx project `docker-sandbox`/`firecracker-sandbox`). READMEs, `tsconfig` references, `agents/doric/package.json`, `bundles/sandbox-adapter` and `package-lock.json` (`npm install`) were updated. `agents/doric/src/index.ts` no longer imported the packages, so only the manifest and the `tsconfig` files of `agents/doric` changed (references).
- **VM registry in `sandbox`.** `createVmRegistry` and `tracked` live in `packages/sandbox/src/lib/vms.ts` (exported by the `sandbox` root), in the `sandbox-adapter` version that preserves `start` and `stats`; the `RunningVm`/`VmProvider` types are local and structurally equal to `RunningMachine`, so that `sandbox` does not depend on `runtime`. The design said to move it from `agents/doric/src/lib/vms.ts`, which stage 5 had already taken to `bundles/sandbox-adapter/src/host/registry.ts`.
- **Contract.** The bundles implement the `sandbox/v1` of stage 5 (`runtime/contracts`), not the draft's `SandboxService`; `describe()` answers `{ provider: 'docker', durableWorkspace: true }` and `{ provider: 'firecracker', durableWorkspace: false }`. Each bundle reads only `DORIC_SANDBOX_IMAGE` and `DORIC_SANDBOX_SSH` in `activate`; `DORIC_SANDBOX_PROVIDER` is not read by them (the one that chooses the bundle is the composition, 10L). Memory 2048 MiB in Docker and 512 MiB in Firecracker; pool, limits, network and log identical to `sandbox-adapter`.
- **`requires: ["log/v1@^1.0.0"]`.** The design said `requires: []`, but the records of `sandpool` (component `sandpool`, level `debug`) go to `log/v1` as today, so the requirement stays.
- **Duplicated logger.** Each bundle has its own copy of `logger.ts` (pino → `log/v1`), equal to the adapter's; there is no shared package with a dependency on `bundle` for it.
- **Not enabled yet.** The default composition, the `build-bundles`/`stage` of `agents/doric` and the `DORIC_SANDBOX_PROVIDER=firecracker` mapping without `DORIC_COMPOSITION` belong to 10L; until then `sandbox-adapter` keeps providing `sandbox/v1` (now importing `docker-sandbox`/`firecracker-sandbox`) and was removed after the switch (10L).

## Stage 10 — `files` bundle (10E)

- **Regions only in 10N.** The client defines `<files-tree>`, `<files-toggle>` and `<files-file-view>`, provides `files.sandbox/v1` and `files.refresh/v1` and registers `files.open-file/v1`, but the three region items (`layout.panel` `files`, `layout.header` and `layout.panel.header` `files-toggle`) stay in `filesRegions`/`registerFilesRegions`, not called in `activate`. While the layout draws the provisional Files view and button, registering them would show two Files views and two buttons; 10N calls `registerFilesRegions(services.use(RegionsV1))` in the same commit that removes the provisional ones.
- **Tree scope by `cwd`.** `files.sandbox/v1.observeTree` receives `{ projectId, cwd? }` (the Thread's raw `cwd`), not `{ projectId, root }`: the query key is `['files', p, 'tree', cwd]` and the root is derived (`workspacePath(cwd)`). The `git.changes/v1` that the tree reads (a structural copy in `client/api/peers.ts`) uses the same scope; 10G must follow that format.
- **Host result.** `files/v1` answers `LeaseOutcome<DirectoryRead | TreeRead | ContentRead>` with `withLease` (it never acquires). The client reproduces the REST mapping of the provisional adapter: `pending` with `retryAfterSeconds: 1`, `not_found` as 404, `not_directory`/`not_file`/`invalid_path` as 422 (`invalid_path`), the identifier and path guards and the failure messages (`Doric backend is unavailable.`, `The request could not be completed.`, `Doric returned an invalid response.`).
- **Coordinator.** The triggers of `useProjectRefresh` become a coordinator without React: a subscription to `layout.workspace/v1` (Project and state, Thread and `cwd`, the Threads' activity, whether something of the Project is in view, connection), focus/visibility and the 15 s interval only while a registrant of the selected Project is active and connected; the chat's activity hook asks for a refresh without depending on the connection, as today. Terminal activity arrives through `request` (10D). "In view" is the presence of an active registrant: the tree and each file tab register themselves while mounted; the selected Thread's footer starts to count when git/usage register.
- **File tab.** `<files-file-view>` wraps the content in `flex h-full min-h-0 flex-col`, because the layout's element-tab host is a `div` without flex; the text, the roles and the footer (`Updating…`) are those of `TabFile`.
- **`domain/*.ts` not created.** `fileLanguage` and `emptyDirectoryNotice` come straight from `desktop/reads`; a local re-export adds nothing.
- **Client dependencies.** React, TanStack Query (`@tanstack/query-core` for the observer without a DOM), zustand, lucide, `layout`, `desktop/ui` and `desktop/reads` are `devDependencies`; the manifest goes to 1.1.0 and requires `runtime.lease/v1@^1.1.0`, `hooks/v1`, `ui/v1`, `regions/v1`, `commands/v1` and `layout.{workspace,panel,tabs}/v1` (1.1.0).

## Stage 10 — `usage` bundle (10I)

- **The contract's types come from `runtime`.** `usage/contracts` re-exports `ThreadUsage`, `UsageTotals` and `ContextUsage` from `runtime/contracts` and uses `threadUsageSchema` as the output of `usage/v1.thread`; the shapes are the same as the projection's.
- **Filling in the capacity.** The host repeats the rule of `usage-context.ts` over `models/v1.contextWindow`: it asks only when there is `context` without `contextWindow`, sends `providerId` only when the measurement has it, accepts only a finite value above 0 and treats a failure of the call as "no answer". The choice of the candidate belongs to the core (10R).
