- **Footer item only in 10N.** `usage-summary` is defined on activation, but the `layout.footer` item (`id: 'usage'`, order 30) goes out in `registerUsageRegion`, which 10N calls when it removes the provisional `layout-thread-usage`; together, the footer would show the usage twice (the same choice as `files`).
- **Selected Thread.** The element reads `layout.workspace/v1.selectedThread()` with `subscribe`, through a structural copy of the ref (like `chat`), and remounts per Thread as the provisional footer did with `key`.
- **Client read.** The same identifier guard, the same messages (`The thread was not found.` for `undefined`, `Doric backend is unavailable.` for a lost bridge) and a 10 s deadline per call [D10-2]. Its own query client uses the layout's defaults (no retry, focus or reconnection by default); the usage query sets its own (3 s, focus, `staleTime` 1 s, `retry` 2).

## Stage 10 — `threads` client (10H)

- **`index.ts` entry.** The stage 8 builder only recognizes `src/client/index.ts`, so the entry is `index.ts` (the design said `index.tsx`); the components live in `delegated-turn.tsx` and `collapsible-block.tsx`.
- **Structural copies.** `chat.views/v1` (only `registerRenderer`), `DELEGATED_INPUT_BLOCK`, `DelegatedInputBlock` and `ChatBlockElement` live in `src/client/chat-contract.ts`, and the part read from `layout.workspace/v1` (`thread`, `subscribe`) in `src/client/source.ts`, so as not to import `chat/contracts` or `layout/contracts` (the same choice as the layout and `notifications`). Both are used only through `whenAvailable`; the manifest now requires only `ui/v1` and `navigation/v1` in the client.
- **Collapsible block copied.** The chat's `CollapsibleBlock` is a molecule of the chat's client, which another bundle cannot import; `threads` has its own copy with only the shape it uses (header with an action between two triggers, settled block), with the same classes and ARIA labels. _Note (2026-11-18, step 5(a) of bundle isolation):_ `CollapsibleBlock` left the chat and is the platform's `desktop/ui/collapsible-block` 1.10.0; the copy in `threads` is replaced in step 5(b).
- **Open state in the element.** `<threads-delegated-turn>` keeps the reader's choice (`chosen`): the instruction starts open, the result closed; `toggle()` inverts what is in view, like `blockToggled(chosen, settledOpenness)`. The class is created by `defineDelegatedTurnElement()` inside `activate` [D10-7].
- **The chat's Markdown.** The text is a `<chat-readonly-markdown>` created by `ui/v1.create` inside a `div` with `display: contents` (React cannot pass `services` to an element of another bundle); the removal (`ui/v1.dispose`) is deferred to a microtask, so as not to unmount the element's root during the parent's commit.
- **`domain/delegated.ts` copied, not moved.** The chat's projector still uses `delegatedInput`, so the chat's file and `tests/delegated.test.ts` stay; `threads` has identical copies (the tests cover the same assertions).
- **Outside the composition until 10L/10N.** The bundle already has a client part, but it enters the desktop's composition only with 10L/10N; until then the chat draws the delegated input with its `DelegatedTurnNode`, with no visible change.

## Stage 10 — `resources` bundle (10J)

- **Contract `resources/v1@1.0.0` with `project()`.** `project(call)` answers `LeaseOutcome<ContainerResources>` (type and `containerResourcesSchema` from `runtime/contracts`); the host does `withLease(readSandboxResources)` in the `projectId` scope, never acquires, and a missing or failing `stats()` becomes an `unavailable` container. `machine.ts` and `container.ts` are literal copies from the `transport` and from `runtime/provisional`; the CPU sample is module state in the generation's process.
- **Minimal adjustment in the `transport`.** With `project` in the contract, the provider of stage 9 stopped compiling; `resources-service.ts` now does `defineService(ResourcesV1.id, …)` and the kernel's document lists only `host`, without changing what the transport serves. 10L removes that provider when it wires the bundle.
- **`layout.workspace/v1@^1.1.0`.** The monitor reads `selection().project?.id` (1.1.0) with `subscribe`, the same `selectedProject?.id` as the provisional footer; the design said `^1.0.0`. The ref is a local structural copy (`api/peers.ts`): `layout` depends on `resources/contracts`, and importing `layout/contracts` would create a cycle in the Nx graph.
- **Region item only in 10N.** `<resources-monitor>` is defined on activation; the `layout.panel.footer` item (`id: 'resources'`, order 100) goes out in `registerResourcesRegion`, which 10N calls when it removes the provisional `ResourceMonitor` (together, the footer would show the monitor twice).
- **Client.** Identifier guard, messages and mapping (`pending` with `retryAfterSeconds: 1`, container validation) equal to the layout's provisional adapter; query keys `['resources', 'host']` and `['resources', projectId]`; its own query client with the layout's defaults, and `TooltipProvider` in the element, since the element's React root does not inherit the layout's.
- **Bridge.** The bridge's allowlist (`agents/doric/src/lib/bridge/allowlist.ts`) still only allows `resources/v1.host`; 10L/10N must add `project`.

## Stage 10 — `terminal` bundle (10D)

- **Two refs for `terminal/v1`.** `TerminalV1` (`ref<Terminal>`, only `run`) and `terminalMethods` (only `run`) stay as in 1.0.0, because the provisional provider of the `runtime`, `files` and the adapter capture still implement or enumerate them; the complete 1.1.0 is `TerminalService` / `TerminalServiceV1` / `terminalServiceMethods` with the same ID. 10L joins the two when the `runtime`'s provider goes away.
- **`watchProject` is a stream with a snapshot.** Instead of the design's `listener(notice)` without a snapshot, `terminal/v1.watchProject(projectId, StreamListener<TerminalWatchItem>)` has the shape of `runtime.terminals/v1.watchProject` (a snapshot on each subscription, then `updated`/`removed`, `output` only after the snapshot and never buffered). The client's output watch depends on the snapshot on each (re)subscription to reread the terminal; the port stays 1:1.
- **`create(threadId, size)`, `snapshot(id, after)`, `stop` → `boolean`.** Positional arguments (tuples) as in the design; `stop` answers whether there was a session, like the registry, and the client ignores it (the route always answered 204).
- **Local refs of the peers.** `runtime.workspace/v1` (`cwd`, `withThread`), `runtime.threads/v1` (background results), `runtime.hooks/v1` (`onThreadsClosingHook`), `layout.{workspace,tabs,sidebar}/v1` and `files.refresh/v1` are structural copies (`src/host/peers.ts`, `src/client/peers.ts`): the `runtime` imports `terminal/contracts`, and `layout`/`files` depend on the `runtime`, so importing their contract modules would close a cycle in the Nx graph (the same choice as `layout`, `notifications` and `resources`).
- **Redaction.** The registry redacts each chunk with the last `secrets/v1.watchRedactions` and rereads `secrets/v1.redactions()` before each start (the runner's `redactor.current()`). The secrets that the `runtime` registers only in its own `redactor` (the Git token applied to the sandbox) do not reach the bundle; the `runtime` must also call `secrets/v1.view().addRedaction` before 10L wires the bundle.
- **`run`.** Without `threadId`/`promptId` in the scope, or with `withThread` other than `ready`, or with the Thread closing, it throws `Thread is inactive.`. The background result goes to `enqueueTerminalResult` without `call` (the originating prompt has already ended); a rejection of the result or of the delivery calls `failTerminalResult`.
- **Cleanup.** The `onThreadsClosingHook` handler logs `Terminal cleanup failed` with `{ projectId }` (the runner used `{ threadId }` on the suspension of a Thread and `{ projectId }` on closing) and never rejects. It requires `log/v1@^1.0.0`, `secrets/v1@^1.1.0`, `runtime.threads/v1@^1.2.0` and `runtime.hooks/v1@^1.1.0` (the versions that bring what is called).
- **Tool.** `src/host/tool.ts` is the literal copy of `files/src/host/tools/terminal.ts` without the simple-command fallback [D10-12]; it always calls the bundle's own `terminal/v1` in the prompt's scope. 10L removes the one in `files`.
- **Client.** Entry `index.ts` (the builder only recognizes that name). The button's order in `layout.header` is 10, the provisional one's (the design said 100; `files-toggle` is 200). The manual state (`manualId`/`manualIds` per Thread) moves to the bundle's store; the bottom section is shown by the activation (`setBottomVisible('terminal-manual', …)`), not by the element, because the items of `layout.main.bottom` only mount with the section visible. The rows follow the Project of each Thread on the first call of `count` (the renderer followed all loaded Projects) and compute the depth from the `parentThreadId`s. The click navigates to `/projects/<p>/threads/<t>` before showing the manual terminal or opening the `terminal:<id>` tab. A change of the activity (id, state, command, cwd) of a Project's list calls `files.refresh/v1.request(projectId)`.
- **Regions only in 10N.** The four elements are defined on activation; `registerTerminalSurfaces` (the `terminal-new` and `terminal-manual` items and the `terminals` rows) is called by 10N when it removes the layout's provisional terminals; together, two buttons and two lists of rows would appear.
- **Bridge.** The allowlist (`agents/doric/src/lib/bridge/allowlist.ts`) still lists `runtime.terminals/v1`; 10L/10N must add `terminal/v1` (`list`, `create`, `snapshot`, `input`, `resize`, `stop`, `watchProject`, never `run`).

## Stage 10 — `git` bundle (10G)

- **Own schemas in `git/contracts`.** Since step 7(c) of bundle isolation, the types are declared in `git/contracts`, and only `LeaseOutcome` comes from `runtime/contracts` (only `import type`); the zod schemas are written in the module: the client part loads `git/contracts`, and the window's import map does not guarantee the contracts module of a host bundle (the same choice as `files`).
- **`git.changes/v1` with `{ projectId, cwd? }`.** The scope is the raw working directory, not `root`: it is the shape the consumer (`files`, decorated tree) already uses and the same as `files.sandbox/v1`'s; the key `['files', p, 'changes', cwd]` does not change.
- **Busy lookup through `runtime.terminals/v1`.** `terminal/v1@1.0.0` does not have `list` yet; the host uses `runtime.terminals/v1.list(projectId)` through `whenAvailable` (the same registry that the runtime consulted), with today's filter (`state !== 'exited'` and `origin === 'agent' || command !== 'bash'`). When the `terminal` that owns the registry comes in (10L), the source switches to `terminal/v1.list` without changing the service (function `TerminalList`). _Note (2026-11-19, step 7(c) of bundle isolation):_ `runtime.terminals/v1` is no longer provided; `git` 1.2.14 reads the list from `terminal/v1` ([docs/designs/bundle-isolation-concept.md](../../designs/bundle-isolation-concept.md)).
- **Invalid `fileDiff` before the lease.** A repository or an absolute path, one with `..`, with NUL or an empty path answer `{ status: 'ready', value: { status: 'invalid_path' } }` without calling `withLease`, as the route answered 422 in any state of the lease.
- **Thread reads through `withThread`.** `status` uses `{ exclusive: false, refreshHint: true }`; `branches` and `switchBranch` use `{ exclusive: true }`. `withThread` also answers `inactive` for a terminal record, which the runtime's `git`/`branches` did not check explicitly (a terminal Thread has no live runtime, so the answer is the same).
- **Branch switch waits for the refresh.** Like the `refreshProject` that `onSuccess` awaited, success asks for `files.refresh/v1.request(projectId)` (the window's other readers) and awaits the refresh of its own client; without `files.refresh/v1`, the retry and the refresh fall to the `git` client.
- **Versioning.** The element reads `settings.config/v1` (`draft`, `setDraft(update)`, `subscribe`) through a local structural ref, because `settings` publishes no contracts module; the list of credentials is `secrets/v1.list` in the `['credentials']` query of the `git` client (one more read than the settings cache, invisible). A failure of the list leaves the section without options, as before.
- **Elements with their own root.** Each element wraps `StrictMode`, the bundle's `QueryClientProvider` and a `TooltipProvider` (the element's React root does not inherit the layout's); the comparison tab has the root `flex h-full min-h-0 flex-col`, like the file tab of `files`.
- **Surfaces only in 10N.** The five elements, the `git.changes/v1` service and the command are registered on activation; the items `layout.panel` (`changes`, 200), `layout.footer` (`git-cwd`, 10), `settings.sections` (`versioning`, 40) and the `/settings/git` route go out in `registerGitSurfaces`, which 10N calls when it removes the layout's provisional Changes and `layout-cwd` and the Versioning section of `settings` (together, two of each would appear).
- **Bridge.** The allowlist (`agents/doric/src/lib/bridge/allowlist.ts`) does not list `git.commands/v1` yet; 10L/10N must add `status`, `branches`, `switchBranch`, `changes` and `fileDiff`. The transport routes stay on the reads of `runtime.lease/v1` until 10L.

## Stage 10 — host switch (10L)

- **Composition.** `agents/doric/composition.json` enables `openai`, `openrouter`, `codex`, `docker`, `terminal`, `usage` and `resources` in place of `models-adapter` and `sandbox-adapter`. `build-bundles`/`stage` and the `.Dockerfile` also build and prepare `firecracker` (prepared, not enabled), `web` and `notifications`. `notifications` stays outside the composition until 10N (decision of 10K: with the chat's `announce` still active, each notice would appear twice). `core/tests/models.test.ts` now uses the three provider bundles.
- **`DORIC_SANDBOX_PROVIDER` [D10-9].** Without `DORIC_COMPOSITION`, `firecracker` makes the kernel boot the shipped composition with `firecracker` in place of `docker` (an object passed to `startKernel`; the overrides stay next to the file). With `DORIC_COMPOSITION`, the operator's composition decides, and the `sandboxProvider` line of the logs reflects the enabled bundle. An unreadable composition falls back to the previous path and the kernel refuses it in `bundle_load`.
- **Resource routes through the platform's principal.** The routes for files, Git, usage, resources and terminals call `files/v1`, `git.commands/v1`, `usage/v1`, `resources/v1` and `terminal/v1` through the platform's services (`createHostTransport` → `features` option), like the desktop's bridge, and not through the `requires` of the `transport`. Reason: the `transport` is structural, and with `requires` a resource bundle disabled by the "Disable a bundle" recovery action or quarantined would bring the host down (`kernel_restart_required`); a `whenAvailable` stub does not bind a scope, and the Project reads need `call.scope.projectId`. The `transport` no longer provides any contract (`resources/v1` belongs to the `resources` bundle) and does not declare the resource contracts.
- **`/projects` socket.** The terminal notices come from `terminal/v1.watchProject`: one stream per subscribed Project, opened before the snapshot read and closed with the last socket; a new provider (generation switch) is subscribed again for each Project. The stream's own snapshot is ignored (the socket reads its own with `terminal/v1.list`); `terminal:removed` keeps the shape `{ projectId, threadId, terminalId }`.
- **Terminals.** The `runtime` stops providing `terminal/v1` (bundle version 2.0.0, `contracts.published.json` rewritten); the `terminal` tool is the one of the `terminal` bundle (order 500) and leaves `files`. `runtime.terminals/v1` and the `terminals` snapshot of the runtime's watches delegate to `terminal/v1` while there is a provider (G-9), so the windows that still read the provisional contract see the same sessions as the routes; without a provider (the runtime's own tests) the provisional registry answers. The two refs (`TerminalV1`/`TerminalServiceV1`) stay until the provisional registry is deleted. The deprecated read `runtime.lease/v1.branches` still consults the provisional registry (empty in production) for busy terminals; `git.commands/v1` uses `runtime.terminals/v1.list`, which delegates.
- **Deprecated reads kept (R-5.1 revised).** `runtime.lease/v1` (`files`, `tree`, `file`, `diff`, `changes`, `fileDiff`, `resources`, `git`, `branches`) and `runtime.threads/v1.usage` (with the runtime's capacity filling) stay for the layout's provisional elements until 10N; the routes no longer call them.
- **`web` enabled without exclusion.** The composition enables `web` right after `files`; the `web` tool (700) and the `web-research` skill (1200) now come from the `web` bundle, with the same request body to the model (global order D10-5). The originals in `files` (`tools/web.ts`, `tests/web.test.ts` and `skills/12-web-research`) were removed, and `host/index.ts` no longer registers `web`; no duplicated skill quarantines `web`.
- **Kinds catalog of `llms` kept.** `packages/llms/src/lib/providers/kinds.ts` is still exported: the synchronous validation of the configuration document in `agents/doric/src/lib/config/{schema,store}.ts` (embedded bundle `host-config`) and the route tests of the `transport` still read `providerKind`; switching them to `models/v1.kind` (asynchronous, in the generation process) is separate work.
- **Bridge.** The allowlist gains `files/v1` (`directory`, `tree`, `content`), `git.commands/v1` (`status`, `branches`, `switchBranch`, `changes`, `diff`, `fileDiff`), `usage/v1.thread`, `resources/v1.project` and `terminal/v1` (`list`, `create`, `snapshot`, `input`, `resize`, `stop`, `watchProject`; never `run`).
- **Quarantine test.** `bundles.test.ts` keeps the case "a broken tool bundle" with `git`: since the `transport` does not require `git.commands/v1`, the host keeps serving.

## Stage 10 — desktop switch (10N)

- **Regions registered by the bundles themselves.** `files`, `git`, `terminal`, `usage` and `resources` call, on the client's activation, the `register*` functions they left ready (items of `layout.panel`, `layout.panel.header`, `layout.header`, `layout.footer`, `layout.panel.footer`, `layout.main.bottom`, the rows of `layout.sidebar/v1` and, in `git`, the `settings.sections` item with the `/settings/git` route). `terminal` registers only while `layout.sidebar/v1` exists (`whenAvailable`). The layout stopped registering `layout-cwd`, `layout-thread-usage` and `layout-new-terminal`, drawing its "Show/Hide files" buttons, the resource monitor, the file/diff/terminal panels and tabs and the terminal rows, and subscribing to `onSandboxActivityHook` (now the `files.refresh/v1` coordinator's). The layout's manifest loses `runtime.lease/v1`, `runtime.terminals/v1` and `resources/v1`.
- **No commits per resource.** The switch was done in a single pass (the orchestrator makes the commits); the proof is the complete e2e run at the end, not one per resource.
- **Provisional code removed.** `bundles/desktop/layout/src/client/provisional/**`, `use-project-refresh`, `queries/project-refresh`, `domain/project-refresh`, `api/chat-hooks` and `molecules/files-toggle` of the layout, the tests that covered them, `announce`/`domain/notifications` of the chat and `notifications.ts` of `desktop/client` were removed, with the `tsconfig` `exclude` entries that kept them out of the compilation.
- **Sidebar rows created by `ui/v1`.** The layout's tree mounted `<tag thread-id>` with `createElement`, and the element never received `services` (the terminal row did not appear). The rows now go through `ElementHost` (`ui.create(tag, { 'thread-id': id })`), like the panel items; the `display: contents` wrapper does not change the ARIA tree.
- **Automatic JSX in the client parts.** esbuild reads the `tsconfig.json` closest to the entry; `files`, `git`, `terminal`, `usage` and `resources` did not have `jsx` in it, and the client came out with `React.createElement` ("React is not defined"). Those `tsconfig.json` files gain `"jsx": "react-jsx"`, like `layout` and `chat`.
- **`SidebarScope` in `desktop/ui/sidebar`.** The file tree and the changes tree use `SidebarMenuButton` (which reads the sidebar's context) in a React root of their own. `SidebarScope` provides only the context (the outer one, or expanded and not mobile), with no element and no shortcut; the patch is recorded in `PROVENANCE.md` and undone by `verify-provenance.mjs`.
- **Cascade across style sheets.** In the settings window the `git` sheet (loaded after the `settings` one) re-emitted `inline-flex` after the bar's `md:hidden`, and the "Back" button appeared twice (a failure of u13 since 10G). The sheets of `files`, `git`, `terminal`, `usage`, `resources` and `threads` now also scan the sources of `layout`, `chat` and `settings`, so that every sheet is the complete ordered set; test `bundles/features/git/tests/styles-cascade.test.ts`.
- **Notifications [D9-6 closed].** `notifications` enters the composition; the chat's `announce`, the client provider of `doric.notifications/v1`, the `doric.shell/v1.notify` method and its case in the dispatcher go away; `doric.notifications/v1` goes back to `environments: ['desktopMain']`.
- **Versioning [G-15].** `settings` no longer has the `versioning` section; the section is the `git` item (order 40, same label, icon and description) at `/settings/git`. Invisible: the window opens at `/settings` and remembers the last section per window.
- **Harness.** `$S/e2e-stage10` with a copy of the checkout (`app-10n`) whose `host-bridge.ts` reads `DORIC_E2E_HOST_URL` (only in the copy, as in `app-10p`). Result: 21/21 API, 18/18 UI, `compare.mjs baseline stage10n` with no differences.

## Close of the migration

- **Exceptions to 1:1 approved by the user (2026-10-11).** (1) The schema moved to generic storage, with a verified and irreversible data migration; (2) the splash closes when the window is ready, with a timeout and recovery screens, instead of polling `/vms`; (3) the external installation of bundles (stage 11) stays out of scope, since it does not exist in the current implementation.
- **Final validation at HEAD.** API E2E gate (21/21), UI through CDP (18/18) and stage 9 (8/8) with no differences against the baseline; terminal and interrupt probes identical; suite with PostgreSQL and sandbox green; Docker image `docker-runtime` building.

## Boundary refactoring — BWP-0 (removal of the semver baseline)

- **User's decision (2026-10-11).** The build's semver check, which compared each bundle with a committed `contracts.published.json`, goes away until real publishing exists (stage 11). When it comes back, it compares with the published registry, not with a file in the repository.
- **Removed.** The 21 `bundles/*/contracts.published.json`; the `contracts-baseline` target of 21 `project.json` files; the `baseline` command of the build CLI and `writeBaseline`; the comparison step of `buildBundle`; `packages/bundle/src/build/compare.ts` (`compareWithBaseline`, `baselineSchema`, `memberChange`, `documentChange`) with the version tests of `build.test.ts`.
- **Kept.** `runtime/semver.ts` stays: the ranges of `requires` depend on it in the loader. `bump` and `ChangeLevel` also stay exported, with no consumer in the build, so that the future check can reuse them.
- **Build behavior.** Without a baseline, the build writes the same `dist/contracts.json` and the same schemas: the comparison made before and after the removal, by SHA-256 of all the `dist` files of the 22 bundles, is identical. A `contracts.published.json` left in the bundle is ignored: it is neither read nor rewritten. The `baseline` command now falls into the usage error.
- **The core's event test.** `bundles/foundation/core/tests/events.test.ts` still proves that only the `runtime` provides a durable event, but it reads the `dist/contracts.json` of each bundle of the composition, the descriptor that the build writes. The `test` target of `bundle-core` depends on the `build` of the 19 other bundles of the composition (`dependsOn` with `projects` in `bundles/foundation/core/project.json`), so `nx test bundle-core` builds them first; without that dependency the test would fail with ENOENT in a checkout without `dist`.
- **Documentation.** `versioning.md` gains the section “Current state of the check”; the READMEs of `packages/bundle`, `bundles/foundation/core` and `bundles/foundation/postgres`, the bundles paragraph of the documentation and the comment of `agents/doric/.Dockerfile` stop citing the baseline. `contracts.md`, `storage.md`, `migration.md` and `bundles.md` now say that the check is suspended until stage 11; `migration.md` keeps the stage 1 plan as a record. The earlier entries of this file that cite `contracts.published.json` are a historical record and were not rewritten.
- **Parity.** The removal does not change behavior observable by the user, REST, Socket.IO, durable data or model requests: only what the build checks and writes. The change is one of build tooling.

## Boundary refactoring — BWP-1 (opaque call scope, M1)

- **User's decision.** The platform carries the call scope without knowing its keys. `CallScope` becomes a bounded text record (`Readonly<Record<string, string>>`): each key matches `scopeKeyPattern` (`^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_.-]{0,63}$`), there are at most `SCOPE_MAX_KEYS` = 16 keys and each value has at most `SCOPE_MAX_VALUE` = 512 characters. `windowKey` is the only reserved key: the platform fills it in for the calls that come out of a window.
- **One schema for all.** `callScopeSchema` (`packages/bundle/src/contracts/call.ts`) is used by the envelope (`runtime/envelope/messages.ts`), by the log record (`contracts/platform.ts`) and by the bridge (`packages/desktop/src/client/protocol.ts`). The `.strict()` with the four named keys goes away; the restriction becomes the key limit, the size and the pattern.
- **Bridge [revised in the cross-review].** The window sends only the three keys that the baseline allowed, `projectId`, `threadId` and `promptId`, through `bridgeScopeSchema`, a `.strict()` as at HEAD. Any other key refuses the call, and `windowKey` is refused in any spelling. The values are still unbounded strings, as before. The bridge does not use `callScopeSchema`: a window could send up to 16 arbitrary keys of up to 512 characters, and each one would reach the calls of every bundle and the log records. The platform still fills in `windowKey` with the originating window.
- **Runtime helpers.** `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/scope.ts` defines `RuntimeScope`, `runtimeScope` (builds the scope from `projectId`/`threadId`/`promptId`, omitting the missing keys) and `readRuntimeScope` (reads those three keys from a call). The runtime (`host/domain/bindings.ts`, `host/services/lease.ts`) and the support tests of the runtime and of git read through them; the owner of the keys is the runtime.
- **Deviations from the design.** (1) `bundles/features/terminal/src/host/provider.ts` still reads `threadId` and `promptId` directly: the `runtime` depends on `terminal`, and importing `runtime/contracts` would close a cycle between the projects (the same reason recorded in `bundles/features/terminal/src/host/peers.ts`). (2) `bundles/foundation/core/src/host/events/facade.ts` still reads the three keys until BWP-7, which replaces the write with a sink registered by the owner; `core` does not depend on `runtime`, and a new dependency only for a read that goes away in BWP-7 is not justified.
- **Deviations reviewed and accepted.** (1) `terminal`: `runtime` depends on `terminal`, so `terminal` cannot import `runtime/contracts` without a cycle; the bundle knows its own concept of Thread, as it already does in `peers.ts`, and the direct read of the keys stays as it is. (2) `core`: the read of the three keys in `events/facade.ts` goes away with the sink of BWP-7 (M5), not in this phase.
- **Parity exceptions.** (a) Envelope: `wireScopeSchema` (`runtime/envelope/messages.ts`) discards, before the limits, the keys that do not match `scopeKeyPattern` and those whose value is not a string, as HEAD discarded unknown keys. Valid extra keys, which HEAD removed, now cross the envelope, and a scope with more than 16 keys or a value above 512 characters is refused as a whole. No current caller builds such a scope; the only literal is `{ projectId }` in `bundles/features/files/src/client/api/reads.ts`. (b) Log record: `logRecordSchema.scope` accepts any bounded key, without the `.strict()` of HEAD's four keys, because `core/shared/log.ts` copies the call's scope. (c) Wire, log lines and bridge payloads keep the same keys and the same format. These exceptions are diagnostic, or restrictive only for scopes that no current caller builds; the user's confirmation is pending.

## Boundary refactoring — BWP-4 (composition: `required` and `alternatives`, M4 and M8)

- **`required` in the composition [M4].** The composition schema accepts `required: { contracts, bundles }`. `agents/doric/composition.json` declares the five structural contracts (`runtime/v1`, `executor/v1`, `sandbox/v1`, `models/v1`, `host-config/v1`) and the `transport` bundle, which were constants in `boot.ts` before. `structuralFault(catalog, required)`, `assertStructural` and `watchStructural` receive the requirement; the refusal message does not change.
- **Absent keeps the baseline's requirement [revised in the cross-review].** A composition without `required` requires the set the host always required: `defaultRequirement` in `boot.ts`, with the five structural contracts and the `transport` bundle. It applies on the paths of `DORIC_COMPOSITION`, of the default composition and of the unreadable composition. So an operator composition without `required` still refuses a host without routing, as at HEAD. The design (M4) foresaw "nothing required", but that change of behavior was not approved by the user and so was not applied. Without the change, the shipped composition declares the same set and the default case does not change. The test `an operator composition without required refuses a host without transport, as HEAD did` confirms the refusal with HEAD's message.
- **`alternatives` in the composition [M8].** `sandbox` declares `env: DORIC_SANDBOX_PROVIDER`, `default: docker` and `options: [docker, firecracker]`. The variable chooses an option; a value outside the options chooses the default, as the baseline did with any value other than `firecracker`. The design foresaw a fatal refusal for an unknown value, but the baseline does not refuse; I kept the previous behavior.
- **Choice by the host, not by the kernel.** `packages/bundle` offers `selectAlternatives`, `enableAlternatives` and `enabledAlternatives` (`runtime/composition.ts`). The kernel does not read environment variables. `hostComposition` (`agents/doric/src/lib/bundles/boot.ts`) makes the choice: without `DORIC_COMPOSITION`, it swaps the default bundle for the chosen option in `enabled` and the kernel boots that object; with `DORIC_COMPOSITION`, it boots the file as it is and the option is that of the enabled bundle (rule D10-9 unchanged).
- **Unreadable composition.** The kernel refuses it in `bundle_load`, as before. The alternatives log comes out empty, and no `docker` label is emitted: the absence of an option no longer falls to the kernel's `docker` literal.
- **`sandbox.ts` removed [revised in the cross-review].** `agents/doric/src/lib/bundles/sandbox.ts` goes away entirely, with the pool settings (image, memory, SSH and limits) and the `docker` literal of the fallback in `index.ts`. The `docker` and `firecracker` bundles already read `DORIC_SANDBOX_IMAGE` and `DORIC_SANDBOX_SSH` and set their own memory (`bundles/sandboxes/docker/src/host/sandbox.ts`, `bundles/sandboxes/firecracker/src/host/sandbox.ts`), so the pool's behavior does not change. The log lines that described them leave the kernel and come back through the bundles' start hooks in BWP-6.
- **Logs [parity exception, diagnostic].** `sandboxProviderName` and `sandboxProvider` become `alternatives` (`{"sandbox":"docker"}`), coming from the composition's data, in `Doric starting`, `Configuring sandbox pool`, `Sandbox pool configured` and `Doric listening`. `sandboxImage`, `sandboxResources`, the limits, `networkMode` and `sshEnabled` leave `Sandbox pool configured` and `Doric starting`; BWP-6 returns them through the start hooks of the `docker` and `firecracker` bundles. The E2E gates (`gatef2.sh`) do not read these fields. The test `boot.test.ts` now checks `alternatives` in `Sandbox pool configured`.
- **Operator without `alternatives` [diagnostic, reviewed].** With `DORIC_COMPOSITION` and a `firecracker` bundle enabled without the `alternatives` block, no option is declared, so the log names no sandbox (`alternatives: {}`). The kernel does not derive the option from the enabled bundles, because that would require knowing the names of the sandbox bundles. The real pool is that of the enabled bundle. The test `an operator composition without alternatives boots as written` covers the case.
- **Desktop.** `compositionFromDistribution` does not carry `required` or `alternatives`, because the desktop resolves only the parts. The fields are optional in the schema, so the existing compositions stay valid.
- **Outside this stage.** `docs/architecture/bundles/observability.md` (the line `Doric listening {sandboxProvider}`) and the documents `composition-and-distribution.md` and `manifest.md` are left to BWP-12.
- **Validation.** `packages/bundle`: 243 tests pass, including the new `tests/composition.test.ts`. `agents/doric`: full suite with a disposable PostgreSQL, 137 pass and 2 are skipped (`integration.test.ts`, which requires `DORIC_TEST_SANDBOX=true` and a real Docker sandbox). `boot.test.ts`, with the database, checks the order of the stages and the new log fields.

## Boundary refactoring — BWP-3 (host tools, M10)

- **Design (M10).** `packages/host` ceases to exist. The `Host` — the per-prompt facade that the runner builds — and the controls `ThreadControl`, `WorkspaceControl` and `TerminalControl` become `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/tool-host/` (`host.ts`, `threads.ts`, `workspace.ts`, `terminals.ts`), exported by `runtime/contracts`. Only the `runtime` builds the `Host`; the tools receive the same capabilities through the contracts `runtime.threads/v1`, `runtime.workspace/v1` and `terminal/v1`.
- **No duplicated types.** The data types already existed as a mirror in `runtime/contracts` (`ThreadView`, `ThreadSummary`, `ThreadPage` and the like in `threads.ts`; `CwdChange` in `workspace.ts`) and in `terminal/contracts` (`TerminalInput`, `TerminalResult`, `BackgroundTerminal`). The `tool-host` files import them instead of redefining them, so type equality holds by construction.
- **Consumers.** `runtime/src/host/domain/runner.ts` and `bindings.ts` import the `Host` from the runtime itself. The tests of `files`, `git`, `threads` and `agents/doric` import from `runtime/contracts`. The `host` dependency leaves `package.json` (workspace root, `bundles/features/files`, `git`, `threads`, `terminal`, `runtime` and `agents/doric`), as do the project references in `tsconfig` and the workspace list of `package-lock.json`; `npx nx sync:check` is up to date.
- **Deviation: `terminal` does not depend on `runtime/contracts`.** The design asked that the `files`, `git`, `terminal` and `threads` bundles swap `host` for `runtime/contracts`. In `terminal` that would close a cycle, because `runtime` depends on `terminal`. `terminal` only loses the `host` dependency; the conformance of `TerminalControl` is now proven in the `runtime`.
- **Parity tests.** `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/host-parity.test.ts` checks at compile time that `RuntimeThreadControls` (without `call`) equals `ThreadControl`, that `RuntimeWorkspace['setCwd']` equals `WorkspaceControl['setCwd']`, and that `Terminal['run']` of `terminal/contracts` equals `TerminalControl['run']`. The type equalities that compared already identical types went away. `bundles/features/terminal/tests/host-parity.test.ts` was restored in the cross-review: the `runtime`'s check covers only the return of `run`, and the shape of `BackgroundTerminal` and the equality of `TerminalInput` and `TerminalResult` were no longer verified. The test now compares `terminal/v1` with literal copies of the shapes of `packages/host` at HEAD, without importing the removed package.
- **New behavioral test.** `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/domain/tool-host-workspace.test.ts`: a prompt moves its own Thread's directory through `runtime.workspace/v1` with five destinations (`alpha` and `src` accepted; `nowhere`, `../README.md` and `/etc` refused as `missing`, `not-directory` and `outside`). Each refusal returns a value, and the directory does not change.
- **Files outside the ownership list.** Besides `src/**`, BWP-3 changed the tests of `files`, `git`, `threads` and `agents/doric` that imported `host`, and `package-lock.json`. Without that, `packages/host` cannot go away.
- **Pending documentation (BWP-12).** The documentation, `docs/architecture/bundles.md`, `contexts.md`, `services-and-channels.md`, `agents/doric/README.md` and `bundles/runtime/runtime/README.md` still describe `packages/host`; their text is left to BWP-12.
- **Parity.** No change of observable behavior: only types and import paths change. There is no parity exception.
- **Validation.** Typecheck and tests of `bundle-runtime`, `bundle-files`, `bundle-git`, `bundle-threads`, `bundle-terminal` and `doric` pass, with a disposable PostgreSQL for the suites that require a database. The validation was done in an isolated tree at HEAD with the BWP-3 patch, because the concurrent work in `packages/bundle` broke the build in this tree.
- **Validation fix.** `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/support/executor.ts` had an unused import (`readRuntimeScope`), which made `tsc` fail with TS6133 and left `scope.test.ts` and `tool-host-workspace.test.ts` out of the run. The import was removed.

## Boundary refactoring — BWP-2 (primary window and `openSettings`, M12)

- **Primary window [M12].** `doric.windows/v1.open` accepts `primary` (`openWindowRequestSchema`, in `packages/bundle/src/contracts/basic/windows.ts`, where the schema of `open` lives). The core marks the `workspace` window as primary when it opens it in `onAppReadyHook`. The platform keeps the mark in the `WindowEntry`, sends the window again to the renderer as `primary` in `describeWindow` and refuses core failures outside the primary window. `bootstrapWindow` decides by the mark (`description.primary`), not by the literal `'workspace'`, and `#windowFailure` in `app.ts` uses `entry.primary`.
- **Only one primary.** `NativeWindows.open` refuses with `registration_conflict` a second primary window while the first is open. Without that rule, any bundle with `doric.windows/v1` could transfer to its own window the decision to report a core failure. The `workspace` window reopened by `focusOrOpen` keeps the mark of the original request.
- **Primary mark on an open window [review of findings].** The mark is fixed when the window is created. An `open` with `primary: true` for a key already open without the mark is refused with `registration_conflict` (`details.windowKey` is the requested key), before the only-one-primary rule; before, it came back with success and the window stayed non-primary. A request without `primary` never demotes an open primary window, because the default `false` is not a request to lose the mark. The Electron test "a primary request for an open window without the flag is refused" covers the case without depending on another primary being open.
- **`openSettings()` removed.** The method of `DesktopPlatform` was the last remnant of `doric:settings:open`, which went away in the stage 9 cut, and had no production caller. The command `core.open-settings/v1` is still served by menus and shortcuts. The Electron test now executes the command by its identifier, with the new `runCommand(id, input = {})`; the assertions on `/settings`, size, title and focus did not change.
- **Parity exception [internal].** The window descriptor (`WindowDescription`, the bridge between main and renderer) gains the `primary` field, `false` in the splash. It is not REST nor Socket.IO, and no screen changes: only the core marks the window, and the core marks only `workspace`.
- **Extended ownership.** Besides the files listed for BWP-2, the work touched `packages/desktop/src/client/protocol.ts` (`WindowDescription` and its schema), `app/doric/src/platform/bridge/dispatcher.ts` (builds the descriptor), `app/doric/src/platform/windows/native-windows.ts` and `registry.ts` (keep and validate the mark), the unit tests of `app/doric/tests`, `packages/desktop/tests/client`, `bundles/foundation/core/tests/desktop/lifecycle.test.ts` and the window fakes of `bundles/{core,chat,threads,settings}/tests/client/support/window.ts`, which now report `primary`. Without these files the field does not reach the renderer and the typecheck does not pass. The fakes preserve the previous behavior: `workspace` in the core is primary, `main` (chat, threads) and `settings` are not.
- **Validation.** `packages/bundle` and `packages/desktop`: typecheck and tests pass (243 tests in `bundle`). `bundles/foundation/core`: typecheck and tests pass. `app/doric`: `tsc` of `tsconfig.electron.json` clean; unit tests pass, 89 with 54 skipped (the skipped ones are the tests of `agents/doric` that require `connectionString`, not touched here); `doric-app:test-electron` passes 38 of 38, including the new primary-window test. The clients `bundle-settings`, `bundle-layout`, `bundle-history`, `bundle-notifications`, `doric-renderer` and `doric-platform` pass their tests; `bundle-chat` (267) and `bundle-threads` (14) also pass their tests.
- **Pending items.** (1) The E2E gate `gatef2.sh` was not run: the script stops and recreates the `doric` stack with `stack.sh down` and `up`, and the instruction forbids touching the live stack. The parity of the API (21/21), of the UI (18/18) and of stage-9 (8/8) was not checked in this round. (2) `desktop/ui` and `desktop/reads` were under concurrent editing by BWP-11 when the validation ran; that is why the typecheck of `bundle-chat` and `bundle-threads` reports errors only in those two packages, none in the files of BWP-2. (3) No commit: the index had deletions by another agent, and the change stayed in the working tree.

## Boundary refactoring — BWP-11 (panel reads and UI in the client, M11)

- **Generic package.** `packages/desktop` keeps what does not name a bundle concept: `createRefreshQueue`; `refreshQueries(client, escopo, casa, periódico)`, the coalesced reread with HEAD's rule for reads that errored or are pending; the paths (`ROOT_PATH`, `joinPath`, `parentPath`, `baseName`, `compactPath`); `messageFrom`; and the reread policy (`readRetry`, `pendingReadInterval`). The retry policy was named `sandboxReadRetry`, which carried the product concept `sandbox` into a platform package; the review of findings changed only the name, without changing the values (`retry: 3`, exponential delay capped at 8 s).
- **Sandbox in `files/contracts`.** `bundles/features/files/src/contracts/reads.ts` publishes what `git` reads of the sandbox: `workspacePath`, `SANDBOX_ROOT`, `ROOT_PATH`, `sandboxNotice`, `ProjectLeaseState` and `SandboxStatus`. That module does not import `desktop/reads` at run time, because the window loads it through its own specifier. The rest belongs to `files` and lives in `src/client/reads/` (the read results, `treeReadState`, `fileReadState`, `answerOf`, `pathSegments`, `truncationNotice`). `refreshSandbox` rereads only the client's own `['files', p, …]` keys.
- **Git.** `bundles/features/git/src/contracts/reads.ts` publishes the change types and `ChangeMark` (color, letter, count and strikethrough of a mark). `src/client/reads/change-tree.ts` has the change tree, the marks (`changeMarks`) and the totals. `ChangeCounts` goes to `src/client/changes/change-counts.tsx`. `git.changes/v1` now carries `decorations`, the marks per path; the file tree draws them (`files-tree.tsx`), instead of decorating from `git` on its own. The git store rereads the project keys that belong to it (changes, comparisons and the threads' summaries) through `refreshQueries`.
- **Desktop UI.** `desktop/ui/workspace` becomes `desktop/ui/panels`, because `workspace` is a product concept. `FileTree` is generic over `TreeNode` and `TreeDecoration` (text, count, color, letter and strikethrough): whoever knows what the mark means builds it. `emptyDirectoryNotice`, `toggleExpanded` and `TreeSkeleton({ root, label })` now belong to the panel; `label` is required, because the text the skeleton announces belongs to the bundle that shows the tree (`files` passes `"Loading files"`, and there is no longer a default in the platform package). `fileLanguage` and `changedLineCounts` go to `desktop/ui/code` (`language.ts` and `diff-counts.ts`), which are the editor's vocabulary. `DiffEditor` receives `diffTokens` (the names of the addition and removal tokens) from the caller, and the diff theme is defined per pair of tokens, so that a window with a diff and with a plain file does not repaint each other's editor.
- **`--git-*` tokens.** They leave `tokens.ts`, the `--color-git-*` mapping in `tailwind.css` and the generated `tokens.css`. The values (light in `:root`, dark in `.dark`) live in `bundles/features/git/src/client/styles.css`, and the classes become `text-[var(--git-…)]`. Tailwind compiles those classes into `color: var(--git-…)`, the same computation that `--color-git-*` did, so the color is identical.
- **Deviations from the design [review].** (1) The tokens do not go through `doric.tokens/v1`: that contract is a single platform provider, with cardinality one and the fixed set of `tokens.json`, with no path for a bundle to contribute. The variables live in the `git` bundle's sheet; its header was corrected (it no longer says "No rules of its own"). Reach verified: `client-routing.ts` builds `parts` with all the client entries of the generation, and each window receives the `git` sheet (`styles.ts`, one `<link>` per scheduled part). So the windows with `--git-*` consumers (file tree with marks, diff and counts) load the variables; the splash renders none. What remains is the risk of checking the colors only on the live stack (stage-9). (2) There is no `files.sandbox-changed/v1` event. The design (M11) asked for it, and the design's review (BWP-11, block "Revised (review round, BWP-11)") replaces it with the `files.refresh/v1` coordinator: `ClientContext` exposes `services` (provide, use, whenAvailable) and has no client event channel, so the event would be new infrastructure. The coordinator is already the single trigger, with 250 ms coalescing, the 15 s poll and the reread of each registrant through its own keys. `git` registers its reads (`use-project-reads.ts`) and its `refresh` rereads only its keys; the coordinator only knows `ProjectReads`, with no Git key. (3) `pendingReadInterval`, `readRetry` and `messageFrom` stay in `desktop/reads` and not in `files`, because they are a generic reread policy: so `git` and `resources` do not depend on `files` at run time because of a retry. (4) `resources` does not import `files`: `layout` depends on `resources` and `files` depends on `layout`, so the `ProjectLeaseState` type is restated in the bundle and the Nx graph gains no cycle. (5) `answerOf`, `pathSegments` and `ROOT_NAME` still have no production consumer, as at HEAD; they were moved without changes.
- **New dependencies.** `git` depends on `files/contracts` (the values `workspacePath`, `ROOT_PATH` and `sandboxNotice`, and types). The default composition keeps `files` enabled; `git` already depends on `layout/contracts` through the same mechanism. `packages/desktop` and `resources` lose the dependency on `desktop/reads` for what belongs to `files`.
- **Parity.** No parity exception. The text, the roles, the visible classes and the cache keys are the same; the Git keys were already reread by the git client, and `files` never reread them (the Git part of the old `refreshProject` was a no-op in the `files` client). The new tests prove the separation: the refresh of `files` does not touch the Git keys, that of `git` touches only its own, and the `git` sheet declares HEAD's five values in both modes.
- **Historical validation.** The read, UI and client tests ran in three Nx targets before the consolidation into `desktop:test`. The other targets of this stage were `bundle-files:test`, `bundle-git:test`, `bundle-layout`, `bundle-terminal`, `bundle-usage`, `bundle-resources`, `doric-platform`, `doric-renderer` and `doric`, besides typecheck and build of the affected bundles, `nx sync:check`, `npm run format:check` and `npm run lint`.
- **Pending items.** (1) The E2E gate and the stage 9 visual one (8/8) were not run: they require the live stack. The diff colors of the Monaco editor cannot be checked by the tests in Node. (2) Concept documents and the documentation (which still cite `desktop/ui/workspace` and `desktop/reads`) are left to BWP-12. (3) No commit.
- **Review of findings (BWP-2 + BWP-11).** Findings fixed: `sandboxReadRetry` renamed to `readRetry` (desktop/reads and the five importers); `TreeSkeleton` with a required `label`; the header of `styles.css` corrected; the requirement of `files.sandbox-changed/v1` was replaced by the coordinator, with a "Revised (review round, BWP-11)" block in `design.md`; the primary mark on an open window without it is now refused (BWP-2). No finding was rejected. Validation on the final tree: `nx reset`, `nx sync`, `typecheck` (46 projects), `test` (47 projects), `test` with a disposable PostgreSQL and `DORIC_TEST_SANDBOX=true` (47 projects, no database test skipped), `doric-app:test-electron` (including the new test), `build` (47 projects), `format:check` and `lint` clean; `docker build` of `doric-host:bwip` passed with `--network host`, because the registry did not resolve in the default build (`EAI_AGAIN`).

## Boundary refactoring — BWP-5 (generation holds, M2)

- **Design decision.** The generation does not know what a Prompt is. `ExecutionRef` (`projectId`, `threadId`, `promptId`) leaves `packages/bundle/src/contracts/platform.ts`. The hold becomes `WorkHold { key, label? }`, validated by `workHoldSchema` (strict): `key` has from 1 to 256 characters and `label`, optional, up to 256. The key is opaque to the platform.
- **Owner from the call.** The owner of the hold is the bundle that calls `doric.generation/v1.hold`, read from `call.caller.bundleId` in the kernel (`packages/bundle/src/process/kernel.ts`). The input never names it: `workHoldSchema` refuses `bundleId`, and a real-process test shows that the loss reports the hold under the bundle that took it (`probe`).
- **Manager.** `process/manager.ts` keeps `Hold` as `{type:'work', bundleId, key, label?}` or `{type:'window', windowKey}`; `isExecution` (the test for the presence of `promptId`) goes away. `GenerationSummary.executions` becomes `work` (count of work holds). `LostGeneration.executions` becomes `holds: {bundleId, key, label?}[]`, with only the work holds. `GenerationManager.acquire` receives `WorkHoldRef` (`bundleId` and `key`), and the `WorkHoldRef` type is exported by the kernel and by the package.
- **Drain.** A draining generation ends when there is no work hold and no call in flight. Before, the condition was `holds.size === 0`. The difference is that window holds do not count, as the design asks. No production path creates a window hold, so the difference is not observable today.
- **Runtime [no change].** `bundles/runtime/runtime` takes no generation hold in production: the stage 4 hold went away (comment in `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/host/domain/runtime.ts`). So there is no hold caller to switch and no loss handler to adjust; `platform-providers.ts` only passes the types on.
- **Consumers in the host [no change of production code].** `agents/doric/src/lib/bundles/handover.ts` reads only `generation`, `incarnation` and `state` from a loss. The test `agents/doric/tests/handover.test.ts` swaps the fields of the fake summaries and losses.
- **Decision of this phase: label limit.** The design does not fix the size of `label`; a maximum of 256 characters was adopted, the same as the key's, to keep the boundary bounded.
- **Parity.** No exception for the public API, REST, Socket.IO, durable data or model requests. What changes is the internal shape of the manager (`executions` → `work`, `holds`) and the removal of the `ExecutionRef` type from the `bundle` package, which had no consumer outside the package itself.
- **Tests.** Updated: `packages/bundle/tests/process.test.ts` (loss with `holds` and the `work` count), `packages/bundle/tests/support/generation-fixtures.ts` (the probe holds `p/t/<promptId>`) and `agents/doric/tests/handover.test.ts`. New: `packages/bundle/tests/work-hold.test.ts` (limits of the key and of the label; no owner field) and the real-process test "a work hold taken through doric.generation/v1 is reported with its key when its generation is lost".
- **Validation.** `npx nx run bundle:typecheck` and `npx nx run bundle:test` (247 tests, all passing). `npx tsc -p agents/doric/tsconfig.spec.json` and `node --test agents/doric/dist-tests/tests/*.test.js` (89 pass without a database; with `DORIC_TEST_DATABASE_URL` pointing to a disposable PostgreSQL 18.4, 141 pass and 2 are skipped because they require the sandbox). `npx nx run doric:build` (it rebuilt the copies of the bundles in `agents/doric/dist`, which were out of date because of the ongoing phase of another phase of the reorganization). `biome format` and `biome check` clean on the files of this phase.
- **Pending items.** (1) The E2E gate (`$S/gatef2.sh`, 21/21 API, 18/18 UI, 8/8 stage 9 and terminal probes) was not run: it requires the live stack, and the tree still has changes from another phase in progress (BWP-6/BWP-7). (2) `npm run format:check` and `npm run lint` are red because of files of another phase (`bundles/foundation/core/src/host/events/*`, `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/events.ts`, `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/host/store/events.ts`, `packages/bundle/src/contracts/basic/events.ts`, `packages/bundle/tests/basic-contracts.test.ts`); no file of this phase appears in the lists. (3) The concept documents of `process-isolation.md`, `hooks.md` and the documentation (which still speak of "execution" and of `ExecutionRef`) are left to BWP-12.

## Boundary refactoring — BWP-7 (event sink, M5)

- **Design (M5).** `events/v1` gains `sink(spec)`: the owner of the events registers once where its rows go, with ordered and mandatory `scopeKeys`, a `stream` with `{chave}` placeholders (each one names a key of the sink) and `scopeDescription`, used in the message of `invalid_scope`. `threadEventsStream` and `threadEventEnvelopeSchema` leave `packages/bundle`: the platform no longer knows the threads stream.
- **Owner of the sink.** `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/events.ts` exports `threadEventSink`, with `scopeKeys` `projectId`, `threadId`, `promptId` and the stream `runtime.thread.events/{threadId}`. The runtime's `activate` registers it and releases it in the cleanup. The envelope (`threadEventEnvelopeSchema`) and the `ThreadEventEnvelope` type now live in `runtime/contracts/storage.ts`; the runtime's readers import from there.
- **The core's facade.** An event row has the sink's keys in its order, then `type` and `event`, the same order as before. A missing sink key, or no registered sink, gives `invalid_scope`. The ownership of `append` (only the owner appends) and the validation of the payload did not change.
- **Owner guard (not a feature).** A sink can only write to a stream whose family belongs to the bundle that registers it, the same ownership rule as `storage/v1`, checked at registration with a sample value. Otherwise, `not_owner`. So a bundle does not redirect events to other bundles' streams. The sink is one per provider: a second live registration gives `registration_conflict`, with the same generation rule as the event registration.
- **Parity.** The stream's name and the stored row do not change. The row's fixture was captured from the code before the sink (`{"projectId":"p-1","threadId":"t-1","promptId":"q-1","type":"notify.sent/v1","event":{...}}`) and is compared byte for byte in the core's test; swapping the order of `type` and `event` makes that test fail. No production path calls `events/v1.append` (the runtime only registers `runtime.prompt-finished/v1`, as before, and writes the threads directly through `storage/v1`), so there is no parity exception for the user.
- **Ownership deviations.** Besides the files of BWP-7, the work touched `bundles/foundation/core/tests/events.test.ts` (new test sink and fixture), `packages/bundle/tests/basic-contracts.test.ts` (the envelope test went to the runtime; the sink schema test took its place), `packages/bundle/tests/values.test.ts` (the `sink` entry of `events/v1` in "stated way across the envelope"), `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/contracts/event-sink.test.ts` (new: the runtime's sink, the envelope and the registration on activation with release in the cleanup) and the READMEs `packages/bundle/README.md` and `bundles/foundation/core/README.md`, which described the removed stream and API.
- **Pending items.** The documentation and the concept documents (`events-and-nodes.md`, `storage.md`, `core.md`, `contracts.md`) still describe the runtime's stream written by the core; they are left to BWP-12.
- **Validation.** An isolated tree at HEAD with this BWP applied (a temporary `git worktree`, `node_modules` cloned). `typecheck` of 46 projects, `test` of 47 projects with a disposable PostgreSQL (2944 pass, 0 fail, 5 skip: Docker sandbox tests without `DORIC_TEST_SANDBOX`), `build` of 47 projects. `biome format` and `biome check` clean on the changed files. The E2E gate `gatef2.sh` was not run: it recreates the `doric` stack, which is forbidden in this task.

## Cross-review — BWP-5 and BWP-7

- **Owner guard per row (sink, finding 1).** Fixed at the root. The registration check uses a sample value (`x` in each key). With a placeholder in the owner's position (`{projectId}.x/{threadId}`), it only passed for a bundle named `x`, and the row could then write to the stream of any owner, such as `runtime`. Now `events/v1.append` computes the row's stream with the call's values and requires the owner of that stream to be the bundle that registered the sink; otherwise it answers `not_owner` before any write. The registration still refuses early. Test: `bundles/foundation/core/tests/events.test.ts`, "a sink whose owner segment is a placeholder writes only the streams of its owner, row by row". Without the fix, that test fails.
- **Window hold (finding 2).** New tests in `packages/bundle/tests/process.test.ts`, with the real manager (`HostGenerations`, obtained from `platform.generations` and checked with `instanceof`): (a) a window hold alone does not appear in `describe()` as work and does not prevent a draining generation from ending; (b) a draining generation ends when the work hold ends, even with the window one still present. No production path takes a window hold; the tests call `holdFor` directly. With the old rule (`holds.size === 0`), both fail.
- **Loss per bundle (finding 3).** The loss test with work holds uses two bundles (`probe` and `other`) in the same generation. The probe fixture accepts `id` (default `probe`). The test checks `holds` with one entry per bundle, each with its key, and `work` equal to 2. The window hold of the same generation does not enter the loss.
- **The runtime's sink compared byte for byte (finding 4) [not changed; deviation from the design recorded].** The design (M5) asks for a runtime test that compares the stored row with the fixture. The byte-for-byte fixture lives in `bundles/foundation/core/tests/events.test.ts`, because it is the core that writes the row. The runtime's test (`bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/contracts/event-sink.test.ts`) pins `scopeKeys`, in order, and the stream, and with those two values the core's writer defines the shape of the row. Joining the two in a single test would require one test package depending on the other (`core` or `runtime`), which the boundary does not allow. Separate finding: no production path calls `events/v1.append` with `runtime.prompt-finished/v1` (the search in `bundles/*/src`, `agents`, `packages` and `app` finds no such use). The runtime writes the threads' rows directly in `storage/v1` (`bundles/runtime/runtime/src/host/store/threads.ts`, with `threadEventsStream`). So `threadEventSink` is registered but with no write in production. Keeping the registration or routing the writes through it is left as a pending item for BWP-12.

## Boundary refactoring — BWP-6 (host lifecycle, M3)

- **Design decision.** The kernel stops knowing the bundles' lifecycle. `AppHooksV1` gains `onHostStartHook` and `onHostStopHook`, of the new kind `sequence` (handlers awaited one by one, without a deadline, with the failure of a handler rejecting the execution; the stop uses `order: 'reverse'`). It also gains `onGenerationChangedHook` (`activated`, `draining`, `lost`, `stopped`), delivered to each live generation, and `onPeerSignalHook`. `doric.generation/v1.signal` carries a signal to the other live generations of the same bundle. `doric.startup/v1` (host only) has `stage(name)` and `log(record)`, and writes the startup lines in the same format as before.
- **Kernel.** `platform.start()` runs the start hooks in activation order and, after them, reads the storage identity. `platform.shutdown()` runs the stop hooks in reverse order, before `platform.stop()`. A generation that starts draining receives `draining`, and only then does the manager check whether it can stop: the runtime takes the `runtime.drain` hold in that hook, and the stop waits for it. The switch is not announced at boot (the boot is not a switch) nor during `stop()`.
- **Runtime.** `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/host/lifecycle.ts` has the start step (stage `workspace_reconciliation`; 'Reconciling persisted projects and threads' and, at the end, 'Workspace reconciled' with `interruptedThreads` and `interruptedProjects`) and the stop step (`dispose`). `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/host/handover.ts` does the handover that the kernel did with `drain`, `recoverWith` and `adopt`: it reacts to `onGenerationChangedHook` and to `onPeerSignalHook` with the topic `runtime.released`. The `runtime.drain` hold is taken before the drain and released at the end; the generation stops afterwards. The methods `recover`, `recoverWith`, `adopt`, `drain` and `dispose` of `runtime/v1` stay in the contract for one version, with no caller in the host.
- **Core.** `bundles/foundation/core/src/host/secrets/activation.ts` registers the credentials start step: it connects the storage through `storage.backend/v1.connect`, marks `credential_activation`, logs 'Activating stored credentials' and reads the secrets once, so that a missing or unusable key fails the host there.
- **Postgres [deviation from the design].** `postgres` does not register its own start hook. `hooks/v1` is provided by `core`, which requires `postgres`'s `storage.backend/v1`; the registration would create an activation cycle. So `connect()` became a method of `storage.backend/v1` (no arguments, `void`): `postgres` implements it in `bundles/foundation/postgres/src/host/start.ts`, which marks `database_client`, logs 'Initializing PostgreSQL client', connects with the check of legacy tables and logs 'PostgreSQL client initialized'. `memory` is a no-op. The change is additive; the data do not change.
- **Configuration, sandbox and transport.** The configuration start step ('Activating Doric configuration', stage `configuration_activation`) stays in the provisional host-config (`agents/doric/src/lib/bundles/configuration.ts`), until BWP-9. Those of `bundles/sandboxes/docker/src/host/start.ts` and `bundles/sandboxes/firecracker/src/host/start.ts` use the `sandbox_pool` stage. The transport (`bundles/foundation/transport/src/kernel.ts`) logs 'Network interfaces configured' with `socketNamespaces` in its own start step. `docker`, `firecracker` and the transport (`transportRequires`) now list `hooks/v1@^1.0.0`.
- **Removed.** `agents/doric/src/lib/bundles/handover.ts` and `agents/doric/src/lib/bundles/database.ts`, and the test `agents/doric/tests/handover.test.ts`, whose cases went to `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/host/handover-hooks.test.ts`. `agents/doric/src/index.ts` kept the composition root: it brings up the bundles, runs `platform.start()`, installs the bridge, waits for `ready`, listens, and on shutdown runs `platform.shutdown()` and `platform.stop()`.
- **Kept because of a dependency [deviation from the design].** `agents/doric/src/lib/credentials/*` was not removed. `CredentialService` is still consumed by the provisional host-config (`configuration.ts` and `config/*`), which goes away in BWP-8 and BWP-9. The activation moved to the core; the removal of the directory is left to those phases.
- **Event delivery [fixed in the BWP-6 review; no deviation].** (a) An `observe` hook is executed once per live generation, and only the handlers of that generation receive it. `live` lists the active or draining generations after the change, without the generation that fell or stopped. (b) In `lost`, `holds` reaches each handler with only its own bundle's holds, as the design (M2 and review (c) of M3) requires. `defineHook` accepts `forOwner` in `observe` hooks; the core's `hooks/v1` and the test one apply the filter per handler before calling it, and skip the handler when `forOwner` returns `undefined`. (c) `onPeerSignalHook` uses the same mechanism: the peer signal only reaches the handlers of the bundle that emitted it, in the other live generations, as M3 defines. No bundle name enters the kernel. Test: `packages/bundle/tests/lifecycle.test.ts`, 'a lost generation hands each bundle only its own holds, and a peer signal stays with its bundle' (two bundles in the same generation; before the filter, the second bundle received the first one's holds and signal).
- **Storage identity.** `storageIdentity` is read after the whole sequence of start hooks. Before, it was read at the credentials stage. An identity failure is attributed to the last reported stage. In production, the identity only fails when the connection has already failed at `database_client`, so the effect is diagnostic.
- **Parity exceptions (startup logs, diagnostic).** (1) `Doric listening` loses `interrupted`; the value comes out in `Workspace reconciled`, from the runtime, before `Binding HTTP listener`. (2) `Network interfaces configured` leaves the kernel and comes from the transport, before the sandbox block. (3) `Configuring sandbox pool` and `Sandbox pool configured` come from the sandbox bundle, with the baseline's fields (`sandboxProviderName`, `sandboxImage`, `sandboxResources`, limits, `networkMode`, `sshEnabled`). BWP-4 had put `alternatives` in them, and that key goes away. (4) The order of the startup lines changed; the new order is the activation order. `Doric starting` and `Doric listening` keep `alternatives`. Checked in the scripts of the E2E harness (`gatef2.sh`, and the api, ui, lib and stack ones of `e2e-stage10`): none reads `interrupted`, `sshEnabled`, `socketNamespaces` or these lines.
- **Test doubles.** `createMinimalHooks` (`bundle/testing`) now executes `sequence` and filters the handlers by generation, as `core` does. The docker, firecracker and transport harnesses now provide `hooks/v1` with that double, since the host always has it through the core. The `storage.backend/v1` doubles gain `connect`.
- **Tests.** New: `packages/bundle/tests/lifecycle.test.ts` (start and stop order with the activation order; start failure with the stage named by the bundle; stop failure reported, with the host still stopping; `order` only in `sequence`; `activated`, `draining`, `stopped` and the peer signal across real processes; `lost` with reported holds). `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/host/handover-hooks.test.ts` (switch, loss, respawn, signal from another bundle, start and stop of the runtime). Four `sequence` cases in `bundles/foundation/core/tests/hooks.test.ts`. Updated: `packages/bundle/tests/values.test.ts` (crossing table: `connect`, `signal`, `doric.startup/v1`) and `agents/doric/tests/boot.test.ts` (order and new fields, against a real host with PostgreSQL).
- **Pending items.** (1) The E2E gate (`gatef2.sh`) was not run in this phase. The harness uses the compose project `doric-e2e-stage10`, separate from the `doric` stack, but it requires the host image and the complete stack, so it is left to the gate phase. (2) The documentation and the concept documents (`process-isolation.md`, `hooks.md`, `core.md`, `storage.md`, `contexts.md`) are left to BWP-12. (3) The firecracker sandbox has no dedicated test for the startup lines; the docker one is covered by the boot test. (4) `lib/credentials/*` stays until BWP-8 and BWP-9.

- **Handover logs [parity exception, diagnostic].** The failures `Generation drain failed` and `Generation recovery failed` carry the `generation` field again (the affected generation), as at HEAD. `Project adoption failed` still has only `err`, as at HEAD. The lines now come out of the runtime's log (component `bundles`), and not out of HEAD's `startup`, which the kernel passed to `installHandover`. Confirmed: no script of the E2E harness reads these lines.
- **Handover tests.** Added in `bundles/runtime/runtime/tests/host/handover-hooks.test.ts`: 'a failed generation activates no runtime: it recovers nothing' (a generation in state `failed` does not call `recoverWith`), a case that was at HEAD in `agents/doric/tests/handover.test.ts` and had not been ported.
- **Pending items of the BWP-6 review.** (1) `agents/doric/src/lib/credentials/*` stays in place: the design asks for its removal, and the remaining consumer is `configuration.ts` (BWP-9). The decision to move the `CredentialService` type to the secrets contract or to keep the directory until BWP-9 rests with the orchestrator; it was not changed in this round. (2) The E2E gate `gatef2.sh` was not run in this round: the gate requires the complete stack of the host image, and the script recreates the `doric` stack with `stack.sh down` and `up`. The parity of API (21/21), UI (18/18), stage 9 (8/8) and terminal probes is still not checked against the current tree.

## Final integration of the boundary (2026-10-12)

- `host-config` and `transport` are installed bundles with their own manifest and descriptor. The composition includes them in `enabled` and `resident`. `resident` selects host parts that need to share native resources; it does not identify features in the kernel. Changing that selection requires a kernel restart.
- `doric.network/v1.register` hands the HTTP and Socket.IO listeners to a local function of the bundle, with release on shutdown. The host creates the listeners; `transport` registers the routes and namespaces. The manifest can declare optional contracts in `optional`, allowing features to be unavailable without adding mandatory dependencies to the activation.
- The bridge refuses methods without `expose: 'desktop'`. Basic contracts are bounded by the protocol's declarations; other contracts declare the exposure in the bundle itself. `desktopScope` restricts the keys accepted per contract, and `windowKey` still comes only from the platform. Separate snapshots cover the host's methods and the desktop services for windows, menus and commands already used by the client.
- `GIT` is registered by `git`, with username and email mandatory and no secret. The generic authentication kinds stay available in the core. The migration `20261012000000_registered_credential_kinds` converts the credential kind column from enum to text, preserving IDs and encrypted bytes. That allows persisting kinds declared by other bundles without a new version of the backend. The 110 PostgreSQL tests, including that conversion, passed on a real database.
- The v0 manifest conversion preserves the ID written by the author; the product exception that renamed `core` to `files` was removed. The distributed bundles already use v1. External installation is still postponed.
- The desktop's connection observes `/doric/bridge`, which belongs to the platform. `/status` stays available in the `transport` bundle for existing consumers.
- The bundle folders are grouped into `foundation`, `runtime`, `providers`, `sandboxes`, `features` and `desktop`; IDs, npm packages, contracts and the directories of the distributed artifacts stay the same. `transport` lives in `foundation`.
- The sandbox recovery also checks the configuration of the container found. An incompatible change of image, limits, network, DNS or SSH replaces the container and preserves the volume. The acquisition fails if it cannot remove duplicates. Concurrent tests verify idempotent release; 60 tests passed with real Docker.
- The complete E2E gate was run once after the integration, repeating only the scenarios affected by the fixes. The evidence and the classified differences are in [Integration validation](./validation-2026-10-12.md); the historical results below do not validate this revision.

## Boundary refactoring — BWP-12 (documentation of the boundary)

- **Binding rule.** The platform installs bundles, composes providers, routes calls and keeps processes, windows and IPC. It does not know bundle concepts (Project, Thread, prompt, queue, Git, settings) nor their methods or storage formats. A named field of a generic schema is also a leak, so `projectId`, `threadId` and `promptId` do not appear in platform code. The rule was confirmed by the user.
- **Scope of this phase.** The documentation (new section _Platform Boundary_, and the sections on the current runtime, configuration, credentials, sandbox, transport and startup rewritten to the state of the code), `agents/AGENTS.md`, `packages/AGENTS.md` and the concept documents of `docs/architecture/bundles/` cited at the end of this section. No Hard Constraint clause was changed. The historical statements (the `packages/host` package, the embedded transport and host-config, `workspace` as a literal, fixed boot dependencies, `allowed-tools` restricted to the bundle itself, the event sink in the core) were replaced, not given an added "now" paragraph.
- **Platform mechanisms (M1 to M12).** Opaque and bounded call scope (`callScopeSchema`, with `windowKey` reserved to the platform); `WorkHold` attributed by the calling bundle; hooks `onHostStartHook`, `onHostStopHook`, `onGenerationChangedHook` and `onPeerSignalHook`; `doric.startup/v1` for startup stages and logs; `required` and `alternatives` in the composition; event `sink` with one sink per provider; `registerKind` in `secrets/v1`; method exposure by declaration (`expose`, `x-doric-expose`) and `desktopScope` per provider; `primary` on the window; `packages/host` removed, with the `Host` types in `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/contracts/tool-host`.
- **Generic additions requested in the parent.** `composition.resident` lists installed bundles that run in the host's native process, not in a generation process; the distributed composition uses `host-config` and `transport`. `manifest.optional` lists contract IDs that a bundle may call on a best-effort basis, with no activation dependency; with no active provider, the call keeps the caller's unavailable behavior (the transport's feature reads are the example). `doric.network/v1.register(listener)` gives a local callback over the native HTTP and Socket.IO handles, which are never serialized: the host owns the listener and the generic bridge (`/doric/bridge`), and the transport registers all routes through that callback. No change in the foundation and core phases.
- **Exposure (BWP-10).** The default is to deny. A method only reaches the desktop window when it declares `expose: 'desktop'`, and each provider declares in `desktopScope` the scope keys a window may send; `windowKey` belongs to the platform only. The snapshot `agents/doric/tests/bridge-exposure.snapshot.json` is a permanent CI gate: changing the exposed set requires updating the snapshot in the review. No `contracts.published.json` is kept (BWP-0).
- **Credentials.** The basic kinds (`API_TOKEN`, `USERNAME_PASSWORD`) stay in the generic contract. The `GIT` kind is registered by the `git` bundle with `username` and `email` only, never with a secret. The stored ID does not change.
- **Approved fix in the sandbox (BWP-14) [intentional deviation from 1:1].** This is the only observable behavior change approved in this refactoring. When acquiring a Project after restarting the host, the Docker provider reattaches the workspace's running container (or removes the stale ones), so that there is one sandbox per Project, with the volume and the files preserved. The baseline (eda9171) created a new container at each restart, accumulating duplicates. In the final validation, the probe `sandboxAfterRestart` observed 1 sandbox, with the same container and the file preserved, in both the normal and the abrupt restart; the SSH terminal also remained operational. The fix is recorded as a fix, not as parity. Evidence in `validation-2026-10-12.md`.
- **Diagnostic log differences [not 1:1 content parity].** Besides the exceptions already recorded in BWP-6 (`interrupted`, `socketNamespaces`, `sshEnabled` and the order of the lines), the `sandboxProvider` key of `Doric starting` and `Doric listening` is replaced by `alternatives: {sandbox: ...}`, generated from composition data. The name of the environment variable stays only in `composition.json`. No script of the E2E harness reads these lines (checked in BWP-6).
- **Paths.** BWP-13 grouped the bundles into `foundation`, `runtime`, `providers`, `sandboxes`, `features` and `desktop`, including the paths of the documentation, Docker, Nx and TypeScript. IDs, npm names and Nx project names do not change. The source folder does not determine the activation phase.
- **Reconciliation of the implementation.** The `host-config` manifest is installed; the product allowlist was removed; the credential kinds are registered by the bundles; the domain widgets and reads left the generic packages. The final search in the platform's production code did not find the Project/Thread/Prompt IDs and fields pointed out in the review.
- **Validation.** The evidence of this revision is that of the final integration, not the historical gates of the earlier stages. The complete run uses an isolated stack and repeats only the scenarios affected by the fixes.
- **Concept documents revised.** `docs/architecture/bundles/`: `contexts.md`, `process-isolation.md`, `core.md`, `services-and-channels.md`, `storage.md`, `contracts.md`, `hooks.md`, `events-and-nodes.md`, `composition-and-distribution.md`, `manifest.md`, `windows.md`, `splash-and-recovery.md`, `platform-packages.md`, `observability.md`, `feature-split.md`, `migration.md`, `versioning.md` and `bundles.md` (index). Each one receives the section corresponding to the new boundary, not a "now" paragraph added to the old text.

## Fixes found in the integrated validation (2026-10-12)

- The distributed catalog must preserve `optional` and `desktopScope`. Discarding them made the bridge refuse calls that the provider had declared.
