- Besides the host contracts, the desktop services for windows, menus, commands, notifications and history cache need to declare the exposure of their methods. The exposure check includes these services. The chat declares `history.snapshots/v1` as an optional dependency, keeping it working without a cache.
- `doric.migrations/v1` needs to be announced to the generation's desktop process. A test with real Electron reproduced the absence of the persisted marker before the fix and passed after it.
- The app's authorized exit waits for the platform's route and state writes. Before, the write started when the window closed could lose the race with the process exit and restore the previous route. The second exit request still forces the shutdown, as before.
- The terminal probe with SSH enabled verified interruption, Thread end, normal restart and abrupt crash of the host. After both restarts, there was a single sandbox, with the same container and the file preserved. A new terminal also worked after the crash. The stack and the sandboxes of that probe were removed at the end.

## Base UI shared through the import map (2026-10-17)

- **Base UI in place of Radix.** The components of `desktop/ui` now use Base UI (`@base-ui/react`), and the platform serves it once per window, like React, because its contexts and stores need a single instance: a bundle's Base UI and that of `desktop/ui` are the same. This replaces the part of S8-21 in which Radix was bundled inside `desktop/ui`.
- **Folder and subpaths.** `@base-ui/react` enters `esmPackages` and comes out of a single esbuild with `splitting` in the folder `@base-ui/react@<versão>/`, with one entry per subpath of `exports` (except `./package.json`), so the subpaths share the chunks. The dependencies (`@base-ui/utils`, `@floating-ui/*`, `reselect`, `use-sync-external-store`) go into the chunks, with the licenses in `licenses/`, and the CommonJS `require('react')` of `use-sync-external-store` goes through the interop module, getting the map's instance. `desktop/ui` keeps the Base UI specifiers external, like every platform specifier, with no copy in the chunks of `desktop@`.
- **Subpath that does not run in a window.** General build rule: a subpath of an ESM package whose code imports a package the workspace does not install stays out of the map, and the build names it; the group is built again without it. In Base UI 1.8.0 these are `internals/temporal-adapter-date-fns` and `internals/temporal-adapter-luxon`, which ask for `date-fns`, `@date-fns/tz` and `luxon`: the map serves 77 of the 79 specifiers.
- **Externals.** `clientExternals` gains `@base-ui/react` and `@base-ui/react/*`, and a desktopMain part cannot import Base UI. The Papercraft preview leaves external, besides React, the Base UI specifiers that the map serves: the host asks its own copy, once per specifier, whether it bundles the subpath with React external, the same question the platform build asks. `radix-ui` leaves `HOST_PACKAGES` and becomes a Project package like any other.

## Coalescence of the recorded deltas (2026-10-21)

- **D-CD-01 Data migration.** `20261021000000_coalesce_thread_deltas`, data only (the `schema.prisma` does not change), runs in `prisma migrate deploy` in an explicit transaction, like D-S7-01. In each stream `runtime.thread.events/%`, a sequence of consecutive entries of `reasoning.delta`, `text.delta`, `refusal.delta` or `tool_call.delta` that differ only in the delta (`delta`, or `argumentsDelta` in the tool call) and in `createdAt` becomes the first entry, with the concatenated delta and the value and the `created_at` of the last one. A delta that says nothing (no text; in a tool call, no arguments, id and name) goes away and does not interrupt the sequence; any other entry interrupts it. These are the rules of `isEmptyDelta` and `mergeDeltas` of `runtime/contracts`, which the `default` executor applies when recording, with one conservative difference: the migration only joins call pieces with equal id and name, not absent ones. A delta of unexpected shape (not a string, or an envelope type different from the event) stays like any other entry.
- **Projector rules.** The `text.delta` sequence is split at the first non-blank delta (JavaScript's `trim()`), because the chat's projector opens the answer there and discards the blanks before it; joining the blank prefix would change the turn's key and the text. Reasoning opens a turn at any non-empty delta, so it is not split. In a rehearsal on a copy of the real database (41 Threads), the chat's projector produced the same 1480 turns before and after.
- **Sequences.** They are never renumbered: turn keys, comment anchors and cursors contain them. The first entry keeps its own, the removed ones leave gaps and each stream's counter does not change. The last entry of the stream never goes away, because the watch only delivers `lastSequence + 1` contiguously from the last entry read: the group that contains it (the sequence, or the part of a split text sequence) is not coalesced, and it stays even when empty. The rest of that sequence is treated like any other: the other empty deltas go away, and the blank part before a split text sequence in the tail is coalesced.
- **Verification.** In the same transaction, with `RAISE EXCEPTION` on any divergence: per stream, each sequence segment (consecutive deltas of one key, without the empty ones) has, in the same place, the same key and the same `string_agg` before and after, so that joining two sequences of the same key separated by another entry fails even with the text of each key equal (a test with a failure trigger that only exists in the test); every entry that is not the first of a group is identical to the previous one, and no entry that is not a delta disappears; counter and last sequence of each stream equal.
- **`storageGeneration`.** Unlike D-S7-01 and like the NDJSON import of stages 6/7, the migration increments `storageGeneration` in the `identity` record of `storage_meta`, but only when some entry changed: the local snapshots of `history` keep the old entries, and reconciling by cursor would lose the deltas absorbed by an entry before the cursor. The marker is the `coalesced-thread-deltas` key of `storage_meta`, from `postgres` (not the `runtime.migration/v1` document, which belongs to `runtime` and describes the `prisma-legacy` copy), with the counts; with it present the migration does nothing, and without it the coalescence is a fixed point and does not increment again.
- **Operation.** Irreversible and offline, approved by the user (HC-007, HC-009): `pg_dump` before, desktop closed and host stopped, `prisma migrate deploy`, then `VACUUM (FULL, ANALYZE) storage_stream_entry` with the host still stopped; undoing is restoring the dump. The step-by-step procedure is in the README of `doric-host` (Persistence and security).
- **Measurements.** On the real database (read only): 646,307 entries in 41 Threads, of which 622,992 deltas (548,781 reasoning, 41,988 text, 32,223 tool_call) and 23,315 others; 616,079 deltas absorbed in 6,749 groups go away, none empty (the 5,010 tool pieces without arguments have id and name and enter their groups), leaving 30,228 entries (23,315 + 6,913 groups). The rehearsal on a copy took about 14 s, and the `VACUUM FULL` took the table from 402 MB to 59 MB. The optional test `coalesce-deltas-size.test.ts` (`DORIC_TEST_COALESCE_SIZE=1`) measures a synthetic log of 100k entries: 100,036 → 3,646 entries, 26.3 → 1.7 MiB of values, and the complete read from 51 → 3 MiB retained in the heap.
- **D-CD-02 Coalesced recording.** Approved by the user (HC-004, HC-007, HC-009) in place of a live-only channel or a new storage primitive, which were rejected. The `default` executor (2.1.0) records through the session wrapped by `coalesced` (`bundles/runtime/default/src/host/deltas.ts`): consecutive deltas of one type (`reasoning.delta`, `text.delta`, `refusal.delta`), or of one call (`tool_call.delta` with the same `index` and `id`), become one event with the concatenated delta. The event is recorded 250 ms after the first delta, on reaching 64 KiB of text (UTF-8 bytes), before any other event or history write, and at the end of the run (end, abort, error and lost generation). Empty deltas go away without interrupting the sequence. All writes, including the window's, go through a single queue; the failure of the window, which nobody awaits, reaches the next write, and the run ends with the same persistence error of the runtime. Event types, `ThreadEvent`, watch items, cursors, projector and the history cache format do not change.
- **Shared rule.** `mergeDeltas`, `isEmptyDelta` and `streamDelta` live in `runtime/contracts` (`runtime` 2.5.0, pure functions only), so that the executor and the chat's property test use the same rule without importing code from another package. The rule is that of D-CD-01: answer text that is still blank is not joined to the first words, reasoning is joined, and a tool delta is only empty without arguments, without `id` and without `name`: the first piece of a call usually carries only the identity, which the coalesced event keeps (the `id` and the `name` come from whichever side has them).
- **Live and durable.** The runtime's session publishes each event after the commit, so what is live is a subset of what is durable: a crash loses at most the retained window, which was never published. Live, the answer arrives in pieces of up to 250 ms.
- **Rewind by counter.** `ThreadStore.rewind` now uses the stream's counter (the `lastSequence` of the read, the last sequence assigned) and not the sequence of the last entry read. With the tail absent, the old anchor refused the append with `sequence_conflict` until it ran out of attempts.
- **Tests.** `bundles/runtime/default/tests/deltas.test.ts` (type, call and blank boundaries, empty ones, the 64 KiB limit, window with an injected clock, write before another event and before the history, failures, and 200 seeds in which the concatenation is preserved and no recorded neighbor could be joined) and `executor-deltas.test.ts` (a real turn, abort and error). In the runtime, `tests/domain/coalesced-stream.test.ts` (live watch equal to the reread from 0; host stop in the middle of the stream: published ⊆ durable, and the resumption continues the same log), `tests/store/append-cost.test.ts` (10k tokens in 400 appends, one commit and one read per event) and three rewind cases with gaps in `store.conformance.test.ts`. In the chat, `tests/projector-coalesced.property.test.ts`: in 300 seeded logs, the per-token log and the coalesced one give the same turns, with the same `events[0].sequence` and the same last `createdAt`, and live batches cut at any point give the projection of the whole log.

## Defined agents: snapshot in the thread (2026-10-28)

- **Spawn options.** `runtime.threads/v1` 1.6.0: `spawn(prompt, title?, options?)`, with `options = { agent?: { name, source: 'built-in' | 'workspace', instructions }, model?, effort? }` strict. `instructions` has at most 64 000 characters, and `effort` accepts the values of `reasoningEfforts` (`none` to `max`, mirrored from `packages/llms`). An unknown key, an `effort` outside the list or instructions that are too long are refused in the input validation, and nothing is created. The `thread-spawn` tool now sends options: see "thread-spawn: agent, model and effort".
- **Snapshot.** The runtime records `agent` (with the instructions), `model` and `effort` in the `runtime.thread/v1` (1.1.0) document at creation, and it is that document that holds from then on: a later change in the agent's definition does not alter the thread. Optional fields, so old documents stay valid without a migration (minor, by the rule of `versioning.md`).
- **Inheritance.** A child without `model` or without `effort` receives the value recorded in the parent, as it already receives `cwd` and `cwdRepo`; with no value in the parent, the executor's default holds. The `agent` is never inherited: a child without `agent` is an ordinary thread. The runtime validates neither model nor effort against the catalog; that validation belongs to the `thread-spawn` tool (DORIC-74). Superseded in "Per-thread execution (2026-11-15)": the child also inherits the `providerId`, the runtime validates, and a spawn with only a model receives its initial effort.
- **Instructions outside the window.** The public `Thread` shows `agent` as `{ name, source }`, `model` and `effort`, and never the instructions; no event carries them. The runner reads them from the recorded document at each run and compaction and hands them to the executor in `thread.agent.instructions` (the `agent` of `threadSchema` is loose, so the field crosses `executor/v1`). That is why retry, the parent's `thread-send`, the `thread-restart` of a child that failed and the run after a host restart use the same values.
- **Versions.** `runtime.threads/v1` 1.5.0 → 1.6.0, `runtime.thread/v1` 1.0.0 → 1.1.0 and the `runtime` bundle 2.5.0 → 2.6.0. `executor/v1` 1.3.0 declares the fields (`ExecutorThread`, `executorThreadSchema`) and `default` uses them (DORIC-73, in "Execution with the thread's agent, model and effort").

## Prompts in Markdown with mustache (2026-10-28)

- **`.md` templates with mustache.** The prompt texts of `default` leave `src/host/prompts/{system,repository,skills}.ts` and the lists and tables assembled in `src/host/skills` for five templates in `src/host/prompts`: `system.md`, `repository.md`, `skills-catalog.md` (the whole `# Skills` section), `skills-changed.md` (the whole "Skills changed" message) and `loaded-skills.md` (the body of the "Loaded skills" message), rendered with `mustache` (^4.2.0, no dependencies). Rejected alternative: template literals in TypeScript. Reason: DORIC-72, DORIC-73 and DORIC-87 need named variables and workspace templates, which are read at run time and cannot be TypeScript.
- **Reading at module load.** Each module reads the templates it uses once with `readFileSync` when it loads (`executor.ts` → `system.md`, `repository.ts` → `repository.md`, `skills/catalog.ts` → `skills-catalog.md` and `skills-changed.md`, `skills/loaded.ts` → `loaded-skills.md`), not in `activate`, because the consumers are synchronous and the tests call them without activation. The loader removes exactly one final `\n`. A missing `.md` makes the import fail, and the bundle goes into quarantine with `invalid_part`.
- **Names, partials and escaping.** `src/host/prompts/templates.ts` walks the tokens of `Mustache.parse` (`name`, `&`, `#` and `^`, including inside sections) and refuses an undeclared name, with the file and the name in the message; the three message templates (`skills-catalog.md`, `skills-changed.md` and `loaded-skills.md`) declare names, and since DORIC-87 `system.md` also declares its own (`promptVariableNames`); `repository.md` declares none. Partials (`{{>`) are refused. `render` does not escape HTML: the `escape` option is passed per call, and the global `Mustache.escape` does not change.
- **Granularity.** One template per text that the model receives whole. The code supplies only data: which always-available skills and which rows fit, the text of each cell already on one line, cut and with `|` escaped, the `~N` locators, the dropped counts, the fences, the cut marks and the omitted ids. The templates do all the formatting with mustache sections, each section tag alone on its line. `renderBlock` renders a template as a block (it strips one leading and one trailing `\n`); `renderSection` renders a section for the given items and is how the budgets are measured (list rows 8000/4000/4000, always-available up to 16 000, loaded skill entries in greedy order), so the row format exists only in the template. The cut marker comes from the template, by rendering an empty cut entry, and serves to read the earlier loaded-skills messages. Rejected alternative: one `.md` fragment per old constant (`prompts/skills/*.md`, with the `prompts/*.ts` modules exporting the same constants and the assembly in TypeScript). Reason: the templates did not format anything, and the user refused. The descriptions of the `load_skill` tool and of its parameter stay as strings in the `defineTool` of `skills/load-skill.ts`, like those of the other tools: they are one line each, with no variable or formatting. The text the model receives stays byte for byte the same: the request body fixtures did not change, a byte proof with about 13 800 random entries against HEAD found no difference, and the tests take the expected texts from `tests/support/prompts.ts`, generated from HEAD with `git show`.
- **Copy in the build and in the test.** `tsc` does not copy `.md`. The `copyMarkdown` function of `packages/bundle/src/build/cli.ts` mirrors the `.md` files of `src/host` into `dist/host` (removing the old `.md` files) and runs in `build` before the test activation, because the host reads the templates on import. The subcommand `copy-md <from> <to>` applies the same rule, and the `test` target of `default` calls it after `tsc` for `dist-tests/src/host`.
- **`.prettierignore`.** `bundles/*/*/src/host/**/*.md` stays outside prettier, which formats Markdown: it would align tables, swap markers and escape characters, changing the bytes the model reads. It also applies to the built-in agents that will come in `src/host/agents/*.md`.
- **Version.** `default` 2.2.0 → 2.2.1, with no contract change.

## Profile in the configuration (2026-10-28)

- **D22 Source of the data.** The user's name and time zone come from an optional `profile` block of the host configuration, filled in by the user themselves. `default` uses them in the prompt variables `user.name` and `timezone` (DORIC-87). Rejected alternatives: the host OS user and `git config user.name`, because the host may be remote (`DORIC_HOST_URL`) and then neither describes who uses Doric.
- **Schema.** `profile: { name?, timezone? }` is strict and comes last in `ConfigInputSchema`, so a configuration without it serializes as before. `name` is trimmed and has 1 to 100 characters, with no line break. `timezone` is an IANA name that the host's `Intl` accepts (`new Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-US', { timeZone })`) and is recorded as chosen, without normalization. Without `timezone`, the host's time zone holds.
- **Removal.** In the Settings window, the "Host time zone" option removes `profile.timezone`, and an empty name removes `profile.name`; a profile without fields is removed, so the draft never carries `profile: {}`. The adapter `settings-api.ts` carries `profile` in both directions, so that saving another section does not erase the profile.
- **Profile section.** It is the first in the navigation (the window still opens on Providers), with a name field and a time zone combobox with `Intl.supportedValuesOf('timeZone')`. The picker always adds `UTC`, which that list may not include, and the recorded time zone, if the list does not have it.
- **Versions.** `host-config/v1` and `host-config.configuration/v1` 1.0.0 → 1.1.0, `host-config` bundle 0.0.1 → 0.1.0; `settings` 1.2.0 → 1.3.0, with `requires` `host-config/v1@^1.1.0` and `provides` `settings.config/v1@1.1.0`.

## Default variables of the system prompt (2026-10-28)

- **`# Environment` section.** `system.md` ends with a `# Environment` section rendered from `PromptVariables` (`src/host/prompts/variables.ts`): `os` (the sandbox's operating system), `date` and `timezone` (today's date and the time zone it is given in) and `user.name`. The lines of `os` and of the name are in mustache sections, with the tags alone on the line, so a missing variable disappears without leaving a blank line. The `# Skills` section still comes after it.
- **D21 No exact time.** `date` is only the day (`YYYY-MM-DD`, with `Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-CA')` in the time zone), never the time. The Claude provider marks the system prompt for caching (`packages/llms/src/lib/providers/claude/body.ts:87-93,121`): a time in the prompt would change the text at each run and invalidate the cache; the day changes at most once a day.
- **Day fixed per run.** The variables are read once at the start of each run (`RunVariables`, one clock per run) and do not change until it ends, so all the requests of a run carry the same prompt, even if midnight passes in the middle.
- **Time zone and name.** `timezone` is the configuration's `profile.timezone` (D22) and, without it, the host's time zone (`Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone`). `user.name` is `profile.name`; without it the line disappears, with no fallback (D22).
- **OS probe.** `os` is `"<PRETTY_NAME> (<uname -srm>)"`, read with a single `exec` in the sandbox through `runtime.lease/v1` (`sh -c 'uname -srm && . /etc/os-release && printf …'`, a 5 s timeout). The result is cached in memory per Project and lease id (`describe().id`), in the executor instance, so each run asks `describe` but only probes again when the lease changes. With no provider, with `describe` or `exec` failing, a non-zero exit, a timeout or empty output, the line disappears and the run goes on; the failure is not cached, so the next run tries again. A sandbox without `/etc/os-release` has no `os`.
- **Injectable clock.** `activate` accepts an optional second argument `{ now? }` (the loader passes none), which reaches `createExecutor` and `createRunVariables`; the tests fix the date through it.
- **Version.** `default` 2.2.1 → 2.2.2, with `requires` `host-config/v1@^1.1.0` (the `profile`).

## Agent definitions in `default` (2026-10-28)

- **D23 One format and one parser.** The built-in agents (`src/host/agents/<nome>.md`, listed in `built-in.ts`, the ten of the code workflow, see «Built-in agents of the EGM workflow») and the workspace ones (`<cwd>/.agents/agents/<nome>.md`) have the same format and go through the same rules: the name is that of the file and matches `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,63}$`; the frontmatter gives `description` (mandatory), `model` and `effort` (optional; empty ones count as absent) through `frontmatterField`, the same lenient read that `description` always had in the skills; `name:` and unknown keys are ignored; the instructions are `skillBody`. An invalid built-in, a file name outside the pattern or two files with the same name make `activate` throw the error before providing anything. Rejected alternative: the `yaml` lib through `parseSkillDocument`, which is strict and refuses keys that are not skill keys.
- **D24 The body is always a template.** The instructions, built-in or workspace, are a mustache template that may only cite the prompt variables (`os`, `date`, `timezone`, `user`, `user.name`), checked with `checkTemplate` and `promptVariableNames`. An unknown name makes the definition invalid, with the reason citing the name and suggesting `{{=<% %>=}}` for literal text. `resolve` returns the raw template; the rendering belongs to the run (DORIC-73), so that the date follows each run. A partial gives `the instructions are not a valid template: includes partial "<nome>"; partials are not supported`.
- **Separate exec.** The scan of `.agents/agents` is its own `RuntimeLeaseV1.exec` (`sh -c`, the directory as a positional argument, 64 files, 5 s), in parallel with that of the skills, not a combined script: each read keeps its own failure (provider absent, non-zero exit, timeout → empty list) and its own record format.
- **Header and size; the documented gap.** The scan prints, per file, the name, the size in bytes and the first 4096 bytes. More than 64 000 bytes is invalid already in the listing; the template is only checked when the header is the whole file. `resolve` reads the whole file with `readFile` and checks everything again, so a file larger than 4096 bytes whose instructions cite an unknown name appears in `list` and fails in `resolve` with `invalid_definition`. Accepted: reading each whole file in the listing would cost up to 64 × 64 000 bytes per run. A file that `resolve` cannot read gives `invalid_definition` with the reason `the file cannot be read`.
- **An invalid file hides the built-in.** A workspace file with the name of a built-in replaces the built-in even when it is invalid: it disappears from `list`, and `resolve` gives `invalid_definition`, not the built-in. So what the workspace author wrote is never silently swapped for another definition.
- **Diff by name.** The message `<bundle-instruction bundle="default" reason="agents-changed">` (no retain) compares by name: a new agent, or one whose `description` or `source` changed, goes into "Added or changed", and a name that disappeared into "No longer available". A change of only `model` or `effort` is not counted, because the catalog does not show them. It comes before "Skills changed", and after a compaction the next comparison goes back to the agents of the system prompt, as with the skills.
- **Version.** `default` 2.2.2 → 2.3.0, with `provides` `default.agents/v1@1.0.0` (host, no method exposed to the window).

## Execution with the thread's agent, model and effort (2026-10-28)

- **D17 A section of the agent's own.** A thread that runs as an agent receives in the system prompt a `# Agent: <nome>` section (`src/host/prompts/agent.md`), after `system.md` and before `# Agents` and `# Skills`: the agent's name and instructions, rendered with the run's prompt variables, so that `{{date}}` follows each run. The default agent's system prompt stays whole; the agent's instructions take precedence over the general guidance on how to do the task. Rejected alternative: replacing `system.md` with the instructions. Reason: the agent would lose the envelope, the tools and the environment that `system.md` describes.
- **Model and effort.** The model is `thread.model ?? execution.model` and the effort `thread.effort ?? execution.effort`, on the configured `execution.providerId`. `ModelsV1.resolve` is called once per run, and the automatic compaction uses the same model; the manual one (`compact`, with no prompt) still uses the configured execution model.
- **Failure before any request.** Instructions that cite a name outside `promptVariableNames`, use a partial or do not parse make the run reject with the error of `checkTemplate` (`Template "agent <nome>" cites undeclared name "<nome>".`) before the first request to the model and before the code workflow is given.
- **No code workflow.** A thread with `agent` never receives the code workflow (`repository.md`): the agent's instructions are its workflow. `workflow.start` receives `agent`, resolves on the spot and does not register the run, so not even `onCwdChanged` delivers it later.
- **Agent without instructions.** The runner builds the `ExecutorThread` with the instructions read from the thread's document; when the document does not have them, it removes the `agent` from what it hands over, and the thread runs as the default agent, instead of failing in the validation of `executor/v1`.
- **Versions.** `executor/v1` 1.2.0 → 1.3.0 (`run` and `compact` receive `ExecutorThread`, validated by `executorThreadSchema`, without an `x-doric-local` marker). The `default` bundle stays 2.3.0 and now provides `executor/v1@1.3.0`; `runtime` still requires `executor/v1@^1.0.0`.

## Ask tool: questions to the user (2026-10-28)

- **Scope.** The `threads` bundle (1.1.0) gains the `ask` tool (order 1660, `alwaysAvailable`), the events `threads.asked/v1@1.0.0` and `threads.answered/v1@1.0.0` and the host service `threads.ask/v1@1.0.0` (`status` and `answer`, both with `expose: 'desktop'`, `desktopScope` `projectId`, `threadId` and `promptId`). It now requires `events/v1@^1.0.0` and `runtime.threads/v1@^1.3.0`. Only the main thread asks: in an agent thread (`origin === 'agent'`) the tool returns a refusal and records nothing. The `askId` is generated by the host, and a new ask supersedes the one not yet answered.
- **The log is the only record.** The service keeps no state: `status` and `answer` read the thread's log with `runtime.threads/v1.threadEvents` and redo the fold at each call (answered > superseded > pending; the first answer holds), so the state survives a host restart. The recorded line is `{ type, ...payload }`; the fold removes `type` before the `safeParse`, and the event schemas stay strict for the `append`.
- **`AskRef` is only `{ askId }`.** The ticket's `sequence` field went away: `events/v1.append` does not return a sequence, and the service finds the question by `askId` in the log.
- **Delivery in the active prompt.** `answer` reads `thread(id)` and sends a single user input with `runtime.threads/v1.steer`: in the `activePromptId`, if there is one; otherwise in the `promptId` recorded in the ask, which the runtime queues and runs at once with the thread idle. `steered` and `queued` count as sent; `missing`, `inactive`, `forbidden` or an exception become `unavailable`, and nothing is recorded.
- **State before validation.** `answer` reads the state before validating: an answered or superseded ask returns the state without sending, even with different answers. Only a pending ask validates the answers against the recorded questions (`invalid_input`). Calls with the same `askId` run one at a time.
- **Send, then record.** `threads.answered/v1` is only recorded after the send is accepted, so a refusal leaves the ask pending. Accepted risk: if the `append` fails after the send, the thread received the answers and the log does not record them, and the ask stays pending.
- **Text sent.** `Answers to your questions:`, a blank line and, for each question, `n. pergunta` and an indented line with the chosen labels in the order of the options and the trimmed free text, joined by `; `.
- **Tool text.** "and wait for the answers" left the first sentence of the description, as it contradicted "end your turn".
- **Fixtures.** `stage1-parity/tools.json` and `stage1-parity/skills.json` gain `ask`; the `request-body` fixtures of `default` stay as they are (they already omit several tools).
- **Out of scope.** The chat's interactive block that shows the questions and calls `answer` is DORIC-83.

## Built-in agents of the EGM workflow (2026-10-28)

- **The ten files.** `default` now ships ten built-in agents, one per step of the code workflow, in `src/host/agents/<nome>.md` and in this order in `BUILT_IN_AGENT_NAMES` (`built-in.ts`): `triage`, `search`, `spec`, `review`, `plan`, `contracts`, `tests`, `implementation`, `cleanup` and `docs`. Each body is the common block (`## Workflow step` and `## Tools`: the input, the limits and the tools) followed by the step's section (`## <Passo>`).
- **Duplicated common block.** The common block is identical in the ten files. The duplication is accepted (D19): each definition is a self-contained file, with no partial or include.
- **Signals and Documents copied.** The large-change signals (in `triage` and `plan`), the unclear-idea signal (in `triage`) and the Documents conventions — feature doc and spec (in `spec`, `review` and `docs`) — are copied into the agents, because an agent never sees the coordinator's workflow.
- **Attribution.** `triage`, `spec`, `review` and `docs` adapt the Elephant-Goldfish Model (commit `3d0b348`, Apache-2.0) and carry the attribution in a mustache comment `{{! … }}` before the common block, which does not reach the model; the bundle's README gains the Credits section.
- **Tests.** The test harness loads no built-in by default (`builtInAgents: []`); a test that wants them passes `builtInAgentFiles`.
- **Version.** `default` stays 2.3.0.
- **Out of scope.** `repository.md` (the coordinator's workflow) does not change until DORIC-80.

## thread-spawn: agent, model and effort (2026-10-28)

- **Superseded (2026-11-15).** The validation moved to the runtime, against the parent's provider, and the tool no longer requires `models/v1` or `host-config/v1`: see "Per-thread execution (2026-11-15)". The items below describe the earlier behavior.
- **Input.** The `thread-spawn` tool gains `agent` (a name that matches `agentNamePattern`), `model` (a non-empty string) and `effort` (one of `reasoningEfforts`), all optional; the schema stays strict and `required` stays `["prompt"]`. With none of the three, the tool calls `spawn(prompt, title, undefined)` as before and reads no other service.
- **Flow.** With `agent`, the tool calls `default.agents/v1.resolve` and passes the contract's error on unchanged (`not_found`, `invalid_definition`). The model is `model ?? agent.model` and the effort `effort ?? agent.effort`. When either of the two exists, the tool reads `host-config/v1.current` and checks the pair on the configured `models.execution.providerId`. The model checked is the requested one, otherwise that of the calling thread (`runtime.threads/v1.thread`, read only when there is no model and there is a `threadId`), otherwise `execution.model`. Finally it calls `ModelsV1.resolve` with the provider, the model and the effort, and its error is also passed on. The spawn options carry only the defined keys: `agent` with `name`, `source` and `instructions` (never the description nor the agent's own model and effort), `model` and `effort`.
- **Lenient model list.** Only the list of the configured provider decides. A provider without `models` accepts any model and effort, and a listed model without `reasonings` accepts any effort; in both cases `ModelsV1.resolve` decides.
- **Error texts.** `Model "<modelo>" is not available on provider "<provider>". Available models: <a>, <b>.` when the requested model is not in the list; `Effort "<esforço>" is not supported by model "<modelo>". Supported efforts: <a>, <b>.` when the listed model does not accept the effort; `Agent types are unavailable in this runtime.` when there is no provider of `default.agents/v1` (the `unavailable` with `details.contract` equal to `default.agents/v1`). All fail before creating the child.
- **Manifest.** `threads` now requires `runtime.threads/v1@^1.6.0` (before `^1.3.0`), `models/v1@^1.0.0` and `host-config/v1@^1.1.0`, and lists `default.agents/v1` in `optional`, with the id without a version. Without `default`, the tool still creates threads without an agent.
- **Version.** `threads` stays 1.1.0.
- **Fixtures.** The `thread-spawn` entry of `stage1-parity/tools.json` and of the `request-body` fixtures of `default` (`tools.json`, `openrouter-body.json`, `openai-responses-body.json`) was generated from the tool's real schema.

## thread-spawn block: agent, model and effort (2026-10-28)

- **Reading.** `parseThreadSpawn` reads `agent`, `model` and `effort` from the args as a trimmed string; an empty value or one that is not a string becomes absent. There is no enum validation: the tool already validates the input before creating the child.
- **Header.** Next to the verb, the block shows `· agente · modelo · esforço` with only the fields present, in a `span[data-slot="spawn-meta"]` with `font-light text-muted-foreground/70`, like the origin of the skill block, limited to `max-w-60` and truncated. With no field, the header is identical to the earlier one.
- **Version.** `chat` goes from 1.5.0 to 1.5.1.
- **Tests.** The parse is tested without a DOM and the block in happy-dom.

## Code workflow with routes, defined agents and living docs (2026-10-28)

- **Coordinator only.** `repository.md` keeps Roles, Tools, Routes (Brainstorm, review stages, Docs), Documents and Coordination; the steps are the built-in agents.
- **Signals by reference.** The large-change and unclear-idea signals stay in Triage and in Plan; the content and status of the docs stay in `spec`, `review` and `docs`. The coordinator decides the route from what Triage reports.
- **Effort in the coordinator's table** (D18). `triage`, `search`, `cleanup` and `docs` low; `plan`, `contracts`, `tests` and `implementation` medium; `spec` and `review` high. No `model`: the agents inherit the coordinator's (the user's decision, P7).
- **Child without an agent.** The delivery does not change (EX5); the text gains one line: with `<parent-instruction>`, the thread does the instruction and does not run the workflow. A deliberate deviation from the ticket, which said to remove the rule.
- **Title `<Type>: <subject>`.** It stays; the `Type:` line at the start of the instruction goes away, replaced by the `agent` parameter.
- **Polling, evidence and resumption.** They stay in the `thread-delegation` skill; the coordinator keeps only the rules for escalating and for redoing the steps that failed.
- **EGM origin.** Commit `3d0b348`; a mustache comment at the top of `repository.md` (it does not reach the model) and `## Credits` in the README.
- **Rejected alternatives.** A single text with the types (repetition and drift, ~18 KB retained); one type per persona (the ten agents are steps, not identities).
- **Cost.** The retained text gets smaller (101 → 67 lines); the large route uses at least 18 threads.
- **Version.** `default` stays 2.3.0 (not released).
- **Tests.** No content test (M15); the exact-text test (`repositoryPrompt` in `tests/support/prompts.ts`) proves that the comment does not leak.

## Ask: clearing the free answer and skipping questions (2026-10-28)

- **Keyboard (DORIC-89).** A click on the `<threads-ask>` block selects the whole block in the chat's Lexical editor, which then cancels Backspace, Delete, arrows, Enter and Ctrl+A typed in the free answer. The pending `<section>` of `AskQuestions` stops the propagation of `keydown` (`stopPropagation`), like `delegated-turn.tsx`; `<threads-ask>` is a React root of its own, so the native event stops there. With the focus on the block, Esc and Tab do not reach the editor either.
- **Skip (DORIC-90).** Back, Next and Send are always enabled (Send only waits while it sends). A question left empty is skipped and goes as `options: []`; sending with all of them skipped is allowed. The answered block shows "Skipped" on the skipped question.
- **Host.** `Ask.answer` stops refusing an answer without a choice; the other rules stay. `formatAnswers` writes `(skipped)` on the values line of the skipped question. The format of `threads.answered/v1` does not change.
- **Version.** `threads.ask/v1` goes from 1.0.0 to 1.1.0 (it accepts what it used to refuse); the `threads` bundle stays 1.1.0 (not released).
- **Tests.** The keyboard is tested on the block mounted in the conversation; skipping, in the client, in the domain and in the host.

## Code workflow as an on-demand skill (DORIC-91, 2026-10-28)

- **Skill instead of push.** The text of `repository.md` becomes the built-in skill `coding-workflow` of `default` (`src/skills/coding-workflow/SKILL.md`, `order: 50`, not always-available), loaded with `load_skill`; `createWorkflow`, the handler of `onThreadCwdChangedHook`, the `coding-workflow` reason and the `default:coding-workflow` key go away. The host does not verify that the model loaded the skill; the system prompt says to check the directory with `cwd` and load the skills that match.
- **Flag and note in `cwd`.** The result gains `repositoryRoot` in every status and, at the root of a repository, `note` with the exact text `Remember to load the skills that match your task when working in a repository.` Only the root counts.
- **`location()`.** `runtime.workspace/v1` 1.2.0 gains `location()`, which probes the hint now and answers `{ cwd, repo? }`; when the hint changes, it records, publishes and fires `onThreadCwdChangedHook`. `cwd` calls it after every result.
- **Bounded probe.** The probe's exec carries `timeoutMs: 5000`; an exit other than 0 (or `null`) throws `HintProbeIncompleteError`, which only `setCwd` translates into "no hint"; `location()` keeps the stored hint when the probe does not finish.
- **Agent filter.** The executor removes `coding-workflow` from the built-in skills of a Thread with `agent`: it is not listed and `load_skill` refuses it.
- **Pin.** The "Loaded skills" message always keeps the `CODING_WORKFLOW_SKILL` entry, before the greedy filling and outside the cut by size.
- **No migration.** `default:coding-workflow` instructions already retained stay; the manifest of `default` removes `runtime.threads/v1` and `runtime.hooks/v1` from `optional`.
- **Versions.** `files` 1.3.0 → 1.4.0 and `runtime.workspace/v1` 1.1.0 → 1.2.0; `runtime` stays 2.6.0 and `default` 2.3.0 (not released).
- **Replaces.** The delivery of `repository.md` in the earlier records (the push and its triggers: lines 1193-1195 in "Prompts in Markdown with mustache", 1234 in "Execution with the thread's agent, model and effort" and 1280 in "Code workflow with routes, defined agents and living docs"), the EGM attribution comment at the top of `repository.md` (line 1286; now at the top of `SKILL.md`) and the `repositoryPrompt` test (line 1290).
- **Tests and docs.** `repository.test.ts` goes away; tests cover `location()`, the probe, the result of `cwd`, the filter and the pin. Spec: [coding-workflow-skill.md](../../designs/coding-workflow-skill.md); features: [coding-workflow.md](../../features/coding-workflow.md), [cwd-tool.md](../../features/cwd-tool.md).

## OpenUI: UI generated in the conversation and papercraft fences (stages 2 and 3, 2026-11-06)

- **Fence contract.** `chat.views/v1` 1.1.0 gains `registerFenceRenderer({ tag, elementTag })` and `watchFences`: the chat computes, only for registered tags, the key `<promptId>/<turnSequence>/<index>` and the state (`streaming`, `closed`, `unterminated`) of each fence of an agent turn and creates one element per key; no host reads fences. A second registration of the same tag is `registration_conflict`.
- **Queue receipt.** A queue receipt no longer ends the turn: "the last turn" is the last one that is not `queued`. The change applies to every reader, with or without the setting, as a fix of the chat.
- **Actions.** `chat.send/v1` 1.1.0 gains `sendAction`, which sends a control's text in a `<ui-action program="…">` envelope through the composer's route, only from the last closed revision of a program in a main Thread.
- **`openui` bundle.** New (`bundles/features/openui`): the `openui-lang` fence, the renderer chunk (`client/chunks/renderer.js`, loaded once per window), the question form (`AskForm`, with `openui.questions/v1` and the event `openui.answered/v1`), the error collector `openui.programs/v1` for the two tags and the `openui` skill. Dependencies: `@openuidev/lang-core` and `@openuidev/react-lang` 1.0.2 and `@openuidev/react-ui` 0.18.1, pinned.
- **Browser build.** Every browser build of `bundle` defines `process.env.NODE_ENV` as `"production"` and uses `minifySyntax`, in both modes, so that the OpenUI development tools are neither mounted nor bundled; client chunks (`src/client/chunks/*.ts`) gain their own CSS next to them and are never loaded by the loader.
- **OpenUI dependencies.** The root `package.json` gained `overrides` that resolve the `zustand` peer of `@openuidev/react-ui` and of `@openuidev/react-headless` to the repository's zustand 5 (`^5.0.15`); `@openuidev/react-headless` stays installed as a peer, without any Doric module importing it (U42).
- **Papercraft in fences.** The `papercraft <identidade>` fence draws a program with the repository's components in an isolated frame; repository libraries in `.doric/papercraft/schemas/` (versioned), the rest of `.doric/papercraft/` ignored by the `.gitignore` the host writes, and the one-time migration of the old `.doric/.gitignore`; the event `papercraft.cwd/v1` links each fence to the library of the Thread's directory; the verified modules live in `userData/papercraft/` and are served at `doric://app/papercraft-modules/*`.
- **Stage 3.** The setting `openui.generative-ui/v1` became `true` by default; with it on, `ask`, `papercraft_preview` and the `papercraft-preview` skill left the model's list (`threads.ask/v1` 1.2.0 `withholdTool`). Stage 4 removed all of that.
- **Tests and docs.** Specs: [openui.md](../../designs/openui.md), [conversation-openui.md](../../designs/conversation-openui.md) and [papercraft-openui.md](../../designs/papercraft-openui.md); feature: [papercraft.md](../../features/papercraft.md).

## OpenUI: removal of the old path, of ask and of the artifacts (stage 4, 2026-11-06)

- **Removed.** The `artifacts` bundle (`artifacts/v1`, `artifact.kind/v1`, `artifacts.cache/v1`, its events and the setting `artifacts.review-policy/v1`); in `papercraft`, `papercraft.previews/v1`, the `papercraft_preview` tool, the `papercraft-preview` skill, the `preview` type and the approval command; in `threads`, the `ask` tool, `threads.ask/v1`, the events `threads.asked/v1` and `threads.answered/v1` and `<threads-ask>`; in `openui`, the setting `openui.generative-ui/v1`, `openui.setting/v1` and the Generative UI section. Fences and skills no longer depend on a setting.
- **Old lines.** Projectors that claim `artifacts.*` (in `papercraft`) and `threads.asked/v1`/`threads.answered/v1` (in `threads`) and draw nothing; comments on artifact revisions are not listed. Postgres does not change.
- **Persisted data.** The desktop deletes `userData/artifacts/` at every start, before opening windows (recursive and forced `fs.rm`; a symlink is removed, not followed). The `.doric/artifacts/` of each workspace stays, unread: Doric does not delete files of the user's workspace, and the user can delete it. The repository has no release notes; the notice is in the documentation and in the [feature doc](../../features/papercraft.md). The route `doric://app/artifacts/*` goes away.
- **Comments.** The target of a papercraft comment is `{ kind: 'papercraft-program', id: <identidade>, revision, anchor }`; the canvas opens by identity (`papercraft.open-canvas/v2`, route `/papercraft/:projectId/:threadId/:identity`).
- **Versions.** `openui` 2.0.0, `papercraft` 1.2.0 → 2.0.0, `threads` 1.1.0 → 2.0.0, `chat` 1.5.1 → 1.6.0.
- **Replaces.** The records "Ask tool: questions to the user" and "Ask: clearing the free answer and skipping questions" (2026-10-28) describe a tool that no longer exists; they stay as history.
- **Rollback.** Only by reverting the merge of stage 4.

## OpenUI: error card, errors in the same turn and skill rules (stage 5, 2026-11-06)

- **Delivery of the errors (U50).** `openui.programs/v1` sends the errors of a closed fence 200 ms after the report, as a `validation` instruct aimed at the last prompt of the Thread: steer while it runs, a new prompt in the queue after it has ended. Each prompt that the validation did not start allows at most 3 immediate sends; the prompt-start hook resets that count and delivers what was waiting. The errors of an `unterminated` fence wait for the next prompt start. See D18 in [openui.md](../../designs/openui.md#d18-validation-feedback-u19).
- **Card (U49) and skills (U51).** The reader sees the errors in a card under the fence, in both hosts; the `openui` and `papercraft` skills gain the patch rule and, in `openui`, the limits of the question form.

## OpenUI: diagrams, phase 1 (`Flowchart`, 2026-11-06)

- **Components.** `Flowchart`, `Node`, `Edge` and `Group` in the conversation's library; an outline during streaming and one ELK layout per closed revision, in the chunk `client/chunks/layout.js`. Findings of the diagrams go into `programCheck`. Spec: [openui-diagrams.md](../../designs/openui-diagrams.md); feature: [openui-diagrams.md](../../features/openui-diagrams.md).
- **No Mermaid for now (UD6); ELK accepted (UD7).** `elkjs` 0.11.1 pinned, EPL-2.0; the chunk keeps the ELK notices and a source statement. The license texts will come from a separate change in `packages/bundle`; phase 1 may be released before it ([FR12](../../designs/openui-diagrams.md#risks), accepted risk).
- **Shared viewport.** The pan and zoom helpers left `papercraft` for `desktop/ui/viewport`, with `wheelGesture`; `papercraft` re-exports them.
- **Versions.** `openui` 2.1.0 → 2.2.0; `papercraft` and `packages/desktop` with no version change. Phase 2 is in the next section.

## OpenUI: diagrams, phase 2 (`SequenceDiagram`, `EntityDiagram`, `MindMap`, 2026-11-06)

- **Components.** `SequenceDiagram` with `Participant`, `Message`, `Note`, `Fragment` and `Section`; `EntityDiagram` with `Entity`, `Field` and `Relation` (`er` and `class` notations, methods as strings); `MindMap` with recursive `Topic`. Reading (`read.ts`), `useDrawn` (`view.ts`), the generic outline and `DiagramFrame` shared; a table-driven check (`DIAGRAM_PARTS`), with findings for parts out of place and a diagram inside a diagram. Spec: [openui-diagrams-phase2.md](../../designs/openui-diagrams-phase2.md); feature: [openui-diagrams.md](../../features/openui-diagrams.md).
- **Still no Mermaid (UD6).** The three types are native, like `Flowchart`.
- **Sequence without ELK.** Doric's `sequence-layout.ts` positions participants, messages, notes and fragments; a `SequenceDiagram` never loads the layout chunk.
- **ELK `mrtree` for mind maps; layered with edge labels for entities.** The cardinalities are ELK edge labels, with spacing for the markers; the `right` of a `class` diagram is the `down` layout transposed, because ELK's `RIGHT` overlaps the edge labels; `er` keeps ELK's `RIGHT`.
- **Version.** `openui` stays 2.2.0: the two phases ship in one commit and one version; no new dependency. Partial rollback (F24): a 2.2.1 removes the twelve phase 2 components from `add` and restores the four phase 1 rules in the skill; the flowcharts stay.
- **Pending acceptance.** The growth of `renderer.js` over the two phases measured 100,194 bytes (5,594,179 → 5,694,373), 194 above the G5 target of 100,000; it is left to the user's acceptance (FR17).

## OpenUI: square area of the diagrams (2026-11-06)

- **Square area with a ceiling (UD14, UD15).** The area of the four diagram types is `aspect-square w-full`, with its height limited by the window: `max-h-[calc(100svh-40px-var(--doric-titlebar-height,40px)-32px)]`. The height follows the width up to the ceiling, with no measured height or inline style; the fit and the refit stay those of `useDiagramViewport`. Spec: [openui-diagrams-square-area.md](../../designs/openui-diagrams-square-area.md); feature: [openui-diagrams.md](../../features/openui-diagrams.md).
- **No `height` prop (UD16).** `height` leaves `Flowchart`, `SequenceDiagram`, `EntityDiagram` and `MindMap`: schemas, models, views and the skill's signatures. A `height` that the model still writes is discarded by lang-core and reported as `excess-args`.
- **Version.** `openui` stays 2.2.0, not yet released.

## UI redesign: platform versions and app/bundles skew (phase 1, 2026-11-08)

Spec: [ui-redesign.md](../../designs/ui-redesign.md) (S35); feature: [ui-redesign.md](../../features/ui-redesign.md), where UR25 is defined. Commits `a66fd980`, `2b00a1dc`, `b0a32f2b`.

- **Two versions.** `PLATFORM_VERSION` goes up to `1.1.0` with the modules `desktop/ui/identity` (strings: `initials`, `identityTone`, `identityRing`, `READER_NAME`) and `desktop/ui/reader-avatar` (the `ReaderAvatar` in React), which leave `chat` for `packages/desktop/src/identity/`. `PLATFORM_ASSETS_VERSION` (`1.0.0`) separates the segment of the folders and URLs, which does not change.
- **Skew policy (UR25): the bundles adapt.** The app brings the platform; the bundles come from the host and may be newer or older than it. A bundle detects platform contracts with `whenAvailable`, declares platform refs with `ref` (instead of importing new names from `bundle/contracts`), loads through a dynamic import a new `desktop/ui` module it can work without, and raises `engines.doric` only for a static import. In an older app, the missing feature disappears or is disabled, without failing the bundle.
- **Basic contracts.** New methods of a basic contract will go into `optional` (S35), so that an older provider still satisfies the contract. The field does not exist yet: `BasicContractSpec` (`packages/bundle/src/contracts/basic/index.ts`) only has `cardinality`, `environments` and `methods`, and the spec brings it in phase 4.2.
- **Chat.** `chat` 1.6.1 statically imports `desktop/ui/identity` and `desktop/ui/reader-avatar` (the latter in five client components, such as `comment-line.tsx`) and therefore declares `engines.doric` `^1.1.0`; in an app earlier than 1.1.0 the catalog refuses it (`engines_unsatisfied`).

## UI redesign: sidebar-08 shell (phase 2, 2026-11-09)

Spec: [ui-redesign.md](../../designs/ui-redesign.md) (versions 9–11); feature: [ui-redesign.md](../../features/ui-redesign.md). Commits `10e737d1` to `6ff47eda`.

- **`<files-toggle>` is born through `ui/v1`.** The layout mounts the element of the `files` bundle with `ElementHost` (in the workspace view and in the stories' shell), never with React's `createElement`: that one skips the `create` of `ui/v1`, the element is left without the services scope and draws nothing (fix `f1ecaa19`). The stand-in of `<files-toggle>` in the stories and tests only draws with the services that the fake `create` attached, so that the shortcut fails in the tests again.
- **New-tab flag in a module store.** Going from `/projects` to `/` mounts a new `layout-workspace` (another route pattern in the core's outlet); the new-tab flag, before a component state, got lost and `/` drew All projects. It lives in `stores/new-tab.ts`, reset on the layout's deactivation so as not to carry over to a clean start (`20ef3582`).
- **doric-e2e harness with the real shell.** `bootPlatform({ shell: 'renderer' })` brings up the real renderer shell with the built bundles (`builtBundle(dist)`: core, layout, resources, files, git, chat); runtime and comments come in as host-only cuts (`hostOnlyBundle`) that serve their contracts with the real versions and schemas (`3938c3d8`, `7f3acc5b`). The screenshots (PNG and JSON of computed styles, 1920×1080, dark and light) only run with `DORIC_SHELL_CAPTURES`. `run.ts` passes `disable-dev-shm-usage`, because the 64 MB `/dev/shm` brought down the GPU process in the whole-window captures.

## UI redesign: restyle of the bundles (phase 3, 2026-11-09)

Spec: [ui-redesign.md](../../designs/ui-redesign.md) (version 12, decisions G1–G10 and R3); feature: [ui-redesign.md](../../features/ui-redesign.md). Commits `e1c3f723` to `9d92dbed`.

- **Harness.** The doric-e2e distribution loads the real `settings` bundle and a contracts-only cut of `claude-login`; the fake host answers `models/v1`, `host-config/v1`, `secrets/v1` and `tools/v1` (`e1c3f723`).
  - Superseded by bundle isolation, step 6(a) ([OD48](../../designs/bundle-isolation-concept.md#decisions), O9): the client of `settings` no longer imports `claude-login/contracts`, so the doric-e2e distribution neither loads nor builds the `claude-login` cut. The Claude login is the `claude-login-field` element of the client part of `claude-login`, drawn by the slot `settings.provider.field`.
- **One version per bundle on the branch (G6).** Each bundle raises its version once on this branch: chat 1.6.1, files 1.5.1 and git 1.2.0 stay; usage, settings, terminal, papercraft and mcp raise the patch once in phase 3. Later fixes on the same branch do not raise it again.
- **Footer strips without color; execution picker on the left (R3).** The footer strips (conversation, viewer, stories) define no color: the items draw in `--foreground` and only the separators are dimmed. The root of `ExecutionPicker` has `mr-auto` and anchors the left of the footer. Cause: phase 2 (`49e4bc1e`) put `justify-end` on the strip when the cwd item, which carried the `mr-auto`, went away, and the picker went to the right (`9d92dbed`).
- **Radius scale in `@theme static` (version 13 of the spec).** `packages/desktop/src/styles/tailwind.css` moves `--radius-sm/md/lg/xl` from `@theme inline` to `@theme static`, so that they are always emitted: the components read `--radius-md` through `var()` in arbitrary values, and a sheet whose build did not see those classes drew square corners.
- **Tables (S22, G10).** The vendored `table.tsx` is not changed; each use removes the rules by class (`[&_tr]:border-b-0` on the `TableHeader`, `border-b-0` on each `TableRow`). No header rule and no dividers: Providers, Credentials and the mcp tables. The two model tables (ProviderModelsTable, ProviderCatalogModels) keep both rules.

## UI redesign: Files column always visible (part 2i, 2026-11-10)

Spec: [ui-redesign.md](../../designs/ui-redesign.md) (version 14, S36, UR26–UR28); feature: [ui-redesign.md](../../features/ui-redesign.md). A single commit (layout, `files`, `git` and tests together).

- **No FilesToggle and no collapse (UR26, S36).** The `<files-toggle>` element (`bundles/features/files/src/client/toggle/files-toggle.tsx`) and its story go away, in both positions (next to the account status in the Files header and in the tab bar with the column collapsed), and with it every way of collapsing the column (button and dragging to zero). The Files column is always visible, 328 px by default, resizable between 14rem and 30rem. The account status is drawn only once, in the Files header. The records above about `<files-toggle>` stay as history.
- **`layout.panel/v2@2.0.0` with only `select(id)`.** It replaces `layout.panel/v1@1.0.0` (`isOpen`, `active`, `show`, `toggle`, `subscribe`); `PanelItemProps` stays. v1 is not provided alongside v2: no bundle is published before stage 11 and the only caller, `files`, moves to v2 in the same change. "Reveal in Files" calls `select('files')`.
- **Versions.** `layout` 1.4.0 → 2.0.0. `files` stays at 1.6.0 and requires `layout.panel/v2@^2.0.0`; `git` stays at 1.2.0 and no longer requires any `layout.panel/` contract (its code never called it). A `files` or `git` built before 2i is quarantined next to layout 2.0.0 (SR15).
- **Unregistered view (UR28).** When the selected view is not registered, the panel draws no view.

## Per-thread execution (2026-11-15)

Spec: [docs/designs/per-thread-model.md](../../designs/per-thread-model.md). Feature: [docs/features/per-thread-model.md](../../features/per-thread-model.md).

- **Execution recorded in the thread.** Each Thread records `providerId`, `model` and `effort` in the `runtime.thread/v1` document. A new top-level Thread copies the workspace default (`models.execution`) at creation; a child inherits the three from the parent, and the spawn may override `model` and `effort` only on the parent's provider. A spawn that names only another model receives that model's initial effort; a spawn that names the parent's own model keeps the parent's effort.
- **Old threads.** A Thread without `providerId` runs on the provider of the workspace default, with the `model` and the `effort` recorded in it over those of the default when they exist, until the first change of its execution; there is no migration.
- **Validation outside the lock (D5).** The validation against the configuration and the call to `models/v1.resolve` run outside the Project's lock, because they may wait for the network; only the write of the document runs inside it.
- **Creation requires the configuration (D6).** Creating a Thread (`createThread`, `POST /threads`) fails without creating anything when the configuration cannot be read.
- **It holds from the next run (D8).** A change of execution holds from the Thread's next run; a prompt in progress keeps the execution it started with.
- **`setThreadExecution`.** `runtime.threads/v1` 1.7.0 adds `setThreadExecution({ id, providerId, model, effort? })`, validated like the spawn: first the configuration (configured provider and listed models; a provider without a list accepts any model), then `models/v1.resolve`. Results: `updated`, `missing`, `invalid` and `unresolvable`. `PATCH /threads/:id` accepts `providerId` and `model` together, and `effort` only with them (a body outside that pair answers `422 invalid_thread`); a refusal answers `422 invalid_execution`.
- **Validation in the runtime.** The check of the spawn's model and effort leaves the `thread-spawn` tool and goes to the runtime; the tool (`threads` 2.0.1) no longer reads `host-config/v1` or `models/v1`. The error texts now come from the runner (`ContractError` `invalid_execution` with `details.issue`); a provider that `models/v1.resolve` does not resolve rejects with the error of `models/v1` itself. An agent definition whose model the parent's provider does not list fails the spawn with `Agent "<nome>" names model "<modelo>", which its parent thread's provider does not list.` followed by the runner's message.
- **Execution unavailable.** A Thread whose recorded provider or model disappeared fails each run with the code `thread_execution_unavailable` and the message that says to choose another model in the footer; the manual compaction fails as `summarizer_failed`.
- **Footer picker.** The `<settings-execution-picker>` (`settings` 1.6.0) edits only the selected Thread, through `setThreadExecution`; with no Thread selected it shows the workspace default read-only, with the title `New threads start on the workspace default. Change it in Settings.`, and it never writes `models.execution`; the default changes only in Settings.
- **Versions.** `runtime` 2.7.0 (`runtime.threads/v1` 1.7.0, `runtime.thread/v1` 1.1.0 → 1.2.0, requires `executor/v1@^1.4.0` because an earlier executor would ignore the Thread's provider), `default` 2.5.0 (`executor/v1` 1.4.0), `threads` 2.0.1 (requires `runtime.threads/v1@^1.7.0`), `transport` requires `^1.7.0`, `settings` 1.6.0.
- **No rollback (R2).** The schema of the Thread document is strict and every write is validated. After `runtime` 2.7.0 records `providerId` in a Thread, it is not possible to go back to a version below 2.7.0: every write to that Thread would fail. Accepted risk, as with the addition of `model` and `effort` in `runtime.thread/v1` 1.1.0.

## Error messages (2026-11-16)

Spec: [docs/designs/error-messages.md](../../designs/error-messages.md). Feature: [docs/features/error-messages.md](../../features/error-messages.md).

- **Component in the platform (D1).** `ErrorNotice` and `toastError` live in `packages/desktop/src/error-notice.tsx`, exported as `desktop/ui/error-notice` through an entry of their own in `exports`, and not through `./ui/*`.
- **Error value in `desktop/reads` (D2).** `ErrorInfo`, `errorInfo`, `errorFrom`, `sameErrorInfo`, `ClientError` and the types `ErrorCatalog` and `ErrorCatalogEntry` live in the React-free module `packages/desktop/src/reads/errors.ts`, exported through `desktop/reads`. The shared entries and `describeError` live in `packages/desktop/src/error-copy.ts`, imported only by `error-notice.tsx`. `desktop/reads` is embedded by each client part, so these exports are not platform API. The copies of `messageFrom` in chat, settings and layout and the chat's `failureText` go away.
- **Split catalog (D3).** The platform's `error-notice.js` keeps only the entries for the codes that the platform and the shared packages define, with texts that do not name a bundle. Each bundle keeps the entries for its own codes and its own text for shared codes, and passes its catalog to `ErrorNotice` and to `toastError`, which merge it over the shared entries.
- **`compaction.failed` carries the error (D10).** The event gains the optional field `error`; `executor/v1` 1.4.0 → 1.5.0 (`default` 2.6.0).
- **One quarantine notice per window (D13).** A single alert notice, at the end of the left sidebar of the frame on screen (in the workspace frame, on the bottom edge of the sidebar after the stack's panels, since it has no footer; in the dashboard frame, just above the "All settings" footer), names all the quarantined bundles of the window's own catalog; the regions they would fill stay empty. `ElementHost` shows a compact notice only when creating the element throws.
- **Versions.** `PLATFORM_VERSION` 1.6.0 → 1.7.0 (`PLATFORM_ASSETS_VERSION` stays 1.0.0); `chat` 1.7.0, `settings` 1.7.0 and `layout` 3.1.0 raise `engines.doric` to `^1.7.0`; `runtime` 2.7.1 only changes the text of `resume_exhausted`, now in English.
- **Old app (R1).** An app below platform 1.7.0 that receives new `chat`, `settings` or `layout` quarantines them (`engines_unsatisfied`); the splash lists them and, after "Continue without them", so does the window's notice. Accepted risk, as with platform 1.1.0.
- **Copies of `desktop/reads` (R10).** Each client part embeds its copy; the rules of `errorInfo`/`errorFrom` and of `describeError` belong to the platform and only change in a minor of it, with the bundles raising `engines.doric`. The copies exchange only plain data, without `instanceof`.
- **Blind spots of the quarantine (R11).** With `layout` quarantined nothing draws the notice or the frame; the settings window has no quarantine notice; a `layout` quarantined on activation in the window is not reported. Accepted in this change.

## Thread archiving (2026-11-17)

Spec: [docs/designs/thread-archive.md](../../designs/thread-archive.md). Feature: [docs/features/thread-archive.md](../../features/thread-archive.md).

- **Archive is a field, not a state (D1).** The public record gains optional `archivedAt` and `pinned`; the `runtime.thread/v1` 1.3.0 document keeps `archive: { at, batch }`, `pinned`, `restoredAt`, `viewedAt` and `result.reason`. Absent means not archived, not pinned, never restored, never viewed. No migration.
- **`updatedAt` untouched (D2).** Archiving, restoring, pinning and recording a view do not move `updatedAt`, so that a sweep does not make a Project look active.
- **Activity unarchives only the Thread itself (D10, U17).** An accepted input, a resumed prompt, a resumed queue and the start of a run unarchive the Thread they reach, never the ancestors; a delivered child result unarchives the parent.
- **Restore and undo by batch (D11).** Restore and Undo unarchive the Thread and the descendants of the same batch.
- **Presence by stream (D12).** `viewThread` is a stream exposed to the desktop; the view lasts while the stream is open, counted per Thread across windows, with no TTL or heartbeat.
- **One sweep every 10 s (D13).** No per-Thread timers; an agent thread is archived between 30 s and 40 s after the end of the turn or of the last view, whichever comes later.
- **Deletion of archived agent threads (D16).** `deleteThread` refuses an agent thread only while it is not archived; the subtree still needs to be terminal.
- **Configuration (D18).** `threads: { autoArchive: { agentThreads, mainThreadsAfter } }`, strict, optional, the last field of `ConfigInputSchema`; absent means `agentThreads: true` and `mainThreadsAfter: '7d'`. `defaultConfig` does not change.
- **The reader's stop (D23).** Only the reader's stop (`result.reason: 'interrupted'`) blocks automatic archiving; other cancellations do not.
- **Question form (D24).** The host decides an open question only from the result's text, with `hasQuestionForm`, fence code moved to `runtime/contracts` and shared with `chat` and `openui`; `completedStatements` discards a final line break of an unclosed fence.
- **Read outside the lock, without waiting for the lock (D25, D26).** Each sweep reads the storage outside the Project's lock and decides again under the lock before writing; a Project whose lock is taken is skipped.
- **One commit per operation (D27).** Archiving and restoring are one commit each; above the batch limit (about 5 000 Threads) the store refuses with `limit_exceeded` and nothing is written.
- **One clock (D28).** All time comes from `Date`; only the sweep's scheduler is injected.
- **Generation switch (D29).** When draining, the archiver stops and closes each view stream with `unavailable`; a Project is only swept after 30 s alive in the process.
- **A restored Thread counts as pending (D6, D7, implementation).** A Thread restored by the reader with no turn finished since the restore counts as pending for the main-threads rule, so the subtree is not archived for inactivity until a turn finishes.
- **Refusals in the desktop.** `busy`, `already_archived` and `not_archived` reach the desktop as `ClientError('conflict')`; an unreadable archive date groups as Older.
- **Versions.** `runtime` 2.8.0 (`runtime.threads/v1` 1.8.0, `runtime.thread/v1` 1.3.0), `host-config` 0.2.0 (`host-config/v1` and `host-config.configuration/v1` 1.2.0), `layout` 3.2.0, `settings` 1.8.0, `chat` 1.8.0, `openui` 2.3.0; the last four require the new minors of the contracts.
- **No rollback of the runtime (R2).** After `runtime` 2.8.0 writes a Thread with a new field, 2.7.x can no longer write it. Accepted risk.
- **Rollback of `host-config` (R16).** Going back to 0.1.0 requires, with 0.2.0 still running, `GET /config`, removing `threads` from the `configuration` and `PUT /config` with the rest; only then installing 0.1.0. With `threads` recorded, 0.1.0 does not read the configuration.
